About these reports (README.md)

Findings of a sweep over the code (crates/ and ui/): bugs, UI/UX problems, and things one side has and its sibling does not.

  • One file per finding in [reports/](reports/), all in the form of [TEMPLATE.md](TEMPLATE.md).
  • Make a report with python3 -I tools/bugs/report.py new --area <AREA> --kind bug|ui-ux|parity --severity high|medium|low --title "<one line>", then fill the sections. Do not copy the template by hand.
  • python3 -I tools/bugs/report.py index checks every report and writes [INDEX.md](INDEX.md).
  • python3 -I tools/bugs/html.py writes [report.html](report.html): every report and every "later" line on one page for reading in a browser (search, filters by severity, kind and area; report.html#<ID> opens one report). Run it again after reports change.
  • Things that are not bugs but are worth a look later (slowness, spec and mock disagreeing, design worries) go in [later/](later/), one file per area, one line each. They are not fixed in the sweep.

Fields

  • kind: bug (wrong behavior), ui-ux (works, but a person is confused, blocked or misled), parity (one side has it and its sibling does not: similar screen parts, Rust and the screen, Japanese and English wording).
  • severity: high (data lost or damaged, sound stops or is wrong, a crash, a hang, a part that cannot be used), medium (wrong behavior a person meets in normal use, with a way around), low (rare corner, small visual or wording fault).
  • status: reported (found, not checked) → confirmed (the verifier checked it is real) → fixed. needs-lead: real, but the fix needs a decision by the lead. Reports found to be false are deleted.

Sweeps

  • 2026-11-25: the first sweep (branch fix/bug-sweep): 114 found, 110 true, 110 fixed. What is left open is in docs/progress.md, section 6, "Bug sweep".

Reports

CORE-AUDIO-001highbugfixedA new voice recording can take the name of a file the song's folder already has; the song then plays and saves that file, and the recording is lost

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-001-a-new-voice-recording-can-take-the-name-of-a-fil.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs:213-223 (start_vocals: a name is free when !dir.join(name).exists(), dir being the work place only)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles.rs:113-125 (Places::folders / Places::find: the song's 音/ and the earlier folders are looked in before the work place)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles.rs:363-366 (settle_until: a name that is already in <song>/音/ is not moved, whatever that file holds)
crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs:621-624 (keep_vocals: a recovered file whose name is already in the table is passed over)

What happens

A recorded file is named <track name> <audio id>.wav (vocalrec::file_name), and start_vocals only checks that the work place (work/音/) has no file of that name. The song's own folder <song>/音/ (and an earlier folder after 「別名で保存」) is not checked, nor are the names in the song's audio table whose files are missing.

When the song's folder already holds a different file of the same name (an audio file the person brought in earlier, which a save moved there), the recording is written to work/音/<name> and gets a fresh uid, but:

  • AudioStore::find looks in the song's folder first, so PcmStore::open for the new uid opens the old file: the new clip plays the other file's sound (its waveform is the recording's, from the writer's peaks).
  • On the next save, settle_until sees <song>/音/<name> already there and does not move the recording; the song file names the old file for both entries.
  • When the song is closed or another song opened, the work place is emptied (clear_work) and the recording is gone for good.
  • If meloet ends during such a recording, keep_vocals finds the name in the table and does not keep the file at all.

The same happens when the table has an entry of that name whose file is missing (for example a brought-in file the person moved away): the old entry then "finds" the recording and plays it.

What should happen

Every other place that gives a new file a name in the work place checks all the places the song looks in, and the table's names: AudioStore::bring_in (audiofiles.rs:294-313, self.find(&name) and taken) and clips::put_in_place (audiofiles/clips.rs:273, taken.contains(&name) || store.find(&name).is_some()). docs/architecture.md 22-4: "a different file with the same name gets « 2»"; 22-16: "a taken name gets « (2)»". A recording must never be shadowed by, or saved as, another file.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. In song B (saved), on the vocal track 「歌」, bring in a wav file named like a recording of another meloet song, for example 歌 40.wav from song A's 音/ folder, with a number larger than song B's next id. Save: the file moves to B/音/歌 40.wav.
  2. Go on recording takes on track 「歌」 until a recording gets audio id 40 (ids rise by a few with every recording, clip and take; new songs start at 4).
  3. That take plays the brought-in file, not the voice. Save, close the song, open it again: the take's voice is gone.

Cause

start_vocals picks the name with .find(|name| !names.contains(name) && !dir.join(name).exists()) where dir is work_audio_dir(state_dir). It never asks AudioStore::find, which also looks in the song's 音/ and the earlier folders, and it does not get the table's names (taken). The id in the name is new to the song, but a file brought in keeps the name it had, so it can carry any <name> <number>.wav.

Suggested fix

In start_vocals, treat a name as taken also when self.audio_store.find(name).is_some() or when the song's audio table already has that file name (the taken list the import uses), and go on to « (2)», « (3)» as now. No protocol, file-shape or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_core_audio_001), with one step taken from the code by reading. The test: an AudioStore with a song folder whose 音/ holds 「歌 40.wav」 (a file brought in and saved earlier) and an empty work place. The rule of start_vocals (service/input.rs:219-223, !dir.join(name).exists() on the work place only) says the name vocalrec::file_name("歌", 40) = 「歌 40.wav」 is free; the recording is written to work/音/歌 40.wav. Then, run on the real store: AudioStore::find("歌 40.wav") gives the song folder's file (content "the file brought in"), so the new clip plays the other file; settle (a save) does not move the recording, because the name is already in 音/; clear_work (the song let go of) removes it. After that the only 「歌 40.wav」 is the brought-in file: the recording is gone. start_vocals itself was not called in the test (it needs the whole service and an input stream); its naming rule is the two lines quoted, and Play::vocal_file_asks gives <track name> <audio id>.wav with no look at the table. Severity high stands (a recording is lost, and until then another file plays in its place), though it needs a brought-in file whose name is <track name> <a later audio id>.wav, for example a take taken over from another meloet song.

Corrections: none. The half about a table entry whose file is missing, and keep_vocals passing over a recovered file of a name the table has (input.rs:621-624), I read only.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs start_vocals: a name is taken also when self.audio_store.find(name).is_some() (all the places the song looks in) or when the song's audio table has that file name; go on to « (2)» as now. The table's names must come with the ask: crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs Effect::StartVocalRecording gains taken: Vec<String> (the file names of doc.audio, as the import passes taken), filled in play.rs:857. Tests: state/vocal_tests.rs (the effect carries the table's names) and a service-level or store-level test of the name choice (a small free function free_recording_name(store, dir, taken, names, ask) makes it testable without a stream).

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs: the names of a recording's files come from the new free_recording_names: a name is taken when the work place has the file (as before), when any place the song looks in has it (AudioStore::find: the song's 音/, the earlier folders), or when the song's audio table names it; then « (2)», « (3)», …. crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs Effect::StartVocalRecording carries taken (the file names of doc.audio), filled in crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs record and passed on in crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs. Tests: service::input::tests::a_recording_never_takes_a_name_the_song_has (the real naming function on a real AudioStore with a song folder; it fails without the fix) and state::vocal_tests::only_the_voice_is_recorded_when_there_is_no_keys_track_and_a_loop_makes_takes (the second recording's effect carries the table's names). state::vocal_tests::the_keys_and_the_voice_are_recorded_together_as_one_step was changed to expect taken: [].

CORE-PLAY-B-001highbugfixedOpening another song leaves the old song's track lanes playing in the engine

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-B-001-opening-another-song-leaves-the-old-song-s-track.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/autosync.rs:147-152 (all clears sent)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/autosync.rs:183-186 (a seat with no lanes and no entry in sent is skipped)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/seats.rs:195-210 (reseat keeps the seats the new song takes again, with no ClearSeat)
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:2076-2106 (replace_song → reseat → stream_opened → send_automation(true))

What happens

When a song with automation lanes on its tracks is replaced by another song (「新しい曲」, open a song, open a recent song) while the output is open, a track of the new song that sits on a seat the old song also used, and that has no lane of its own, is still moved by the old song's lanes. For example, the old track 1 had a volume lane that goes down to −∞; the new song's track 1 has no lanes. After the new song opens, its track 1 follows the old volume line (also when stopped, at the play line), and its fader in the song does nothing. The same for pan, sends, mute (the mute lane makes it silent), the knobs of a built-in sound, and the parameters of whatever effect or plugin the new song has in the same slot or as its instrument (the old lane's parameter number is sent to the new plugin). It stays so until that track's lanes change (a lane is added to it).

What should happen

The engine plays the lanes of the song that is open, and nothing else (docs/architecture.md 21-7: "the core packs each track's non-resting lane points into one box"; 21-5: a lane with no points, or none, leaves the hand-set value). The sibling send_audio(false) in play/audiosync.rs:119-130, called by the same reseat, does it right: with fresh == false it sends EngineOp::Clips also with an empty list to every taken seat, so the old song's audio clips go. The master's lane is also always told, "none" included (autosync.rs:153-161, architecture 25-32).

This is not the known hole of docs/progress.md line 377 (section "Round 12") ("a track's lanes deleted (or rested) while no stream runs stay in the engine"): here the stream runs the whole time, and nothing is deleted or rested by the person.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. With the output open, make a song whose track 1 has a volume lane with points (or a mute lane with a point at 1.0).
  2. 「新しい曲」 (answer 「保存しない」), or open another song whose track 1 has no lanes.
  3. Play, or just move the play line: track 1 of the new song follows the old lane; with the old mute lane it is silent.

Cause

replace_song calls reseat, which gives the new song's tracks seats 0..n. A seat that both songs use is not emptied (clear_seat only runs for old seats the new song does not take, seats.rs:204-208), so the engine's track keeps its AutoSet (EngineCommand::ClearTrack is the only thing besides SetAutomation that drops it). Then stream_opened calls send_automation(true), which first clears self.autosync.sent and then, for each seat, skips it when before.map_or(heads.is_empty(), …) is true: with sent empty, before is None, so every seat whose new track has no playing lane is skipped and no EngineOp::Automation { seat, lanes: [] } is sent. The gone loop cannot help either, because it walks the sent map that was just cleared.

Suggested fix

In send_automation, when all is true, send EngineOp::Automation { seat, lanes: Vec::new() } for every seat in now whose heads are empty (or: do not skip a seat with empty heads when all is true), as the master's lane is sent "also none". This also closes the known hole of progress.md line 377 (a new stream). No protocol or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_core_play_b_001): with the stream open (ready()), a volume lane with two points on track 1, then Play::replace_song(Song::new_default(), ..). Track 1 of the old song and of the new song both sit on seat 0, and among the effects of the replace there is no EngineOp::Automation and no EngineOp::ClearSeat at all. On the engine side (read): a track's AutoSet is replaced only by EngineCommand::SetAutomation (engine.rs:1951-1957) and dropped only by ClearTrack (engine.rs:2093-2112); seating another instrument does not touch it. So the old song's lanes keep moving the new song's track on that seat. I did not run the engine to hear it. Severity high stands (wrong sound, down to silence, in a song that has no lanes; the fader does nothing).

Corrections: none. This is wider than the known line in docs/progress.md ("lanes deleted (or rested) while no stream runs stay in the engine"): here a stream runs the whole time.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/autosync.rs send_automation: with all, do not pass over a seat whose heads are empty: send EngineOp::Automation { seat, lanes: Vec::new() } for it (as the master's lane is told "also none"). Seats the new song does not take are emptied by reseat already. Test in crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation/tests.rs: after a replace, every taken seat got an Automation op, empty for a track without lanes. The fix also closes the known line of docs/progress.md for a new stream; take that line out with the fix if a test shows it.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/autosync.rs send_automation: with all (a new stream, another song) a seat whose track has no playing lane is no longer passed over: it is sent EngineOp::Automation { seat, lanes: [] }, as the master's lane is told "also none". This also closes the known hole of docs/progress.md (a track's lanes deleted or rested while no stream runs stayed in the engine): a new stream is now told every seat's lanes. docs/architecture.md 25-32 says so.

Tests (in play/autosync.rs): another_song_leaves_no_lane_of_the_one_before_on_its_seats (「新しい曲」 → 「保存しない」 after a volume lane on track 1: every seat of the new song is told, with no lane) and a_new_stream_hears_of_a_lane_deleted_while_none_ran. Changed to the right behavior: a_song_without_lanes_tells_the_engine_nothing is now a_song_without_lanes_tells_a_new_stream_so_and_then_nothing (a new stream is told "no lane" once per seat, and nothing after), and play/tests.rs the_stream_opening_gets_everything now also expects Automation { seat: 0, lanes: [] }.

MODEL-A-001highbugfixedJoining clips keeps origin indexes on notes of a take with no origin, so the saved song cannot be opened

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-A-001-joining-clips-keeps-origin-indexes-on-notes-of-a.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:574-581 (join_clips: notes.push(Note { start, length, ..*n }) keeps n.from)
crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:595-601 (the joined take is made with origin: None)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1540-1543 (take_file writes NoteFile::From for every note whose from is Some)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1896-1907 (to_take: a From note in a take without origin makes the whole song None)

What happens

When one of the joined clips shows a quantized take, the joined take has no origin (origin: None), but its notes still carry the origin indexes (from: Some(i)) of the old quantized take. The song plays and edits normally. When it is saved (or the recovery file is written 2 s later), each such note is written as NoteFile::From(start, length, key, velocity, i). Opening that file again fails: to_take accepts a From note only when i < from_len, and from_len is 0 for a take with no origin, so to_song returns None and the song is ReadError::Broken. The recovery file is read with the same songfile::decode, so it cannot bring the song back either. The whole song is lost from the person's point of view.

What should happen

Note::from is "None for a note written, copied or pasted, and in a take with no origin" (crates/meloet-model/src/song.rs:33-35). Architecture 24-6 says the joined take keeps "other takes and the quantize origins are not kept". The sibling code that drops an origin does clear the indexes: unquantize sets n.from = None when it drops the origin (quantize.rs:164-168), and overdub_recording sets from: None when it merges clips and drops the origin (record/overdub.rs:104-110).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Record notes into clip A, then press Q (quantize) on it, so its shown take has an origin and its notes have from.
  2. Have a second keys clip B right after A on the same track.
  3. Select A and B, 「クリップをつなげる」 (Command::JoinClips, also the AI link's join).
  4. Save, close, and open the song again: it is refused as broken (also the recovery file after a crash).

Cause

join_clips builds each note of the new take with ..*n, which copies from from the shown take of each joined clip, while the new take is made with origin: None. The model never checks that from is only set under an origin, but the song file reader does, and turns the whole song away.

Suggested fix

In join_clips, write from: None for every note put into the joined take (as overdub_recording does for its merged take). No protocol or file-shape change. A test: join a quantized clip with a neighbour and check that no note of the joined take has from, and that songfile::decode(songfile::encode(..)) reads the song back.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_model_a_001): a new song with two neighbouring keys clips, quantize::quantize on the first, arrange::join_clips on both, then songfile::decode(&songfile::encode(..)). Before the join the song reads back; after it the joined take has origin: None and notes from: [Some(1), None], and decode gives Err(Broken). The recovery file goes through the same decode, so it is lost too. Severity high stands (the saved song cannot be opened).

Corrections: none; the lines and the cause are as written. Both callers (play/clips.rs:40 and the AI link's play/ai.rs:377) go through the same arrange::join_clips.

Fix plan: in crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs join_clips, write from: None in the Note pushed into the joined take. Tests: in crates/meloet-model/src/arrange/tests.rs (no note of the joined take has from), and in crates/meloet-core/src/songfile/tests.rs (quantize, join, decode(encode(..)) gives the song back). A song file already written this way stays unreadable; making the reader forgive a From note in a take with no origin (read it as plain) would bring such files back, but that is a choice for the lead and not needed for the fix.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs join_clips: every note put into the joined take gets from: None. crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs to_take (the lead's decision): a note with an origin index in a take with no origin reads as a plain note (the index is dropped), so songs and recovery files already written with this fault open again; an index past the origin's notes in a take that has an origin still does not read. docs/architecture.md 14 says so.

Tests: arrange::tests::joining_a_quantized_clip_keeps_no_origin_indexes (meloet-model), songfile::tests::a_joined_quantized_clip_comes_back and songfile::tests::an_origin_note_in_a_take_with_no_origin_reads_as_plain (meloet-core). Changed: songfile::tests::origins_that_cannot_be_do_not_read no longer counts "an origin note in a take with no origin" as broken.

MODEL-A-002highbugfixedPasting a kept vocal clip after its audio left the audio table makes a silent clip and a song file that cannot be opened

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-A-002-pasting-a-kept-vocal-clip-after-its-audio-left-t.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:183-200 (paste_clips: the kept clips go in through place as they were kept)
crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:261-270 (place: the new Doc keeps doc.audio as it is)
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1196-1199, 2093 (clip_board is kept across undo; it is cleared only when the song is replaced)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1307 and 1447-1456 (the file's audio table is used_audio(doc), taken from doc.audio only)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1627-1628 and 1501-1502 (audio_take: an audio take whose file is not in the table makes the song None)

What happens

Mod C keeps Arc<Clip>s (KeptClip). A vocal clip's takes hold their Arc<AudioSource> directly. If the step that put that file into the audio table (Doc::audio) is undone after the copy — 「音のファイルを入れる…」 (import_audio), a vocal recording, 「逆にする」 — the table no longer has the file, but the clip board still has the clip. Mod V (or 「貼りつけ」 on a vocal lane) then puts a vocal clip into the song whose file is not in Doc::audio.

  • The engine is told only the table (EngineOp::AudioSources { sources: doc.audio }, play/audiosync.rs:110-113), which does not hold that file.
  • Saving writes the clip's audio take with audio: <id>, but the file's table is used_audio(doc), a filter of doc.audio, so the file is not in it. Opening the song again fails at audio.get(&t.audio)? and the whole song is ReadError::Broken. The recovery file uses the same encoder, so it is broken too.

What should happen

Doc::audio is "every audio file a take of the song plays" (crates/meloet-model/src/song.rs:368). Every other step that puts an audio take into the song also puts its file into the table in the same step (import_audio, audio/clips.rs:301; add_vocal_recordings, audio/record.rs:215; reverse_clips, audio/reverse.rs:260). Architecture 17 ("Paste: Rust remembers the clips … takes are only shared by Arc") expects a paste to give the same song as the clips had.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. On a vocal track, 「音のファイルを入れる…」 a WAV file (one step, the file goes into Doc::audio).
  2. Click the new clip, Mod C.
  3. Mod Z (the import is undone; the table goes back to before it).
  4. Select the vocal track, Mod V.
  5. Save, close, open the song: it is refused as broken. (The same with a vocal recording instead of the import.)

Cause

paste_clips (through place) only places clips; it never looks at Doc::audio. Keys takes need no table, so this is right for keys clips, but a kept vocal clip can outlive its file's entry in the table, because History::undo brings back a Doc whose table does not have it while the core's clip board keeps the clip.

Suggested fix

In paste_clips, for every placed vocal clip, add each take.source that doc.audio does not hold (by AudioId) to a copy of the table, kept in the order of id, and set new.audio to it, in the same step. Its id was given out by the same next_id and is never given again, so it cannot collide. No protocol or file-shape change. A test: import, copy, undo, paste, then songfile::decode(songfile::encode(..)) reads the song back and Doc::audio holds the file.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_model_a_002): from the vocal song of songfile/vocal_tests.rs, one History step puts clip 43 and its file (audio 51) in; arrange::copy_clips of clip 43; History::undo; arrange::paste_clips on the vocal track. The pasted song's audio table is [AudioId(50)] only, and songfile::decode(&songfile::encode(..)) gives Err(Broken). The core keeps clip_board across undo (play.rs:1198, cleared only at play.rs:2093 when the song is replaced), and Play::paste_clips (play.rs:1245) has no guard of its own. Severity high stands.

Corrections: none. The engine side is as written too: EngineOp::AudioSources carries doc.audio only, so until the save the pasted clip is silent.

Fix plan: in crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs paste_clips (after place), for each kept vocal clip add every take.source whose AudioId is not in doc.audio to a copy of the table (sorted by id, as the reader does) and set it on the new Doc in the same step. Tests: crates/meloet-model/src/arrange/tests.rs (the table holds the file after the paste) and crates/meloet-core/src/songfile/vocal_tests.rs (import, copy, undo, paste, decode(encode(..))). The file on disk: check while fixing that a recording / import undone and then pasted still has its file where audiosync looks for it (the file of an undone step is kept for redo, so it should; I did not run that part).

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs paste_clips: each file a kept vocal clip plays that is not in Doc::audio goes back into a copy of the table (in the order of id), set on the pasted Doc in the same step; when nothing is missing the table stays the same Arc. The core's sync_audio_sources sees the new table and sends EngineOp::AudioSources with the file; undoing the paste sends the table without it. The file itself stays in the work place or the song folder while the song is open (architecture 22: the in-memory table keeps unused files, the work place is cleared only when the song is let go of), so the store finds it. docs/architecture.md 17 (Paste) says so.

Tests: arrange::tests::a_pasted_vocal_clip_brings_its_file_back_into_the_audio_table (meloet-model) and songfile::vocal_tests::a_vocal_clip_pasted_after_its_import_was_undone_keeps_its_file (meloet-core: Play import, copy, undo, paste; the engine gets the table with the file; decode(encode(..)) gives the song back; undo sends the table without it).

UI-ARRANGE-001highbugfixedAutomation lane: the kept look of a point drag is applied to the next lane's points and throws during render

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-001-automation-lane-the-kept-look-of-a-point-drag-is.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:37-38 (points / pts: empty while the points of the lane's new laneId have not arrived)
ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:74-80 (pending is cleared only by a $effect; shownDrag uses pending.drag with no check of pending.laneId)
ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:81-91 (rects: from: d.points.map((i) => pts[i]!))
ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:153-165 (up(): keeps pending after moveAutoPoints)
ui/src/components/automation/autoGeom.ts:214-217 (draggedPoints: pts[i]!.tick with indexes of the old lane)

What happens

After a point drag in an automation lane (or the master's lane) is released, pending keeps the drag so the points do not jump back. MoveAutoPoints makes a new LaneId (architecture 21-5: "new id whenever the points change"), so the state patch that answers the move gives the lane a new laneId whose points are not fetched yet (LaneStore.get is asked only when something reads them; nothing fetches ahead). In that flush pts is [], but shownDrag is still pending.drag (with moved: true), so A.draggedPoints([], drag) reads pts[i]!.tick of undefined and throws a TypeError.

The throw happens inside the use:glSlot update of the lane (a render effect), which Svelte 5.57 runs before user $effects, so the $effect that would clear pending (line 74) has not run yet. There is no <svelte:boundary> in the app, so the error leaves the batch: Batch.#process catches it, calls reset_all(root) (every effect of the app tree is marked clean) and throws it again (svelte 5.57.3, ui/node_modules/svelte/src/internal/client/reactivity/batch.js:366-378, reset_all at 1330; error-handling.js invoke_error_boundary rethrows with no boundary). Results, read from the code:

  • an uncaught TypeError in the console after every point drag in the real app;
  • the parts later in the tree than this lane, and every $effect of that batch (for example AutoClock's position refresh, the mixer, the history band count), do not take that state until something dirties them again;
  • when the new points arrive (autoState version goes up), the lane draws again; if pending is still set (the clearing $effect was marked clean and nothing has written pending, lane or current.rev since, which is the case when send() resolved before the patch came), the stale pending.drag is laid over the new points: the moved points are shown moved a second time (by dTick / dValue again), with accent frames at the moved places, until another state change makes the clearing $effect run. When send() resolves after the patch, its .then writes pending and the clearing runs at once, so only the throw is seen.

The fake bridge does nothing for moveAutoPoints (ui/src/bridge/fake.ts:3249-3252, no new lane id), and no test drags a point of a mounted lane, so tests do not see it.

The same throw can come during a drag (not only after it) if the lane's laneId changes while the point is held (for example Mod Z while holding a point, or an edit from the AI link): dragPoints (autoGeom.ts:203) and moveReading (AutoLane.svelte:101) read pts[d.grab]! of the new list.

What should happen

The kept look should show the moved points until the answer's points are there, then go, with no error (the comment at AutoLane.svelte:68: "The look kept after release ... so the point does not jump back"). The sibling CompBand.svelte:54 does it right: it uses pending only while pending.clip === clip.id.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. In the real app (not the fake scenes): add a volume lane to a track, add two points, drag one point sideways or up and down, release. When the answer arrives, the console shows TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'tick') from draggedPoints; depending on which comes first (the command's answer or the patch), the lane line may then show the point moved twice as far as dragged until anything else changes the state.

Cause

shownDrag = drag ?? pending?.drag ?? null (line 79) does not check that pending.laneId is still lane.laneId, and draggedPoints / rects index pts with the indexes of the old lane. The clearing $effect runs after the render effect that already threw.

Suggested fix

Apply the kept drag to the points it was made on, not to pts: while pending.laneId !== lane.laneId, draw draggedPoints over the points of pending.laneId (peekLanePoints still has them; LaneStore.keepOnly keeps dropped lanes), and drop pending once the new lane's points have arrived (points?.id === lane.laneId). At the least, use pending.drag only while pending.laneId === lane.laneId (as CompBand does) and make draggedPoints / dragPoints skip indexes that are not in pts. No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed, with a test that was run (2026-11-25, scratch worktree, not kept): a vitest test mounted AutoLane.svelte (svelte 5.57.3, jsdom) with a lane of three points (the points given through a mocked peekLanePoints), sent pointerdown / pointermove / pointerup on the middle point (moveAutoPoints was sent), then gave the lane prop the same lane with a new laneId whose points are not there, and called flushSync(). Result: flushSync threw TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'tick') with draggedPoints in the stack. In that run pending.laneId !== lane.laneId already held, so the clearing $effect would have dropped pending; the throw came first.

Why the order is so (read in ui/node_modules/svelte, 5.57.3): use:glSlot={{ kind: "rects", rects }} reads rects inside a render_effect (src/internal/client/dom/elements/actions.js:22), and render effects are run before user $effects. With no <svelte:boundary> in ui/src (grep: none), the error leaves Batch.#process, which calls reset_all(root) and throws again (reactivity/batch.js:366-378).

Not run, read only: the "moved a second time" picture after the new points arrive (it needs send() to resolve before the patch; the logic in the report is right as far as I can read it), and the throw during a held drag when the laneId changes (dragPoints, moveReading: the same pts[i]! on an empty or shorter list).

Rust side checked: auto::move_points returns a new LaneId (crates/meloet-model/src/automation.rs:425-429), so every real move goes this way. The fake bridge does nothing for moveAutoPoints, which is why no test saw it.

Severity stays high: an uncaught error on every point move in the real app, and the batch is thrown away (other parts of the screen miss that state).

Fix plan (agreed, the smallest): in ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte make the kept look safe at the source: const shownDrag = $derived(drag ?? (pending && pending.laneId === lane.laneId ? pending.drag : null)), and drop a held drag when lane.laneId is no longer the one it was started on (remember the lane id in the drag, or cancel it in the same derived). In ui/src/components/automation/autoGeom.ts make draggedPoints and dragPoints return the input unchanged when an index is not in pts (so a wrong index can never throw in render). With only this, the new lane's points are not here yet, so the lane is empty for the frames until they arrive (as it already is after an add or a delete). If the lead wants no blink, draw the old lane's points (peekLanePoints(pending.laneId)) with the drag until points?.id === lane.laneId; that is the report's first choice and also needs no protocol change. Add a test of the mounted lane with a new laneId after a move (the form above), and make the fake bridge give a new lane id on moveAutoPoints so scenes go the real way.

Fix

Not committed yet (the lead commits).

  • ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte: the kept look is tied to the lane id it was made on. While the answer's new lane id has no points yet, the drag is drawn over the points it was made on (peekLanePoints(pending.laneId)), and the kept look ends when the new points arrive (or, with the same lane id, when the rev is reached). A held drag remembers its lane id (dragLane): when the lane gets another id, it is not drawn, the next move cancels it and the release sends nothing.
  • ui/src/components/automation/autoGeom.ts: dragPoints and draggedPoints return the input unchanged when an index is not in the points, so a wrong index cannot throw while drawing.
  • ui/src/bridge/fake.ts: moveAutoPoints moves the points under a new lane id and chooses them, as auto::move_points does (fake scenes now go the real way).

Tests: ui/tests/automation.lane-drag.test.ts — "after release, the moved look stays on the old points until the new lane's points arrive; no error, and not moved twice", "a point held while the lane gets a new id (undo, the AI link) lets go of the drag: no error, nothing sent" (both threw TypeError … reading 'tick' before), and "the moved points under a new lane id, chosen; …" for the fake.

CORE-AUDIO-003mediumui-uxfixedWhen the files of a voice recording cannot be made, the recording goes on without the voice and nothing tells the person

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-003-when-the-files-of-a-voice-recording-cannot-be-ma.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs:591-615 (Side::start: fs::create_dir_all, WavFile::create, the folder sync; any error ends the start)
crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs:257-263 (start_vocals: the error goes only to stderr, then Input::VocalsNotStarted)
crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:1394 (Input::VocalsNotStarted => self.play.vocals_not_started(), no notice)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/vocal.rs:398-403 (vocals_not_started: the vocal tracks are dropped from the recording)

What happens

When the disk writer cannot make the files of a recording (no room on the disk, no right to write in the work place, no state folder, a file name the file system refuses), start_vocals writes meloet: cannot record into … to stderr and answers VocalsNotStarted. The rules then take the vocal tracks out of the recording, and the recording goes on (the engine was already told to record, play.rs:855-900). Nothing is shown: no status line, no dialog. The armed vocal track does not play its clips during the recording (it is in muted), no waveform grows, and after the stop no take is made for the voice. A person who sang a whole take learns only afterwards that nothing was kept, and never why.

What should happen

docs/bugs brief: an error must not be swallowed so the person never learns. The siblings tell the person when a recording cannot do what it should: the StatusMessage::NoInput notice (play.rs:838) when an armed track has no input, and the messages of spec 6 for a sound system or input that went away during recording (「…録音を止めました。そこまでの演奏は テイク 2 に残っています。」). docs/architecture.md 4-2: when recording cannot go on, "a notice is shown".

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Make the work place unwritable (for example chmod a-w ~/.local/state/meloet/work/音), or fill the disk.
  2. Arm a vocal track that has an input and press record.
  3. The count-in and the recording run as usual; nothing says the voice is not being recorded. After the stop there is no vocal take.

Cause

Input::VocalsNotStarted carries no reason, and its handler only clears rec.vocal.tracks; no StatusMessage is set.

Suggested fix

Set a notice in the Input::VocalsNotStarted arm of state.rs (and, if wanted, stop the recording when it had only vocal tracks). Needs new wording (spec 6 has none for this), so the text is the lead's or the owner's decision; no protocol change if an existing StatusMessage shape with a new variant is acceptable (that is a protocol addition).

Verification

Real, checked by reading (no test run). Side::start (vocalrec.rs:591-615) fails on create_dir_all, WavFile::create or the folder sync (and when no input is open); start_vocals (service/input.rs:257-263) prints to stderr and answers Input::VocalsNotStarted; its arm in state.rs:1394 only calls Play::vocals_not_started (play/vocal.rs:398-403), which drops the vocal tracks from the recording. No notice is set anywhere, and the recording goes on.

Looked for an existing message, as the lead asked: StatusMessage has NoInput, InputLost (「…が抜けました。録音を止めました。…」), RecordingStoppedAudioSystem / RecordingStoppedOutputMissing, RecordingGaps, DiskTooSlow (reading, while playing), BounceFailed (msg.bounceFailedWrite 「音にできませんでした。作業の場所に書けません。」), ClipWorkFailed (about clips). None says "the voice is not being recorded because its file could not be made"; each names another cause, so using one would mislead. ui/src/text/ja.ts has no other fitting key (the save dialog's 「ここには保存できませんでした（書きこむ権利がないか、空きが足りません）…」 is about the song's place).

Why needs-lead: the fix needs new wording (spec 6 has none) and a new StatusMessage variant (a protocol addition: decide the version). Choices:

  1. A new variant (for example VocalsNotStarted) with a text from the owner, set in the Input::VocalsNotStarted arm; when the recording had only vocal tracks, also stop it (nothing would be recorded). Cost: protocol + ja.ts / en.ts + the status line's table + a fake scene. Recommended. A text to offer the owner, in the words of msg.bounceFailedWrite: 「声を録れません。作業の場所に書けません。」 (and for a recording with keys: the keys go on).
  2. No new wording: stop the recording at once with no take and say nothing. Still silent; not recommended.
  3. Leave it and list it in docs/progress.md.

Severity medium stands: a whole take can be sung into nothing; it needs a work place that cannot be written.

Fix

Protocol 38 (the lead's decision): StatusMessage::VocalsNotRecorded { keys } (crates/meloet-protocol/src/lib.rs). crates/meloet-core/src/play/vocal.rs vocals_not_started returns whether the recording goes on with the keys; the Input::VocalsNotStarted arm of crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs sets the notice and stops a recording of vocal tracks alone, and Model::status shows this notice before the recording's own line, so it is seen at once. Texts msg.vocalsNotRecorded and msg.vocalsNotRecordedKeys (ui/src/text/ja.ts, en.ts; builder:2026-11-25, spec 7 A-24 "Texts when a recording or a file cannot be written"), shown by ui/src/components/StatusLine.svelte. Tests: state::vocal_tests::a_recording_of_vocals_alone_whose_files_cannot_be_made_stops_and_says_so, the step "Said at once, over the recording that goes on" in state::vocal_tests::a_recording_stopped_before_it_rolls_leaves_no_file, tests38::the_messages (protocol), and the rows of 「知らせ」 in ui/tests/connect-components.test.ts.

CORE-AUDIO-004mediumui-uxfixedA recording that a full disk or the 4 GB wav limit stops ends with no notice, unlike a recording the sound system stops

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-004-a-recording-that-a-full-disk-or-the-4-gb-wav-lim.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs:959-962 (Job::write: a file without room sets said.full)
crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs:684-686, 988-990 (a failed write or fdatasync sets said.error)
crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs:423-427 (poll_input: full or error → Input::VocalsCannotGoOn)
crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:1409-1413 (VocalsCannotGoOn only calls stop_recording; no notice is set)

What happens

When the disk is full, a write or sync of a voice file fails, or a file reaches the size a wav can hold (about 4 h 8 min at 48 kHz, 24-bit stereo; 93 min at 96 kHz, 32-bit stereo), the disk writer marks the status and the core stops the recording exactly as if the person had pressed stop. The take is kept, but the status line says nothing about why the recording stopped. Only stderr gets "the recording was not written whole" (for an error; nothing at all for the 4 GB limit). The person sees the recording end by itself and may press record again into the same full disk.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 22-7: "4 GB limit …: stop recording before the limit and keep what was recorded (same path as "the sound stopped", 4-2)". 4-2: "If the sound stops while recording, the recording is closed …, what was recorded is kept as a take, and a notice is shown." The sibling for a stream that went away does show one: cut_notice (state/vocal.rs:65-73, RecordingStoppedAudioSystem / RecordingStoppedOutputMissing, spec 6 「…録音を止めました。そこまでの演奏は テイク 2 に残っています。」).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put the state folder on a nearly full file system (or fill the disk while recording).
  2. Record a vocal track until a write fails with "no space".
  3. The recording stops; the take is there, and nothing tells why.

(The 4 GB case: record a stereo vocal track at 96 kHz with 32 bits for 93 minutes.)

Cause

Input::VocalsCannotGoOn carries neither the reason nor the take, and its arm in state.rs sets no StatusMessage; vocals_written returns a Cut only for the stream cases.

Suggested fix

Keep the reason (full or an error) with the recording, as Cut is kept for a stream that went away, and set a notice when the take is in, like cut_notice. Needs wording for "the disk is full" and "the file reached its largest size" (spec 6 has neither), so the lead or the owner decides the text; a new StatusMessage variant is a protocol addition.

Verification

Real, checked by reading (no test run). Job::write sets said.full when a file has no room left (vocalrec.rs:959-962) and said.error on a failed write or sync; poll_input (service/input.rs:423-427) turns either into Input::VocalsCannotGoOn; its arm (state.rs:1409-1413) only calls stop_recording. The take is kept, and no notice is set; a stream that went away gets one through cut_notice (RecordingStoppedAudioSystem / RecordingStoppedOutputMissing). Architecture 22-7 says the 4 GB stop goes the "same path as the sound stopped (4-2)", and 4-2 says "a notice is shown".

Looked for an existing message: RecordingStoppedAudioSystem names PipeWire, RecordingStoppedOutputMissing names the output, InputLost names the input that was unplugged (「…録音を止めました。録れた所までは残しています。」), RecordingGaps and DiskTooSlow are about skips. None can be used without saying a wrong cause. ja.ts has pack.diskFull (about the sound pack) and nothing for a recording.

Why needs-lead: new wording for two causes (the disk cannot be written; the file reached the largest size a wav can hold) and a new StatusMessage variant (protocol addition). Choices:

  1. One new variant with a reason (full / error), said when the take is in, as cut_notice does: for example 「ディスクに書けなくなったので、録音を止めました。録れた所までは残しています。」 and 「ファイルがいちばん大きい所まで来たので、録音を止めました。録れた所までは残しています。」 (the second half is the wording of msg.inputLost). The reason travels with the recording from Input::VocalsCannotGoOn { full }. Recommended; the owner decides the text.
  2. One text for both causes (shorter, less exact).
  3. Leave it and list it in docs/progress.md.

Severity medium stands (the take is kept, but the person is not told why the recording ended, and can record again into the same full disk).

Fix

Protocol 38 (the lead's decision): StatusMessage::RecordingStoppedDisk { reason } with DiskStop::{CannotWrite, FileFull} (crates/meloet-protocol/src/lib.rs, vocal.rs). Input::VocalsCannotGoOn carries the reason (crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs poll_input: a write error is CannotWrite, else the wav size limit FileFull); its arm in crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs keeps it with the recording as Cut::Disk (crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs, play/vocal.rs vocals_cut), and cut_notice (state/vocal.rs) says it once the take is in, as for a stream that went away. Texts msg.recordingStoppedDisk and msg.recordingStoppedFileFull (builder:2026-11-25), StatusLine.svelte. Tests: state::vocal_tests::a_recording_its_files_cannot_be_written_on_stops_and_says_why_once_the_take_is_in, tests38::the_messages, the rows of 「知らせ」 in ui/tests/connect-components.test.ts.

CORE-EXPORT-001mediumbugfixedmp3 export check says the file does not clip when 32-bit was chosen for wav before

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-EXPORT-001-mp3-export-check-says-the-file-does-not-clip-whe.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs:919 (check: let clips = over > 0 && self.bits != RecordFormat::Bits32 && !matched;)
crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs:524-531 (ExportChange::Format: the bits are kept when the new format has no bits)
crates/meloet-core/src/export/sink.rs:384-391 (Mp3Sink::push clamps every sample to -1.0..=1.0)

What happens

The check of the export dialog decides clips from self.bits alone. When the person chose 「32 ビット」 for wav in 「くわしく」 and then switches the format to mp3, self.bits stays Bits32 (mp3 has no bits; they are kept for the other formats). So for an mp3 whose render goes over 0 dB, the check says clips: false: the screen shows only 「いちばん大きい所 … ・ 音割れなし」-style text with no clipping lines, and the button is 「書き出す」 instead of 「このまま書き出す」. But the mp3 sink clamps every sample to full scale, so the written mp3 is clipped at every place counted in over. The person is told the file is fine and gets a clipped file.

What should happen

ExportCheck::Done::clips is "the file as it would be written with bits clips" (protocol crates/meloet-protocol/src/export.rs, doc of ExportCheck, spec 6 「書き出しで音が割れる」). An mp3 is always written within full scale (sink.rs, the comment of Mp3Sink::push: "the file holds what a player can play"), so for mp3 clips must be over > 0 (unless matched), whatever bits were kept for wav. Only a 32-bit float wav keeps samples over 0 dB.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open 「書き出し…」 on a song whose master goes over 0 dB (for example one track with its fader at +6 dB).
  2. Format wav, open 「くわしく」, choose 32 ビット.
  3. Switch the format to mp3. Wait for the check to finish.
  4. The check shows no clipping and the button says 「書き出す」; the mp3 written is clipped.

(The unit test a_matched_file_does_not_clip_and_its_gain_can_be_down only covers wav.)

Cause

check looks at self.bits without looking at self.format. ExportChange::Format keeps self.bits when format.bits_choices() is empty (mp3, MIDI), as spec 3 F2 asks ("The bits chosen for wav and flac stay as they were"), so Bits32 can be the kept value while the format is mp3.

Suggested fix

In check, count the bits as floats only when the format takes them: for example let floats = self.format == ExportFormat::Wav && self.bits == RecordFormat::Bits32; let clips = over > 0 && !floats && !matched; (flac cannot hold Bits32: ExportChange::Format resets it.) No protocol, wording or looks change. A test: wav 32 bits, then mp3, a render with over > 0 gives clips: true.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). check (export.rs:919): clips = over > 0 && self.bits != RecordFormat::Bits32 && !matched, with no look at self.format. ExportChange::Format (export.rs:524-531) keeps self.bits when the new format has no bits choices (mp3, MIDI), so Bits32 chosen for wav stays while the format is mp3. Mp3Sink::push (export/sink.rs:380-391) clamps every sample to ±1.0. So the check says "no clipping" for an mp3 that is written clipped. Severity medium stands (the person is told the file is fine; the way around is to set wav back to 24 bits).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs check: let floats = self.format == ExportFormat::Wav && self.bits == RecordFormat::Bits32; let clips = over > 0 && !floats && !matched; (ask self.format.bits_choices().contains(&RecordFormat::Bits32) instead of naming wav if that reads better). Look for other readers of self.bits that forget the format (the done text, the job's own bits) while there. Test in export/tests.rs or mp3_tests.rs: wav 32 bits, then mp3, a render with over > 0 → clips: true.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs check: the bits count as floats only when the format takes 32 bits (self.bits == Bits32 && self.format.bits_choices().contains(&Bits32)), so an mp3 with over > 0 clips whatever bits were kept for wav. The other readers of self.bits were looked at: the job's target and the done view carry the bits, but the mp3 sink does not read them and the done view shows the bitrate for mp3. docs/architecture.md 23-7 and 23-8 say "a 32-bit wav".

Test: export::tests::an_mp3_clips_whatever_bits_were_kept_for_wav (export/tests.rs).

CORE-EXPORT-002mediumbugfixedA file name typed in the export save window loses the text after its last dot

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-EXPORT-002-a-file-name-typed-in-the-export-save-window-lose.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs:738-741 (ExportNews::PlacePicked: path.file_stem() becomes self.name)
crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs:896-899 (file_name: format!("{name}.{}", self.format.extension()))

What happens

The name chosen in the save window of 「えらぶ…」 is kept as Path::file_stem, which cuts off everything after the last dot, whether or not that part is an audio ending. The format's ending is then added. A person who types 「デモ v1.2」 (no ending) gets the file 「デモ v1.wav」; 「Mix 2026.11.25」 becomes 「Mix 2026.11.wav」. Worse, the save window asked about writing over the name that was typed (set_do_overwrite_confirmation(true), crates/meloet-app/src/pcwindow.rs:132-135), not about the name that is really written. If 「デモ v1.wav」 is already in the folder (an earlier export), it is written over without any question.

What should happen

Only the format's own ending is replaced ("The ending is the format's, whatever was typed", export/tests.rs, the test of ChooseExportPlace). Text that is not an audio ending stays part of the name. The sibling split_save_as (crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs:565-571) does this right for 「名前をつけて保存…」: it strips only the .meloet suffix and keeps the rest of the name.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Export once to 「~/音楽/デモ v1.wav」.
  2. Open 「書き出し…」 again, press 「えらぶ…」, type 「デモ v1.2」 in the save window and press save (no question: that file does not exist).
  3. The dialog's place shows 「~/音楽/デモ v1.wav」; 「書き出す」 writes over the first file.

Cause

file_stem() treats any last dot as the start of an ending. Names with dots (versions, dates) are common.

Suggested fix

In PlacePicked, take the whole file name and strip only an ending meloet writes (wav, flac, mp3, mid, any case), as split_save_as does for .meloet; otherwise keep the whole name. No protocol, wording or looks change. A test: 「デモ v1.2」 gives the place 「…/デモ v1.2.wav」, 「できた.FLAC」 gives 「…/できた.wav」 under wav.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). ExportNews::PlacePicked (export.rs:738-741) keeps path.file_stem() as the name, and file_name (export.rs:896-899) adds the format's ending. Path::file_stem cuts at the last dot whatever follows it, so 「デモ v1.2」 becomes 「デモ v1.wav」. GTK's save window asks about writing over the name that was typed (pcwindow.rs:132-135), and the export itself moves the finished file over whatever has the final name (export/job.rs move_to, a plain rename), so the cut name can replace an earlier export with no question. split_save_as (state/songs.rs:565-571) strips only its own ending.

Severity changed from low to medium, as the finder also thought: names with a dot (a version, a date) are normal use, the file gets a name the person did not type, and an earlier export under the cut name is replaced without the question the window is there to ask. It is not high: what is lost is an exported file, not the song.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs PlacePicked: take path.file_name() and strip only an ending meloet writes (the endings of ExportFormat, any case: wav, flac, mp3, mid); keep everything else. Test in export/tests.rs next to the ChooseExportPlace test: 「デモ v1.2」 → 「デモ v1.2.wav」; 「できた.FLAC」 under wav → 「できた.wav」.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs ExportNews::PlacePicked: the name is the whole file name typed with only the chosen format's own ending taken off, in any case (typed_name); other text after a dot stays (「デモ v1.2」 → 「デモ v1.2.wav」; 「できた.flac」 under wav → 「できた.flac.wav」, the lead's decision). When the file that would be written is not the one the window asked about (typed without the ending, or in other capitals) and is there already, the place is not taken: the dialog keeps again and Exporting::settle shows the save window again with that file's name, so the PC's own "write over?" question is about it; put away, the place stays as it was. No new wording. docs/architecture.md 22-20 (the export place) says so.

Tests: export::tests::a_name_typed_keeps_its_dots_and_only_the_formats_ending_is_taken_off and export::tests::the_window_comes_again_to_ask_about_the_file_really_written (export/tests.rs).

CORE-PLAY-A-001mediumbugfixedClicking a clip on a vocal track selects it without the vocal-track rules: the keys lose their target and the track gets no record arm

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-A-001-clicking-a-clip-on-a-vocal-track-selects-it-with.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1070-1079 (Play::click: sets session.play_target directly)
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1061-1064 (select_clip) and 1096-1097 (select_clips with one clip) call click
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play/vocal.rs:203-216 (selected_track_changed, the rules that are skipped)
  • Same root cause, rarer: crates/meloet-core/src/play/tracks.rs:24-27 (delete_track moves the selection) and crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1414-1417 (after_change moves it to the first track) also set play_target without selected_track_changed.

What happens

A click on an audio clip of a vocal track (Command::SelectClip, or SelectClips with add/toggle and one clip) makes that vocal track the selected track, but:

  • session.last_keys is not set to the keys track that was selected before. Song::keys_target() then falls back to last_keys, which is None (or an older keys track). The input meter's 「弾くと鳴る先」 shows 「—」 (or a wrong track) and the keyboard stops sounding, although the person only clicked a clip.
  • The vocal track does not get its ● (armed_vocals is not changed), so R does not record the voice (and with no keys target either, recording is not possible at all). A click on the same track's header (SelectTrack) does both.
  • The NoInput notice that SelectTrack gives for a vocal track with no input (state.rs 1857) is not given either.

What should happen

Spec 3 (3-bands.md line 99): "Pressing a clip also selects its track. The selected track becomes 「弾くと鳴る先」." Spec 3 line 256 (94-1, 35-A): "While a vocal track is selected: the keyboard keeps playing on the keyboard track selected last". Architecture 22-15 / 24-C: a vocal track that is selected gets its ● (selected_track_changed doc comment: "a vocal track that is selected gets its ●"). Selecting a track by its clip must follow the same rules as selecting it by its header (Play::select_track, play.rs 1043-1052), as open_piano_roll (clips.rs 73-76), make_copy (tracks.rs 126-129) and place_pattern (patterns.rs 150-154) already do. The fake (ui/src/bridge/fake.ts settleSong) also keeps the keys on the previous keys track when selectClip selects a vocal track.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open a saved song with keys track 1 and vocal track 2 that has an audio clip (last_keys and armed_vocals are not saved, so both start empty), and click track 1's header so the keys sound on track 1.
  2. Click the audio clip on track 2 in the arrange view.
  3. song.keysTarget is now null: pressing keys sounds nothing and the input meter shows 「—」. Track 2's ● stays off, so nothing can be recorded (TransportView::can_record is false; R gives NoArmedTrack).

Clicking track 2's header instead keeps the keys on track 1 (last_keys = 1) and turns its ● on. (When last_keys holds an older keys track from an earlier vocal selection, the keys go to that older track instead of track 1.)

Cause

Play::click writes self.song.session.play_target = Some(track.id) (play.rs 1073) and never calls selected_track_changed(before), which is the only place that sets last_keys and inserts into armed_vocals for a newly selected vocal track. select_track calls it; the clip path does not.

Suggested fix

In Play::click, when not busy and the track differs, use the same path as select_track: let before = self.song.session.play_target.replace(track.id); if before != Some(track.id) { self.selected_track_changed(before); }. Consider the same in delete_track and the after_change fallback. If the NoInput notice should also be said on a clip click, state.rs would check selected_vocal() after SelectClip as it does after SelectTrack (lead's choice). No protocol, file or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Play::click (play.rs:1070-1079) writes session.play_target = Some(track.id) and nothing else; selected_track_changed (play/vocal.rs:203-216), the only place that sets last_keys and adds to armed_vocals, is called by select_track, open_piano_roll (clips.rs:75), make_copy (tracks.rs:129), place_pattern (patterns.rs:153) and vocal.rs:245, not by click. The screen sends only selectClip for a press on a clip (Arrange.svelte:1378-1434), no selectTrack with it. With last_keys empty, Song::keys_target() is None after the click, so the keys sound nowhere and the vocal track has no ●. Severity medium stands.

Corrections: when the selection moves from a keys track to a vocal track by a clip click, last_keys is not cleared either, so the second sentence of "What happens" holds as written (an older keys track can be the target).

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs Play::click: let before = self.song.session.play_target.replace(track.id); if before != Some(track.id) { self.selected_track_changed(before); } inside the !self.busy() branch. The same two lines where delete_track (play/tracks.rs:24-27) and after_change (play.rs:1414-1417) move the selection, if a test shows they land on a vocal track (they are the same rule; keep the change to what a test proves). The NoInput notice on a clip click: give it the way SelectTrack does in state.rs:1857 (the existing message, no new wording). Tests in crates/meloet-core/src/play/clips_tests.rs or tests.rs: after SelectClip of a vocal clip, keys_target is the keys track selected before and the vocal track is armed.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs Play::click (a clip clicked, SelectClip and SelectClips with one clip): when the selected track changes, selected_track_changed(before) runs, as for a click on the track's header. The same rule where the selection moves by itself: play/tracks.rs delete_track (with the deleted track as the one before, on the song before the step) and the switch to the first track in play.rs after_change (with none before: that track is gone). crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs: SelectClip and SelectClips give the existing NoInput notice when the click made a vocal track the selected one and there is no input (no_input_if_vocal_selected).

Tests (in play/clips_tests.rs): a_click_on_a_vocal_clip_selects_its_track_as_its_head_does, a_vocal_track_selected_in_place_of_a_deleted_one_gets_its_arm, a_vocal_track_selected_when_undo_takes_the_selected_one_away_gets_its_arm.

CORE-PLAY-A-002mediumbugfixedL with no loop range while playing makes the 4-bar range at the bar where play started, not at the play line

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-A-002-l-with-no-loop-range-while-playing-makes-the-4-b.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:991-996 (Play::toggle_loop: map.meter_at(self.position))
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:490-491 (position: "while moving, where it started")

What happens

With no loop range in the song, L (or the keyboard's Cycle / Middle button, state/buttons.rs 84) while the song plays makes the range 4 bars from the bar where play was started (or last located), not from the bar the play line is in. Played from bar 1 and pressed at bar 20, the loop becomes bars 1–5. The engine is past that loop's end, so it does not go round (engine.rs SetLoop: before_loop_end is false); the ruler shows a loop far behind the play line, and the next Space jumps back to bar 1 (play_stop starts at the loop's head when the position is outside it).

What should happen

Spec 3 (3-bands.md line 52): "Turning the loop on when there is no range gives 4 bars from the bar of the current position." While playing, the current position is the play line (what the listener hears). Every other command in this file that works "where the play line is" while moving uses Play::play_line() (play.rs 961-970): paste_clips (1242), set_tempo and set_meter (timeline.rs 197, 226), add_marker (timeline.rs 378), step_marker, open_piano_roll, place_pattern, bar_step.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. New song with no loop range; Space at bar 1.
  2. When the play line is at bar 20, press L.
  3. The loop is bars 1–5 (EngineOp::Loop { start: 0, end: 4 * bar }), the song keeps playing past it.

The test the_loop_turned_on_with_no_range_takes_four_bars_from_the_current_one (play/tests.rs 323) only checks the stopped case, where position is the play line.

Cause

toggle_loop reads self.position, which while playing is the tick play started from (or the last Locate), not the heard position.

Suggested fix

Use self.play_line() in place of self.position at play.rs 994 (let head = map.meter_at(self.play_line());). Stopped, play_line() is position, so the stopped case does not change. No protocol, file or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Play::toggle_loop (play.rs:986-1000) uses map.meter_at(self.position), and position is "where play and record start from; while moving, where it started" (play.rs:490-491). play_line() (play.rs:961-970) is what the other "where the play line is" commands use. Spec 3 line 52: "4 bars from the bar of the current position"; nothing in 7-readings.md reads it as the start position. The existing test covers only the stopped case. Severity medium stands.

Corrections: none. (What the engine does with a loop that ends behind the play line I did not run; the report's reading of SetLoop is not needed for the fault.)

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs toggle_loop: map.meter_at(self.play_line()). Test in crates/meloet-core/src/play/tests.rs next to the_loop_turned_on_with_no_range_takes_four_bars_from_the_current_one: playing, with a heard position in bar 20, L gives bars 20 to 24.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs toggle_loop: the 4 bars start at the bar of play_line() (what the listener hears while moving; position when stopped).

Test (in play/tests.rs): the_loop_turned_on_with_no_range_while_playing_takes_the_bar_of_the_play_line.

CORE-PLAY-B-002mediumbugfixedPressing M on a track whose mute lane moves flips the hidden hand mute, not what is heard

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-B-002-pressing-m-on-a-track-whose-mute-lane-moves-flip.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/mix.rs:20-23 (toggle_mute: edit::toggle_mute flips t.muted, then rest_with)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:916-917 (the sibling mute_pressed takes the opposite of the lane's value)

What happens

A track has a mute lane that moves (not resting, with points). While stopped, or playing with 「● 書く」 off, the person presses M. What sounds and what the mixer's M shows is the lane's value at the play line (ui/src/components/mixer/MixerStrips.svelte:99-103, shownMuted), not the hand-set muted of the song, which the person cannot see. The press flips that hidden hand value and rests the lane in one step. When the hidden value already equals what the lane gives at the play line, the press changes nothing that is heard or shown, except that the lane goes to 「休み」:

  • the lane says "muted" (1.0) at the play line, the hidden hand mute is off: M is lit and the track is silent. Pressing M to unmute sets the hand mute on and rests the lane: the track stays silent, M stays lit. A second press is needed.
  • the lane says "playing" (0.0), the hidden hand mute is on (the lane was drawn after the track was muted): pressing M to mute sets the hand mute off: the track keeps playing.

The status line still says the mute was moved by hand and its automation was put to rest (StatusMessage::AutomationRested, msg.automationRested), although nothing changed.

What should happen

Spec 3 (Y16, docs/spec/3-bands.md:204): when a parameter that has automation is moved by hand, the lane rests and "the value stays where the hand put it". For a mute button the hand puts it to the opposite of what it shows. The sibling path in the same feature does this right: with 「● 書く」 on while playing, mute_pressed (automation.rs:916-917) reads lane.value_at(tick) and writes the opposite. docs/spec/7-readings.md:445 (mute sets) also describes resting "as pressing M does" only when the mute changes.

The test a_mute_lane_takes_the_place_of_the_mute_put_by_hand (play/automation/tests.rs:229-233) asserts the present result (hand mute on, lane at 0.0, press → hand mute off, silent unchanged), so it would need to change with the fix.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Track 1: add a mute lane, put one point at 1.0 at tick 0 (the track goes silent; M in the mixer is lit). The hand mute stays off.
  2. Stopped (or playing with 「● 書く」 off), press M on track 1 in the mixer.
  3. The track stays silent and M stays lit; the lane is at rest. Undo count is +1.

Cause

toggle_mute builds the edit with edit::toggle_mute(self.doc(), track), which flips Track::muted, the hand-set value, whatever the lane makes heard. rest_with then rests the lane, so from then on the hand value is what counts. When the hand value was already equal to the lane's value at the play line, the new hand value is the opposite of what the person asked for.

Suggested fix

In toggle_mute, when the lane moves, take the target value as mute_pressed does: wanted = !(lane.value_at(self.play_line()).unwrap_or(hand) >= 0.5), and set muted = wanted (a set, not a toggle) together with the rest via rest_with (when the hand value is already wanted, the edit is unchanged and apply_resting makes the rest step alone). Needs a small model helper to set the mute to a value (or toggle only when t.muted != wanted). No protocol or file change; the test above changes.

Verification

Confirmed by reading; the existing test a_mute_lane_takes_the_place_of_the_mute_put_by_hand (play/automation/tests.rs:193-237) shows today's behavior itself: hand mute on, a mute lane with a point 0.0 (the track plays, M is not lit), ToggleMute → the lane rests, the hand mute goes off, and silent is empty: the track still plays after the person pressed M to mute it.

Is it meant? I found nothing that says so. Spec 3 (Y16, 3-bands.md:204): the lane rests and "the value stays where the hand put it"; for a button the hand puts it to the opposite of what it shows, and the mixer shows the lane's value at the play line (MixerStrips.svelte:99-103 shownMuted), not the hidden muted. 7-readings.md has no reading for M under a mute lane (line 445 only says a mute set rests the lane "as pressing M does"). Architecture 21-11 says "value + rest in one step" for a control whose lane moves, and the comment of toggle_mute says "the mute and its rest as one step"; neither says the value is the flip of the hidden one. The sibling mute_pressed (play/automation.rs:904-922), the same press with 「● 書く」 on, reads lane.value_at(tick) and writes the opposite. So the test fixes an accident of the first build, not a decision, and it changes with the fix. Severity medium stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/mix.rs toggle_mute: when the track's mute lane moves, wanted = !(lane.value_at(self.play_line()).unwrap_or(hand) >= 0.5); build the edit only when t.muted != wanted (then edit::toggle_mute), else pass Err(EditError::Unchanged) to rest_with, which then makes the rest step alone. No model change is needed. Tests: change the last part of a_mute_lane_takes_the_place_of_the_mute_put_by_hand (after the press the track is silent and muted is true) and add the other case (lane 1.0, hand off: the press leaves the track playing with muted false). If the lead reads Y16 the other way, the choice is to keep the code and write the reading into 7-readings.md; I do not recommend it.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/mix.rs: toggle_mute (M on the screen) with a mute lane that moves sets the hand's mute to the opposite of what the lane gives at the play line, lane.value_at(self.play_line()), and rests the lane; when the hand's mute is that already, the step is the rest alone (RestLane). The AI's set_mute, which sets the hand's mute that get_mixer shows by sending ToggleMute, keeps flipping the hand's mute: crates/meloet-core/src/state/ai.rs sends the AI's ToggleMute to the new Play::ai_toggle_mute. docs/architecture.md 21-11 says both. Tests: play::automation::tests::a_mute_lane_takes_the_place_of_the_mute_put_by_hand changed (hand mute on, lane at 0.0: the press now leaves the hand's mute on, rests the lane, and the track is silent; it used to turn the hand's mute off and the track kept playing), play::automation::tests::the_mute_writes_a_point_or_rests changed (stopped where the lane gives 1.0, the press leaves the hand's mute off and the step is RestLane; it used to turn the hand's mute on), new play::automation::tests::m_on_a_mute_lane_that_mutes_lets_the_track_play, new play::mix::tests::the_ai_flips_the_mute_put_by_hand_whatever_its_lane_gives.

CORE-PLAY-C-002mediumbugfixedAn AI state put whose plugin process stops after taking the bytes is loaded again with the put state, not the song's

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-002-an-ai-state-put-whose-plugin-process-stops-after.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/hosts.rs:656-666 (Ask::SetState: on Reply::Ok the put state becomes Slot::state, the state reload uses)
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3574-3591 (PluginAsk::PutState: set_state, then state; a Gone of the second one is told as StatePut(Err(Gone)))
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/aistate.rs:186-190 (Err(HostError::Gone) → revive(ticket, restored), from_song only after an undo or redo)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/aistate.rs:351-366 (reload_at → Effect::ReloadPlugin, which loads what Hosts remembers)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/aistate.rs:199-203 (the text the AI is given)

What happens

When the AI link puts a state into a plugin (set_plugin_state, set_plugin_state_text, load_preset, the Surge XT tools) while the sound runs, the plugin host answers the set with Ok before the plugin has really taken the state: per docs/architecture.md 24-16 G, Surge XT takes it only in its next audio block, and the host makes the following GetState wait up to 3 blocks. If the process stops in that time (the state crashes the plugin as it is read in the audio block, or it hangs and is killed), the core gets StatePut(Err(Gone)) and loads the plugin again with Effect::ReloadPlugin, and Hosts loads it with the state it remembers, which is now the state the AI put in, not the song's. The AI is told "it is loaded again with the song's state, and no step was made", and the song keeps its old state, but the plugin plays the AI's state; a later save asks the plugin and writes the AI's state into the file without a step. When that state is what crashed the plugin, the reload crashes again (plugin_unreadable leaves it Stopped), and every 「もう一度読みこむ」 the person presses loads the same state and crashes again: the plugin can only be removed.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 24-16 G: "The process stops (Gone) → ReloadPlugin with the state Hosts kept (a failed set does not replace it)", and AiPutEnd::Stopped (ai.rs:716-718): "The plugin's process stopped while it took it (the state may be broken): it is loaded again with the song's state." After a put that ends Stopped, the song and the plugin have the song's state (ai.rs:704-705: "after the others the song and the plugin have the song's state").

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A track with Surge XT (VST3) plays (or at least the output runs, so its blocks run).
  2. Through the AI link, set_plugin_state with a container whose component part is broken (architecture 24-16 G: "A Surge XT VST3 state with the component part cut in half makes the plugin host crash (SIGSEGV)"; with the sound running the plugin reads it in its next block, after SetState was answered Ok).
  3. Hosts stores the bytes as Slot::state; the GetState that follows gets Gone; the core reloads with Effect::ReloadPlugin; Hosts::reload puts the broken bytes in again.

The same without a crashing state: any stop of the process between the Ok of the set and the answer to the read (a hang killed after 5 s) reloads the plugin with the AI's state while the song keeps its own.

Cause

Run::answer for Ask::SetState remembers a state as soon as the process answers the request, which for a plugin with running blocks only means the bytes were handed over. ai_state_put treats Err(Gone) as "the set failed" and revives through ReloadPlugin (from Hosts' memory) unless an undo or redo came in between (restored).

Suggested fix

In ai_state_put, for Err(HostError::Gone) always revive the put plugin from the song: self.revive(ticket, true, effects) (the from_song path, load_from_song, already exists for the undo case). The other plugins of the same process that are revived keep using Hosts' memory. Alternatively, Hosts could remember a put state only after a later GetState of that slot answers. No protocol, wire or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run; it needs a real plugin with the sound running, which I did not start). Run::answer for Ask::SetState (hosts.rs:656-666) stores the put bytes as Slot::state as soon as the host process answers Ok. PluginAsk::PutState (service.rs:3574-3591) sends set_state and then state; when the first is Ok and the second is Gone, the core gets StatePut(Err(Gone)). ai_state_put (aistate.rs:186-190) calls revive(ticket, restored), and from_song is true only after an undo or redo; else reload_at pushes Effect::ReloadPlugin, and Run::reload loads slot.state, which is now the put state. Architecture 24-16 G says a state put while blocks run is taken by Surge XT only in its next block, after SetState was answered; so a crash there comes after the Ok. The same chapter promises "ReloadPlugin with the state Hosts kept (a failed set does not replace it)" and the answer text says "loaded again with the song's state". The case measured in 24-16 G (the half-cut state, "it is reloaded and the song is safe") was with no sound running, where the set itself fails; that case is right today. Severity medium stands (the song's plugin plays a state the song does not hold, and a state that crashes is put in again at every reload).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/aistate.rs ai_state_put: for Err(HostError::Gone) call self.revive(ticket, true, effects), so the put plugin is loaded anew from the song (load_from_song); the other plugins of the same process keep the ReloadPlugin path. Test in the aistate tests: a put whose answer is Gone gives Effect::LoadPlugin with the song's state, not ReloadPlugin. If crates/meloet-ai/tests/plugstate.rs (ignored, real plugins) has the half-cut case, add the same with the sound running when someone can run it.

Fix

As the lead decided (not the plan above): Hosts keeps for a reload what it had until the plugin's own state is read back after an AI put. Hosts::try_state (crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/hosts.rs) sets a state without remembering it; the Hosts::state that follows remembers what the plugin says, and a stop in between reloads the state from before the put. PluginAsk::PutState uses it (service.rs); the put-backs of undo and redo (Hosts::set_state) are remembered at once as before. ReloadPlugin stays the way back for the put plugin, so the AI is now told what is reloaded: "the state it had before the put (with the song's state when an undo or a redo came meanwhile)" (play/plugs/aistate.rs, crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/plugstate.rs, the doc of AiPutEnd::Stopped in ai.rs, docs/architecture.md 24-16 G).

Tests: tests/plugin_state_put.rs a_reload_is_at_the_rate_asked_for_and_without_a_state_tried_and_never_read_back (a stand-in plugin host writes down what it is given: after try_state and a stop, the reload gets the state from before; after set_state, the state put); play::plugs::aistate::tests::a_state_that_stops_the_process_makes_no_step_and_the_plugin_is_loaded_again checks the text.

CORE-PLAY-C-003mediumbugfixedA track's plugin that became ready under a replace audition loses its state when the sample rate changes

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-003-a-track-s-plugin-that-became-ready-under-a-repla.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:661-665 (live and Phase::Ready → PlugState::Of(old.ticket))
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:674-676 (Ready && !placed → Effect::DropPlugin { ticket: old.ticket }, pushed BEFORE the LoadPlugin)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:681-688 (the LoadPlugin with PlugState::Of(old.ticket))
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3338-3348 (DropPlugin removes ready[old] and ids[old] and unloads it)
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3649-3652 (PlugState::Of(other) with no ids[other] → StartState::Fresh)

What happens
