When the output opens at another sample rate (set_plug_rate → load_again(true)) while a track's own plugin is loaded but not in the engine, the plugin is loaded again as it starts (StartState::Fresh), not with its state: the patch or settings the song has for it are not in the new instance. The song still holds its state, but the plugin plays its default sound; a save asks the plugin (plugin_tickets) and writes the default state into the file in place of the song's. A track's plugin is "ready and not placed" exactly while the shelf auditions another sound on that track in 「入れかえる」 mode (plugin_loaded: Spot::Seat(seat) if trial_seat == Some(seat) => plug.placed = false).

What should happen

A plugin loaded again for another rate keeps its sound: load_again says "live: with the state each has now", and the test plugins_are_loaded_again_for_another_sample_rate_and_for_a_new_engine (state/added_tests.rs:1388) expects PlugState::Of(ticket), "with the sound it has now". Plugin state in the song is never lost (CLAUDE.md, song file rules; architecture 20-6).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open a song whose track 1 has a plugin instrument with a saved patch (or have meloet start with no output chosen yet, so the plugin loads at 48 kHz).
  2. While that plugin is still loading, open the shelf on track 1 in replace mode and try another row (a trial on its seat). The plugin finishes loading: Ready, placed = false.
  3. Choose an output that runs at 44.1 kHz: AudioOpened → set_plug_rate(44_100) → load_again(true): effects DropPlugin { old }, then LoadPlugin { new, state: Of(old) }. The service handles them in order; the second finds no ids[old] and loads Fresh.
  4. Press Esc: the seat's own instrument comes back (place_waiting) with the default sound. Save: the file now holds the default state.

Also reached with two rate changes during one audition (the first one's reload lands under the trial and is Ready && !placed at the second).

Cause

In load_again, the DropPlugin of an unplaced old instance is pushed before the LoadPlugin that asks for that instance's state by its ticket. Effect::DropPlugin removes the ticket from PluginSide::ids, so load_plugin cannot find the instance to read and falls back to StartState::Fresh.

Suggested fix

Push the DropPlugin { ticket: old.ticket } after the LoadPlugin (the hosts' thread answers in order: it reads the old instance's state for the load, then unloads it). Or, for !old.placed, use PlugState::Bytes(old.plugin.state) (Fresh when empty), since an unplaced instance was never changed. No protocol or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). In load_again (play/plugs.rs:659-690) a plug that is Ready gets PlugState::Of(old.ticket) when live; when it is also !old.placed, Effect::DropPlugin { ticket: old.ticket } is pushed before the Effect::LoadPlugin. The service handles effects in order: DropPlugin (service.rs:3338-3348) removes ready[old] and ids[old], then load_plugin (service.rs:3649-3652) finds no ids[old] for PlugState::Of(old) and loads StartState::Fresh. A seat's own plugin is Ready and not placed only while a replace audition runs on that seat (plugin_loaded, plugs.rs:549). The plugin then has its default state while the song still holds the saved one, and a save asks the plugin. Severity medium stands: it is a rare order of events (an output at another rate during a replace audition whose own plugin became ready under it), but the saved song loses the plugin's state.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs load_again: for Ready && !placed use the song's state instead of the instance's (PlugState::Bytes(old.plugin.state.clone()), Fresh when empty): an instance that was never placed was never played or changed, so both are the same, and the order of the two effects no longer matters. Keep the DropPlugin. Test in state/added_tests.rs: a rate change during a replace audition gives LoadPlugin with Bytes(the song's state).

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs load_again_at (the body of load_again): only a plugin that is in the engine is loaded again with PlugState::Of its ticket; one that became ready under a trial and was never placed gets the song's state (Bytes, Fresh when empty). The DropPlugin stays.

Test: play::plugs::tests::a_tracks_plugin_that_became_ready_under_a_trial_keeps_the_songs_state_at_another_rate.

CORE-STATE-001mediumbugfixedA recording after the first one of a run no longer writes the waiting change to the recovery file first

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-STATE-001-a-recording-after-the-first-one-of-a-run-no-long.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/state/recovery.rs:208-217 (recovery_tick: let started = busy && !std::mem::replace(&mut self.recovery.was_busy, busy);)
crates/meloet-core/src/state/recovery.rs:54-55 (was_busy, never set back to false)

What happens

Only the first recording (or count-in) of a run writes a change that still waits for its 2 seconds into the recovery file at once. From the second recording on, a change made less than 2 s before Record is not written when the recording starts; it waits until the recording has ended. If meloet ends in the middle of that recording (crash, SIGKILL, power cut), the next start brings back the song as it was before that change, and a vocal recording kept from work/rec.json is put on that older song (another tempo map, a track that is not there any more, and so on).

What should happen

The comment in the same function: "A recording starts over a change that still waits: the song is written now. The voice recorded from here on is placed on this song, and a start after meloet ended in the middle must find it, not the song before the change." This holds for every recording, not only the first one of a run. docs/architecture.md 15 (written 2 s after a change; "Not during recording or count-in; after what was recorded is in the song") relies on this write at the start.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Start meloet, set the count-in to 0, record a short take and stop (any recording; now was_busy is true for good).
  2. Change something (for example SetTempo, or add a vocal track) and press Record within 2 s.
  3. The effects of that Record hold no Effect::WriteRecovery (with the same steps as the first recording of a run, they do; see the test nothing_is_written_while_recording_and_the_take_is_written_after).
  4. Kill meloet during the recording: the recovery file still holds the song before step 2.

The existing test a_recording_over_a_song_that_is_written_already_writes_nothing_more does a second recording too, but its change was written already, so it passes with the fault.

Cause

busy && !std::mem::replace(&mut self.recovery.was_busy, busy): && does not run its right side when busy is false, so was_busy is only ever written while busy, and it stays true after the first recording ends. At every later start of a recording replace returns true, so started is false, and the early return in the if busy block skips the write.

Suggested fix

Always update was_busy, for example: let was_busy = std::mem::replace(&mut self.recovery.was_busy, busy); let started = busy && !was_busy; Add a test: record once, then change and record again within 2 s, and expect a WriteRecovery from the second Record. No protocol or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run; the line decides it alone). recovery_tick (state/recovery.rs:209): let started = busy && !std::mem::replace(&mut self.recovery.was_busy, busy);. && does not run its right side when busy is false, so was_busy is written only while busy and is never set back to false after the first recording of a run. At every later start replace returns true, started is false, and the if busy { … return; } block writes nothing. The comment in the block says the write is meant for every recording that starts over a waiting change. Severity medium stands (after an end in the middle of a recording, the song before the last change comes back, and the kept voice is placed on it).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/state/recovery.rs recovery_tick: let was_busy = std::mem::replace(&mut self.recovery.was_busy, busy); let started = busy && !was_busy;. Test in state/recovery/tests.rs next to nothing_is_written_while_recording_and_the_take_is_written_after: record and stop once, then a change and Record inside 2 s gives Effect::WriteRecovery.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/state/recovery.rs recovery_tick: was_busy is now set after every input (let was_busy = std::mem::replace(..); let started = busy && !was_busy;), so every recording or count-in that starts over a waiting change writes it at once, not only the first one of a run.

Test: state::recovery::tests::every_recording_over_a_waiting_change_writes_it_first (state/recovery/tests.rs).

CORE-STATE-002mediumui-uxfixedSave as opens in the current song's own folder, so the new song folder is made inside the old one

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-STATE-002-save-as-opens-in-the-current-song-s-own-folder-s.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3235-3240 (Effect::PickSaveAs uses self.start_folder())
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3798-3806 (start_folder: the parent of the song file, which is the song folder <songs>/<name>/ itself)

What happens

For a song that was saved before, 「名前をつけて保存…」 opens the PC's save window in the song folder <songs>/<name>/ (where <name>.meloet and 音/ are). A person who only types a new name and presses save gets <songs>/<name>/<new name>/<new name>.meloet: the new song folder sits inside the old song's folder, next to its 音/. Deleting or moving the old song folder later takes the new song with it.

What should happen

docs/spec/3-bands.md 89: 「名前をつけて保存」 "makes a folder named 「名前」 in the chosen place". docs/architecture.md 14: a song is a song folder in <music>, one folder per song. The place the window offers first should be the place that holds song folders: the folder that holds the current song folder (or the songs folder), not the inside of a song folder. The first save already goes to <songs>/<name>/ without asking.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Save a new song: it is <songs>/はじめての曲/はじめての曲.meloet.
  2. Menu 「ファイル ▸ 名前をつけて保存…」: ShellRequest::PickSaveAs { folder: Some(<songs>/はじめての曲) }; the shell calls set_current_folder with it (crates/meloet-app/src/pcwindow.rs:124-126).
  3. Type 「夏の曲」 and press save: split_save_as gives that folder, and save_new_with makes <songs>/はじめての曲/夏の曲/夏の曲.meloet.

Cause

start_folder was written for 「ひらく…」 and the folder window of 「保存できない」 (it is the folder of the song file in use), and Effect::PickSaveAs reuses it. For a save that makes a new song folder in the chosen place, the folder of the song file is one level too deep.

Suggested fix

For Effect::PickSaveAs start in the parent of the song folder (song_path().parent().parent()), else the songs folder; keep start_folder as it is for the open window. The folder window of 「保存できない」 (Effect::PickFolder) also makes a new song folder in the chosen place, so the same start fits it; the lead may decide that one. No protocol, wording or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Effect::PickSaveAs (service.rs:3235-3240) starts the save window in start_folder(), the parent of the song file, which for a saved song is its own song folder <songs>/<name>/. split_save_as (state/songs.rs:565-571) takes the parent of the path the window gives as the place and makes the new song folder there, so a new name typed without changing the folder lands in <songs>/<name>/<new name>/. Architecture 18 ("Save as") and 14 (one folder per song in the songs folder) and spec 3 ("makes a folder named 「名前」 in the chosen place") do not say where the window starts; nothing says the inside of a song folder is meant. Severity medium stands (the plain use of the command gives a song inside another song's folder).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs: a second helper for windows that make a song folder in the chosen place: the folder that holds the current song folder (song_path().parent().parent()), else the songs folder, each only when it is a directory (see CORE-STATE-005 for the order of the filter). Effect::PickSaveAs uses it; Effect::PickFolder (「保存できない」 → 「場所をえらぶ」, which also makes a new song folder in the chosen place) uses it too. 「ひらく…」 keeps start_folder. A small unit test of the helper with a temp folder.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs: a second helper Worker::place_folder (the folder that holds the song's folder, else the songs folder, each only when it is a directory; the shared filter is first_folder). Effect::PickSaveAs and Effect::PickFolder (「保存できない」 → 「場所をえらぶ」) use it; 「ひらく…」 keeps start_folder. docs/architecture.md 18 says where the windows start.

Test: service::shell_tests::the_pcs_windows_start_among_the_song_folders_or_in_the_songs_folder (service/shell_tests.rs, a new test file of the core with a shell that records what it is asked).

EDGE-001mediumbugfixedThe shell's own zoom subscription counts as the screen's greeting, so the plugin scan and the held song start before the page loads

docs/bugs/reports/EDGE-001-the-shell-s-own-zoom-subscription-counts-as-the.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • crates/meloet-app/src/main.rs:251-276 (follow_scale calls core.subscribe(...))
  • crates/meloet-app/src/main.rs:557-560 (follow_scale runs in the Tauri setup hook)
  • crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:888-891 (Core::subscribe always sends Msg::ScreenReady)
  • crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:2844-2849 (ScreenReady releases the held scan jobs and gives Input::ScreenUp)

What happens

At start, the shell subscribes to the core for itself (to follow the zoom for the smallest window size). Core::subscribe treats every subscription as "the screen's page is up": it sends Msg::ScreenReady. So ScreenReady reaches the core thread in Tauri's setup hook, before the WebView has even started to load the page, and long before the page's own greeting (hello -> subscribe).

What waits for the screen therefore does not wait at all:

  • the plugin scan jobs held by PluginSide::held are given to the scanner at once (hundreds of child processes start while WebKit loads its libraries);
  • Input::ScreenUp runs songs_screen_up (a song handed over by the PC, meloet song.meloet, is opened at once), pack_screen_up (the catalog check and a resumed fetch) and updates_screen_up.

The 10 s fallback (SCAN_WAITS_FOR_SCREEN) never matters, because the release always comes at once.

What should happen

The scan, the given song and the other "screen up" work start after the page's greeting. docs/architecture.md:850: "The scan starts after the screen's hello (subscribe), or after 10 s ... Why: under VS Code's CodeLLDB, concurrent spawns left meloet stopped with no window, and spawns overlapping WebKit's library loading showed as pauses; the window also comes up sooner." CLAUDE.md ("Things about this PC"): "Fixed by starting children one at a time and starting the scan after the screen's greeting (2026-11-19). If either is removed, it happens again." docs/architecture.md:2614: "A path that comes before the screen's greeting is kept until Input::ScreenUp." Commit aa16851 says the same; follow_scale (commit e9cb85d, 2026-11-15) was older and was not changed with it.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Start the real app (any start; with plugins on the PC and no saved scan, or meloet some.meloet). In Tauri 2.12.5 setup runs on RuntimeRunEvent::Ready right after the config windows are built (tauri-2.12.5/src/app.rs:1441-1445, 2688-2701), before the main loop has loaded any page. setup -> follow_scale -> core.subscribe -> Msg::ScreenReady -> plugins.release() and Input::ScreenUp. A log line or a breakpoint in PluginSide::release would show it fires before the page's hello command.

Cause

Core::subscribe (service.rs:888) does two things: it adds the sinks and it announces the screen. The shell's internal subscription in follow_scale only wants the patches, but it gets the announcement too. It is the first subscribe of the run, so the ScreenReady it sends is the one that counts (release and the screen_up flags act once).

Suggested fix

Smallest change: let the shell subscribe without announcing the screen, for example a separate core method (Core::listen(messages) that only calls hub.subscribe, or subscribe with a flag) used by follow_scale, while the subscribe command of the page keeps sending ScreenReady. Alternatively send ScreenReady from the hello command instead of from subscribe. No protocol, song-file or wording change. Needs a core change (crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs), which is outside this area.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (the real app was not started). Tauri 2.12.5 (~/.cargo/registry/src/*/tauri-2.12.5/src/app.rs, the version in Cargo.lock): the builder's setup closure is called from fn setup (app.rs:2688-2701), which first builds the windows of the config and then calls the closure, on RuntimeRunEvent::Ready (app.rs:1442-1443), on the main thread, before the event loop has run any page load. In meloet's closure (main.rs:557-560) follow_scale calls core.subscribe(..) (main.rs:254), and Core::subscribe (service.rs:888-891) always sends Msg::ScreenReady; the core thread then calls plugins.release() and handle(Input::ScreenUp) (service.rs:2844-2849). The page's own subscribe command (main.rs:105) can only come after the page loaded. So the shell's subscription is always the first, and the scan, the song handed over by the PC, the pack check and the update check never wait for the page; SCAN_WAITS_FOR_SCREEN never matters. Architecture 20-10 and the CodeLLDB note in CLAUDE.md say the scan starts after the screen's greeting. Children are still started one at a time (launch::spawn), so I do not claim the CodeLLDB hang is back; the pauses while WebKit loads and the later window are what 20-10 names. Severity medium stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs: a second method (for example Core::listen(messages)) that only calls self.hub.subscribe(messages, <a telemetry sink that says false>) and sends nothing; crates/meloet-app/src/main.rs follow_scale uses it. The page's subscribe command keeps Core::subscribe. Test in service/tests.rs or plug_tests.rs: with wait_for_screen, listen does not release the held scan and subscribe does. After the fix, check by hand once that a start under VS Code's 「meloet（デバッグ）」 still shows the window.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs: a new Core::listen(messages) that only adds the sink (hub.subscribe with a telemetry sink that says no) and sends no Msg::ScreenReady. crates/meloet-app/src/core_handle.rs (CoreHandle::listen) and crates/meloet-app/src/main.rs follow_scale use it; the page's subscribe command keeps Core::subscribe. docs/architecture.md 20-10 says the shell's own listening does not count.

Test: service::shell_tests::the_shells_own_listening_is_not_the_screens_greeting (service/shell_tests.rs: with wait_for_screen and a song handed over, listen leaves the scan held and the song not opened; subscribe starts both). Not checked by hand under VS Code's 「meloet（デバッグ）」 (the fixer did not start the app).

ENGINE-001mediumbugfixedThe master inserts' latency is not added to the heard position: play line, recorded keys and metronome are off by it

docs/bugs/reports/ENGINE-001-the-master-inserts-latency-is-not-added-to-the-h.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:2729-2731 (dac_us: playback_us + frame / rate, nothing for the master's inserts)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:2739-2758 (segment_now: every clock stretch starts at dac_us)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:1808-1811 (the look-ahead covers the tracks' instrument and inserts only)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:1643-1648 (master_latency is read only by the export: crates/meloet-core/src/export/job.rs:501, 820)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:3084-3110 (the metronome's clicks join after the master's inserts, so they are not delayed with the song)

What happens

When an insert of the master delays the sound (a plugin that reports latency, such as a limiter with look-ahead or a linear-phase EQ), everything that comes through the master reaches the ear that many frames (L) later than the engine's clock says. Nothing makes up for it while playing:

  • The clock stretches say the song position at frame 0 of a stretch reaches the DAC at playback_us, but what the person hears there is the song L frames earlier. The play line on the screen (telemetry heard_now) runs ahead of the sound by L.
  • A key the person plays in time with the song is stamped with the position the clock gives for its time (stamp, stamp_recent, the stop position in stop), which is L later than what they heard: recorded and kept notes come out late by L. The vocal recording uses the same clock (frame_at, time_of_frame), so a voice sung to the song is placed late by L too.
  • The metronome is added after the master (it skips the master's strip), so its clicks sound L before the beats of the song they belong to.

With a 5 ms look-ahead limiter that is 240 frames at 48 kHz; a linear-phase EQ can be tens of ms.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 20-8: "Master insert latency is the same for everything: not compensated, but added to the position at the ear (chapter 11)." (The Japanese original, docs/history/architecture.ja.md:841, says the same: マスターのインサートの遅れは … 「耳に届いている位置」に足す.) The export already treats it (it cuts master_latency() frames at the head); the live clock does not.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put a plugin effect that reports latency on the master (any plugin whose getLatencySamples / lv2:latency is not 0, such as a look-ahead limiter; which of the plugins on this PC do was not checked).
  2. Turn the metronome on and play: the click comes before the song's beat by the plugin's latency.
  3. Record keys in time with the song: the notes land late by the same amount; the play line is ahead of what is heard.

Cause

The heard-position clock is built only from cpal's playback time and the frame count (dac_us). Strip::latency() of the master strip is summed only for Engine::master_latency(), which only the export reads. The tracks' latencies are made up for by reading their notes early (lookahead), but the master's latency is the same for every track and is not added anywhere on the live path.

Suggested fix

Needs a decision by the lead on where L goes, because the clock is also the frame line of the latency measurement (time_of_frame for the probe click, which is added after the master and is not delayed):

  • Add the master's latency (as of the block) to the time each stretch's song position reaches the ear, for example a separate per-segment offset master_us = master_latency / rate that heard, stamp and segment_at add, while frame_at / time_of_frame (the engine-frame line used by the probe and by vocalrec) keep the plain DAC time; and
  • delay the metronome clicks by the same L (start a click L frames after its beat frame), so the click and the song meet at the ear again.

No protocol or song-file change. A test like tests/lookahead.rs with a fake master effect of 100 frames latency can check that a key played at the time the beat is heard is stamped on the beat.

Verification

Real, checked by reading (no test run; it needs a plugin on the master that reports latency, and I did not start plugins). Architecture 20-8 says the master inserts' latency is "not compensated, but added to the position at the ear (chapter 11)". In the code Strip::latency() of the master is read only by Engine::master_latency() (engine.rs:1646), and that is called only by the export (export/job.rs). dac_us / segment_now build every clock stretch from playback_us alone, and the metronome's click_sum is added to the output after the master's inserts (engine.rs:3091-3104). Only plugin effects report a latency (Effect::latency is 0 for every built-in effect, effect.rs:46), so this shows only with a plugin on the master that has latency; with meloet's own limiter nothing is off. Chapter 11 itself has no word on it, and docs/progress.md does not list it as open.

Corrections: none to the lines. On the clock and the latency measurement: the probe click is added after the master (self.probe.add, engine.rs:3087) and the measurement reads TransportClock::time_of_frame (probe.rs:199); the vocal recorder reads frame_at / kept_frame_at / time_of_frame (vocalrec.rs:912, 1030, service/input.rs:404, 534). So the frame line must stay the plain DAC time, or the measured latency would grow by the master's latency.

Why needs-lead: it is a real-time change of the clock (a new field in Segment, which is packed into fixed words), of the metronome and of the recorded-position rules, and it needs a measurement with a real latent plugin. Choices:

  1. Do what 20-8 says. Place: a per-stretch ear_us (master_strip.latency() / rate, read once a block) in Segment (clock.rs), added only where a time is turned into a song position (Segment::at, so heard_at, heard_now, and the engine's own stamp / stamp_recent / stop position), not in frame_at / time_of_frame. The core's vocal_mark (service/input.rs:395-412), which turns a song frame into a time through time_of_frame, adds the same amount. The metronome click starts master_latency frames after its beat frame. Cost: engine (audio thread, clock word layout), core vocal marks, tests like tests/lookahead.rs, and a measurement (record-timing.sh) with a latent plugin on the master. This is the one I recommend, as its own job after the sweep (meloet-very-hard), not as a quick fix.
  2. Only delay the metronome by the master's latency. Cheap, fixes the click against the song, leaves the play line and recorded keys late by L.
  3. Change 20-8 to say it is not added, and list it in docs/progress.md as a known limit. No code.

Severity medium stands: recorded positions are wrong by L, but only with a latent plugin on the master, and taking it off while recording is the way around.

Fix

Choice 1 (the lead's decision). No protocol, song-file or wire change.

  • crates/meloet-engine/src/clock.rs: Segment keeps ear, the master inserts' latency in frames of the block it starts in (in the spare half of the word that holds map_gen; the ring still has 11 words). TransportClock::segment_at takes the newest stretch whose song reaches the ear by then (start_us + ear ≤ t) and notes when the next one starts; Segment::at reads the line at t − ear, and stands at the end of the stretch (speed 0) while a later stretch with a larger delay is not heard yet. When the delay is longer than the ring reaches back, the oldest stretch kept is counted back from (else the keys would be lost). New TransportClock::heard_time_of_frame = time_of_frame(frame + ear). frame_at, kept_frame_at, time_of_frame are unchanged.
  • crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs: ear = master_strip.latency() once a block (after the commands), written into each stretch; both places that start a metronome click give it that delay; a change of the delay moves the waiting clicks by the difference; turning the metronome (or recording_only) quiet drops them.
  • crates/meloet-engine/src/transport.rs: Click::start_after (delay 0 starts at once, as before), up to 16 waiting clicks in a fixed array, delay_waiting, drop_waiting. No memory taken on the audio thread.
  • crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs: the vocal mark asks the clock for heard_time_of_frame (its body moved into Worker::mark so a test can reach it; the half-beat lines are as they were).
  • docs/architecture.md: 12, 20-8, 21-3 (metronome), 22-26 (marks).

With no latent plugin on the master (ear = 0) every number is as before: no existing test was changed, and all pass.

Tests: in crates/meloet-engine/tests/lookahead.rs, with a fake master plugin Lag of 0, 100, 1000 and 30 000 frames: the_position_heard_is_behind_the_dac_by_the_masters_delay (also round a loop), keys_played_with_the_beat_that_comes_out_land_on_it (recorded keys with the count-in rule, the stop, kept keys), the_metronome_clicks_with_the_song_behind_the_masters_delay (playing and counting in, also a delay longer than a beat), the_latency_measurement_does_not_hear_the_masters_delay, the_song_of_a_frame_is_heard_when_it_comes_out_of_the_master, a_change_of_the_masters_delay_never_moves_the_position_heard_back; with 140 000 frames, longer than the ring, keys_land_on_the_beat_also_behind_a_delay_longer_than_the_clock_keeps; in core, a_voice_is_marked_where_the_song_came_out_of_the_master (crates/meloet-core/src/service/input/tests.rs, the real disk writer on the engine's clock). Five of the engine tests and the core test were seen failing on the old code; the long-delay test was seen failing without the oldest-stretch fallback.

Checked by tests only. A measurement in the real app with a latent plugin on the master (record-timing.sh, the click against the song) is still to be done.

MODEL-A-003mediumbugfixedOverdub into an empty place drops the pedal, bend and modulation played before the first note's gap

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-A-003-overdub-into-an-empty-place-drops-the-pedal-bend.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/record/overdub.rs:58-65 (no clip there: the take goes through in_the_gap)
crates/meloet-model/src/record/overdub.rs:252-287 (in_the_gap: cut = low - take.origin, then .filter(|p| p.tick >= cut && …) on the controls)

What happens

With 「重ねる」 on, when no clip of the track holds a recorded note's start, overdub_recording cuts the take to the empty place around the notes: from low, the end of the last clip that ends at or before the first note (or the take's origin), to the next clip. Every control point before cut is thrown away. That includes the points the recorder writes at tick 0 of a take for a pedal, bend or modulation still held when recording (or a loop pass) started (HeldControls, Lap::head), and a pedal pressed after the start but before low. The new clip then has no pedal down (or no bend, no modulation) under its first notes, although the person held it and heard it while playing. Played back, the notes sound dry until the next pedal point. The points are gone from the song, not only trimmed off.

What should happen

A recording keeps what was played. Where a clip does not hold the start of the controls, the model's own rule is to carry the last pedal, bend and modulation before the clip's start to the start: track_events does this for a trimmed head ("The last pedal, bend and modulation before the start, when not at rest, are put at the start", crates/meloet-model/src/playback.rs:30-34, architecture 13 "How clips play"), and the recorder writes a held pedal at the head of each pass (architecture 13 "Recording in a loop"). Without 「重ねる」, add_recording keeps every control point of the take.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A keys track has clip A in bar 1 (any notes). Turn 「重ねる」 on.
  2. Put the play line at bar 1, hold the sustain pedal down, press R.
  3. Play notes only from bar 2 on (after A's end), keep the pedal down, stop.
  4. The new clip starts at A's end; its take has no CC 64 = 127 point, so its notes play without the pedal. The same happens in a loop recording over A and empty bars, where each pass starts with the held pedal at its head.

Cause

in_the_gap moves the take's tick 0 to low by cut ticks and keeps only the control points in [cut, cut + length). It does not look at the values that are in force at cut, so the head points (tick 0) and any pedal, bend or modulation change before cut vanish.

Suggested fix

In in_the_gap, before filtering, find the last sustain (CC 64), modulation (CC 1) and pitch bend value at or before cut among the take's controls, and when one is not at rest, put it at tick 0 of the cut take (unless a point of the same controller is already at cut). This is the rule of track_events and of the recorder's pass heads. No protocol or file-shape change. A test next to with_no_clip_there_a_new_clip_fills_only_the_empty_place with controls = [pedal(0, 127)].

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-model --lib verify_model_a_003): a clip in bars 1-2, then overdub_recording of a take from tick 0 with CC 64 = 127 at tick 0 and one note in bar 3. The new clip (bars 3-4) has no control point, and the old clip gets none either: the pedal is gone from the song. Severity medium stands (pedal held from the start of a recording is the normal way to play; the way around is to write the point by hand).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: in crates/meloet-model/src/record/overdub.rs in_the_gap, before the filter, take the last CC 64, CC 1 and pitch bend value at a tick < cut (the take's controls are sorted by tick); for each that is not at rest and has no point of the same controller at exactly cut, put it at tick 0 of the cut take. Reuse the rule playback.rs track_events already has for a trimmed head rather than writing a second one, if it can be shared. Test next to with_no_clip_there_a_new_clip_fills_only_the_empty_place in record/overdub/tests.rs.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/record/overdub.rs in_the_gap: the points before the cut go through the recorder's own head rule (record.rs set_head), and the pedal, bend and modulation not at rest go to tick 0 of the cut take, except a controller that has a point right at the cut. docs/architecture.md 24-7 says so.

Test: record::overdub::tests::with_no_clip_there_the_new_clip_starts_with_what_was_held (meloet-model).

MODEL-B-001mediumbugfixedRemoving a missing plugin instrument keeps its lanes of parameters 0-3, which then move the sine's knobs

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-B-001-removing-a-missing-plugin-instrument-keeps-its-l.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/mixer.rs:752-758 (remove_missing: track.instrument = InstrumentRef::SINE, then drop_orphan_lanes)
crates/meloet-model/src/automation.rs:163-167 (Track::has_target for AutoTarget::Instrument on a built-in sound)
crates/meloet-model/src/knobs.rs:84-91 (knob_usable: the sine uses all four knobs)

What happens

「見つからない物を曲から外す」 on a plugin that is a track's instrument gives the track the sine wave. Lanes of that plugin's parameters whose plugin id is 0, 1, 2 or 3 stay in the song. They are now read as lanes of the sine's knobs (SoundKnob::ALL[param]: 「明るさ」, 「立ち上がり」, 「のび」, 「ゆれ」), so the plugin's old points move the sine's brightness, attack, release or vibrato while the song plays. Lanes of the same plugin with ids 4 and up are dropped, so the result also depends on the number the plugin happened to give.

What should happen

All lanes of the old instrument's parameters go in the same step, and the knobs start again, as for every other way the instrument is replaced: docs/architecture.md 21-5 ("Removing or replacing an effect, or replacing the instrument, removes that parameter's lanes in the same undo step ... RemoveMissing in model do this") and 25-27 ("Replacing the sound puts the knobs back and drops their lanes in the same step"). The sibling edit::with_instrument (crates/meloet-model/src/edit.rs:783-798), used by set_instrument and remove_instrument (「外す」 of a stopped plugin), drops every AutoTarget::Instrument lane unless the new instrument is the same plugin, and resets knobs.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A keys track whose instrument is a plugin, with a lane of one of its parameters whose id is 0..=3 (LV2 control ports use the port index as the id, crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs:691, so small ids are common), with some points.
  2. Open the song on a PC without that plugin; the track plays the sine and send_automation leaves the instrument lanes out (crates/meloet-core/src/play/autosync.rs:136-143).
  3. Choose 「曲から外す」 for the plugin (MissingRef::Plugin, crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:2419 → Play::remove_missing → mixer::remove_missing).
  4. The track's instrument is now InstrumentRef::SINE, a built-in sound, so autosync sends the kept lane to the seat and the sine's knob follows the plugin's points. The lane also shows under a knob name.

Cause

remove_missing sets the instrument field directly and relies on drop_orphan_lanes, which keeps every lane for which has_target is true. For a built-in instrument has_target(Instrument { param }) is SoundKnob::from_index(param).is_some_and(knob_usable), and knob_usable(SINE, _) is true for all four knobs, so plugin lanes with param 0..=3 count as having a target. track.knobs is also not reset here, unlike with_instrument.

Suggested fix

In remove_missing, when uses(&track.instrument), replace the instrument the way edit::with_instrument does (drop every AutoTarget::Instrument lane and reset knobs), for example by making with_instrument pub(crate) and calling it before drop_orphan_lanes. No protocol or song-file change. A test like lanes_go_with_what_they_move with an instrument plugin lane of param 2 would watch it.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). mixer::remove_missing sets track.instrument = InstrumentRef::SINE by hand and then calls drop_orphan_lanes; Track::has_target(Instrument { param }) on a built-in sound is SoundKnob::from_index(param) + knob_usable, and knob_usable(SINE, _) is true for all four knobs, so lanes of the plugin's parameters 0 to 3 stay and are read as knob lanes. edit::with_instrument (the path of set_instrument and remove_instrument) drops every AutoTarget::Instrument lane and resets knobs; this path does neither. Architecture 21-5 names RemoveMissing among the steps that remove the lanes. Severity medium stands (wrong sound after a normal action on a song opened without its plugin; LV2 parameter ids are port indexes, so 0 to 3 are common).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-model/src/edit.rs: make with_instrument pub(crate); crates/meloet-model/src/mixer.rs remove_missing: when uses(&track.instrument), build the track with with_instrument(&track, InstrumentRef::SINE) before emptying the inserts and drop_orphan_lanes. Test next to lanes_go_with_what_they_move in the mixer tests: a missing plugin instrument with lanes of param 2 and param 9; after remove_missing neither is there and knobs is the default.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/mixer.rs remove_missing: a track that sounds with the missing instrument gets the sine wave through edit::with_instrument (made pub(crate) in crates/meloet-model/src/edit.rs), so every lane of the old instrument's parameters goes and the knobs start again, as set_instrument and remove_instrument do. Test: automation::tests::a_missing_instrument_takes_its_lanes_and_the_knobs_along (lanes of param 2 and 9, knobs set by hand; after the step only the volume lane is left and the knobs are the default).

MODEL-B-002mediumbugfixedTurning keys into audio mutes the keys track by hand only; a moving mute lane keeps it playing over its own audio

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-B-002-turning-keys-into-audio-mutes-the-keys-track-by.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/audio/bounce.rs:137-141 (bounce_track: only muted: true is set on the keys track)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/mix.rs:50-56 (silent_tracks: a mute lane that moves takes the place of the hand's mute)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/bounce.rs:120-123 (bounce_doc keeps the mute lane in the render)

What happens

When the keys track has a mute lane that moves (not resting, with points), 「音のクリップにする」 / 「このトラックを音にする」 sets the track's hand mute, but the engine ignores the hand mute of a track whose mute lane moves. Wherever the lane says "not muted", the keys track keeps playing. The render kept the same mute lane, so the new vocal track has sound in exactly those places: the part is heard twice (about +6 dB, the same notes on top of each other). The song keeps muted: true on a track that is heard.

What should happen

Spec chapter 3 (docs/spec/3-bands.md:455): "The keys track is muted (all of it)." The siblings that set a mute on a track whose mute lane moves rest the lane in the same step so the mute is heard: Play::toggle_mute (crates/meloet-core/src/play/mix.rs:21-26, docs/architecture.md 21-11) and recall_mute_set (crates/meloet-model/src/mute_sets.rs:192-202, "the lane rests in the same step, as when M is pressed on such a track, so that the set is heard").

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A keys track with notes in bars 1-8 and a mute lane with points 0.0 at bar 1 and 1.0 at bar 5 (plays bars 1-4, muted from bar 5).
  2. Right-click the track head, 「このトラックを音にする」.
  3. The render (bounce_doc) keeps the mute lane, so the audio has bars 1-4 and silence after. bounce_track sets muted: true on the keys track but leaves the lane moving.
  4. Play: in bars 1-4 both the keys track (its lane says not muted, silent_tracks skips the hand mute because laned is true) and the new vocal track sound.

Cause

bounce_track builds the keys track as Track { muted: true, ..source } and does not touch its AutoTarget::Mute lane. silent_tracks treats a track as silent by its hand mute only when !laned (no mute lane that moves), so the hand mute set by the step has no effect while the lane moves.

Suggested fix

In bounce_track, when the keys track's mute lane moves, also put it to rest in the same step (automation::set_resting(&mut new, from, AutoTarget::Mute, true) after the track is replaced), as recall_mute_set does. Undo brings both back. No protocol or song-file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). bounce_track (crates/meloet-model/src/audio/bounce.rs:137-141) sets only muted: true; Play::silent_tracks (play/mix.rs:50-56) counts the hand mute only when the track has no mute lane that moves; bounce_doc (play/bounce.rs:104-123) strips only the fader, pan and send lanes, so the render follows the mute lane too. Result as written: where the lane says "not muted", the keys track and its audio both sound. Spec 3 A7: "The keys track is muted (all of it)." Severity medium stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-model/src/audio/bounce.rs bounce_track: when the keys track has an AutoTarget::Mute lane with moves(), set it to rest in the same step (the way mute_sets.rs:192-202 does). Test in the bounce tests of the model (the lane rests after the step; undo brings it back moving) and one in crates/meloet-core/src/play/bounce/tests.rs that silent_tracks holds the keys track's seat after the step.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/audio/bounce.rs bounce_track: when the keys track's mute lane moves, it is put to rest in the same step (automation::set_resting, as recall_mute_set does); the doc comment says so. docs/architecture.md 25-31 says it too. Tests: audio::bounce::tests::a_mute_lane_that_moves_rests_in_the_same_step_so_the_mute_is_heard (model: the lane rests after the step, undo brings it back moving) and play::bounce::tests::the_keys_track_is_silent_after_the_step_under_its_mute_lane (core: after bounce_made the engine is told the keys track's seat is silent).

PLUG-001mediumbugfixedPlugin host: touch() leaves a seat without ringing the bell, so a block asked meanwhile can make meloet stop the whole host

docs/bugs/reports/PLUG-001-plugin-host-touch-leaves-a-seat-without-ringing.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs:394-399 (Handle::touch: takes the seat, no self.bell.ring() after)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs:561-575 (run: a seat another thread holds is passed over, then the thread sleeps on the bell for up to IDLE_NS, line 34)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/control.rs:241 (take_hold calls audio.touch(slot, id, true))
crates/meloet-plughost/src/control.rs:251 (let_go calls audio.touch(slot, id, false))
crates/meloet-plugin/src/ipc/ends.rs:228-243 (await_overdue: nobody took the request and progress did not move, so the slot and the whole mapping are stopped)

What happens

When the plugin host's main thread is inside Handle::touch (it holds the slot's seat for a moment) at the moment the engine asks that slot for a block, the audio thread that wakes for the block finds the seat busy, passes it over, finds nothing else to do and goes back to sleep on the bell. touch then leaves the seat without ringing the bell, so nothing wakes the audio thread again until its 100 ms time limit (IDLE_NS) or until some other slot is asked.

In a host with one plugin (the usual case: one instance of a plugin per process) nothing else rings. The engine's deadline passes (silent or dry block), and at the next block's deadline await_overdue sees: request not taken, progress unchanged, no thread inside a plugin. It marks the slot and the shared header as stopped. meloet then kills the process and shows the plugin as stopped (「（名前）が止まりました…」); every plugin of that host is silent until 「もう一度読みこむ」.

It is timing-dependent and rare per event, but it can happen any time the person takes hold of or lets go of a parameter in a plugin's window while the engine runs blocks.

What should happen

The main thread rings the bell every time it leaves a seat, so a block asked while it was there is not passed over. This is the rule the audio thread relies on: the comment in run (rt.rs:562-563) says "the main thread rings the bell when it leaves one", and every other seat user does it: install (rt.rs:347), remove (354), set_param (366), params_taken (375), outputs (415), forget_params (425). A busy or late host must not be taken for a dead one (docs/architecture.md 20-2, 23-14).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Load one VST3 or LV2 plugin with a window (one plugin in its host process) and let the engine run blocks (playing, or the output just open).
  2. Open its window and grab a knob without moving it (VST3 beginEdit / LV2 ui:touch grabbed: Said::ParamBegin alone, no set_param that would ring), or release a knob after the last move (Said::ParamEnd), or let an LV2 parameter that has no touch go quiet for 0.5 s (LET_GO).
  3. If the engine submits that slot's block while touch holds the seat, and the woken audio thread looks at the seat in that moment, the host is stopped two blocks later.

Cause

Handle::touch is the only main-thread method that takes a seat and does not call self.bell.ring() afterwards. The audio thread reads the bell count before it walks the seats (let seen = bell.count()), so a ring made by the engine before that walk does not wake it again; only a later ring does. With no ring, the thread sleeps for IDLE_NS (100 ms), longer than two engine blocks, and the engine's watch in await_overdue cannot tell this from a frozen process.

Suggested fix

Ring the bell at the end of Handle::touch, as the sibling methods do (after the Taken guard is dropped):

pub fn touch(&mut self, slot: u32, id: u32, on: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(run) = self.seat(slot)?.run() {
        run.touch(id, on);
    }
    // A block asked for while the main thread was at the seat was passed over.
    self.bell.ring();
    Ok(())
}

No protocol or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run; it is a race between two threads of the host and the engine, and I found no small test that forces the order). Handle::touch (rt.rs:394-399) is the only main-thread method that takes a seat and does not ring the bell after; install, remove, set_param, params_taken, outputs, forget_params all do, with the same comment. The path to a stopped host holds: the engine's submit rings once (ends.rs:292-296); the audio thread wakes, reads seen, finds the seat busy and sleeps up to IDLE_NS (100 ms); the engine does not ring again while the block is pending (submit returns at once); the first wait stores seen_progress; at the next block await_overdue sees taken != seq, progress unchanged (only take_request and finish raise it) and no thread inside a plugin, and marks the mapping stopped.

Correction: the window is narrower than the report's steps suggest. The main thread holds the seat in touch only for a push into a small Vec, so the audio thread must look at the seat in those few instructions. It is rare per touch; but a host with one plugin has no other ring to save it, and the result is a stopped plugin, so severity medium stands (I would not argue with low by frequency).

Fix plan: crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs Handle::touch: self.bell.ring(); after the seat guard is dropped, with the same comment as the siblings. No test can watch the race itself; a unit test that touch raises the bell count is enough.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs Handle::touch: rings the bell after it leaves the seat, as every other seat user does.
  • Test: rt::tests::taking_hold_of_a_parameter_rings_the_bell_after (meloet-plughost): the bell is read as an audio thread reads it before it looks at the seats; after touch (take hold, let go) it must have moved, so a thread that found the seat taken does not sleep until IDLE_NS. The race itself has no test: the main thread's moment inside touch cannot be held from a test without new machinery.
PLUG-004mediumbugfixedLV2: a block that goes round the plugin while the main thread holds the gate loses its notes, so a note-off can be lost and a note keeps sounding

docs/bugs/reports/PLUG-004-lv2-a-block-that-goes-round-the-plugin-while-the.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs:1728-1736 (Lv2Processor::process: when gate.enter() fails the block is passed by and block.events are never looked at)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs:178 (self.events.clear() after process: what the block did not use is gone)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs:2264-2289 (save_state holds the gate through state.to_string and the copy, longer than the plugin needs it)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs:2248-2262 (set_param takes the gate for every SetParam of a control port)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs:970-980 (the same pass-by for VST3 while buses are switched or the plugin is switched off and on)

What happens

While the host's main thread holds an LV2 plugin's gate (taking its state, putting one back, setting a control port from meloet, saving a preset), a block that the engine asks of that plugin "goes round" it: an instrument gives silence for that block, an effect gives the dry input. The block's events, the MIDI note-ons and note-offs and the automation points, are dropped with it: process returns before it reads block.events, and SlotRun::serve clears them after.

A dropped note-off leaves the note sounding in the plugin (the engine believes it is off, so stopping the song does not end it either); a dropped note-on is a note that is not heard. With a pad or organ sound the note hangs until the same key is played again.

The gate is taken in normal use while the song plays: every song save with plugins asks each plugin's state (docs/architecture.md 20-11), every visit to a plugin's window asks it on entering and on leaving (20-13, 15-C), a panel step asks it after commit: true, and every SetPluginParam of a control port takes it. save_state holds it also while it writes the state as Turtle text, which does not touch the plugin.

What should happen

A block that cannot run the plugin may lose its sound (that is the gate's purpose, lv2.rs:9-11, and architecture 24-16 notes the gap), but the notes of that block must still reach the plugin, or at least no note-off may be lost: a key let go must end its note.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put Surge XT (LV2) or another LV2 synth with a held sound on a track with notes, and play the song.
  2. While it plays, save the song (Ctrl+S), or click into and out of the plugin's window, or drag one of its parameters in the params panel.
  3. When a block whose note-off falls in the time the gate is held is passed by, that note keeps sounding after its end.

Cause

Lv2Processor::process treats a busy gate like "no plugin this block" and returns at once, without keeping block.events. Nothing carries them to the next block. save_state keeps the claim to the end of the function, so the gate is held for the Turtle writing too, which makes the window wider.

Suggested fix

Two small changes in formats/lv2.rs:

  1. In process, when the gate is busy, keep the block's MIDI events for the next block instead of dropping them (a fixed-size buffer in Lv2Processor, made at load, pushed into the MIDI port at frame 0 of the next block that runs; when it is full keep at least the note-offs). The same can be done in Vst3Processor::process for its rarer pass-by.
  2. In save_state, let the claim go right after instance.state() (as save_preset does at lv2.rs:2364-2369); to_string and the copy need no gate.

No protocol or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run; I did not start plugins). Lv2Processor::process (lv2.rs:1723-1736) returns at once when gate.enter() fails, before it reads block.events, and SlotRun::serve (rt.rs:171-178) clears self.events after process whatever happened. So the note-ons, note-offs and parameter points of a passed-by block are gone. The gate is claimed on the main thread in save_state (through state.to_string and the copy), load_state, set_param of a control port and save_preset; all happen while a song can play. Vst3Processor::process (vst3.rs:970-980) has the same early return. Architecture only says the block "goes without the plugin" (the sound); nothing says its events may be dropped. Severity medium stands (a hanging note after a save while playing).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan (one place for both formats, no new buffer): crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/mod.rs Processor::process returns whether the plugin ran (bool); lv2.rs and vst3.rs return false on the gate's early return; crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs SlotRun::serve clears self.events only after a block that ran, so the kept events go with the next block (they are pushed while len < capacity, as now; a kept event's frame is brought into the next block's length at frame 0). Also in lv2.rs save_state: drop the claim right after instance.state() (as save_preset does), since to_string and the copy do not touch the instance. Test in rt.rs tests with the fake processor: a block whose gate is held, then one that runs, gets the first block's note-off.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/mod.rs: Processor::process returns whether the plugin ran.
  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs, formats/vst3.rs: false on the gate's early return (LV2 also for a block longer than it was made for), true at the end. VST3 had the same hole and is fixed the same way. formats/fake.rs: true.
  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/rt.rs SlotRun::serve: after a block the plugin did not run, its events stay in the slot's event buffer (made at load: the ring's size + 256), their frames set to 0, and go with the next block before its own. When the buffer is full, an event that ends a sound (note-off, note-on of velocity 0, CC 120 / 123, pedal CC 64 / 66 below 64) takes the place of the first event that does not. A block with RESET drops the kept MIDI that ends no sound (a key from before is not started after the reset). Handle::forget_params drops only the parameter changes; kept MIDI stays.
  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/lv2.rs save_state: the gate is held for instance.state() and the kept values only; the Turtle text is written after it is let go (as save_preset).
  • Test: rt::tests::the_events_of_a_block_the_plugin_did_not_run_go_with_the_next (meloet-plughost; also counts allocations of the audio thread's code: 0).
UI-ARRANGE-002mediumbugfixedA loop range that is not on bar lines is drawn at the nearest bar lines, and its numbers differ from the status line

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-002-a-loop-range-that-is-not-on-bar-lines-is-drawn-a.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/arrangeGeom.ts:16 (lineNear: the nearest bar line)
ui/src/components/arrangeGeom.ts:296-302 (loopBars: both ends to the nearest bar line)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:370 (songLoop from loopBars)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:380 (loopX), 572-578 (loop lines on the clip area), 1722-1724 (「マーカー ・ ループ 6–7」), 1770-1772 (the accent loop mark on the ruler)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:1267 (startRulerDrag grabs the rounded ends)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/timeline.rs:464-480 (loop_from_marker: the range is the marker ticks as they are)
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:757-766 (bars: the status line rounds outward, first bar touched to the bar line at or after the end)
(the piano roll does the same: ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte:1015)

What happens

The arrange turns the loop range into bar line numbers with "nearest", and draws the loop mark, the two loop lines over the clips and the heading 「マーカー ・ ループ a–b」 from those numbers. When the loop range is not on bar lines, all of these sit at the nearest bar lines, not where the loop really turns. The status line's 「ループ a–b」 comes from Rust, which rounds outward instead, so the two texts show different numbers for the same loop.

A loop that is not on bar lines is easy to make: 「ここから次のマーカーまでをループに」 uses the two markers' ticks as they are, and markers snap to beats (K at the play line, a double press on the marker row, a marker drag). It also happens when a time signature change is put in earlier: the loop keeps its ticks (architecture 21-2) while the bar lines move.

Worked example (4/4, read from the code): markers at 5.3.0000 and 7.2.0000, then 「ここから次のマーカーまでをループに」. The loop plays from bar line 5.5 to bar line 7.25. The arrange draws it from bar line 6 to bar line 7 and says 「ループ 6–7」 (5.5 is a tie, so the later line; 7.25 goes to 7). The status line says 「ループ 5–8」. Grabbing an end of the drawn mark and dragging it also moves the other end to its rounded bar line on release (setLoop with the rounded fixed bar).

What should happen

What is drawn should be where the loop turns, and the two texts should agree. Spec 3 (ruler rules): "The loop range stays in whole bars"; spec 3 (Y18) offers 「ここから次のマーカーまでをループに」. Either the range made from markers is put on whole bars (then the drawing is right), or the screen draws the range at its real ticks and makes the numbers with the same rule as Rust's bars.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Stop at a place inside bar 5 and press K, move to a place inside bar 7 and press K, right-click the first marker, choose 「ここから次のマーカーまでをループに」, press Space: the loop turns at the markers, while the loop mark and lines are at the nearest bar lines and the heading and the status line show different numbers.

Cause

loopBars rounds each end to the nearest bar line (lineNear), while loop_from_marker (and an earlier time signature change) gives ranges that are not on bar lines, and Rust's bars rounds outward. Three different readings of the same TickRange.

Suggested fix

Needs a decision by the lead: (a) make loop_from_marker put the range on whole bars (the start bar's head and the bar line at or after the end), as spec 3 says the loop range stays in whole bars; or (b) keep the ticks and change the screen to draw the loop mark and lines at loopRange.start / loopRange.end, with the heading numbers made by the same rule as Play::bars. No protocol or wording change in either.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). loopBars (arrangeGeom.ts:296-302) rounds both ends to the nearest bar line, loop_from_marker (play/timeline.rs:464-480) keeps the marker ticks, and Rust's bars (play.rs:757-766) rounds outward; the worked example is right (5.5 → line 6 by the tie rule, 7.25 → line 7; Rust says 5–8).

Which side to follow is decided by the documents, so this is not needs-lead:

  • architecture 21-2: "Notes, clips, automation points, markers, tempo points and the loop range keep their ticks" when a time signature change is added earlier. So a loop range that is not on bar lines exists whatever loop_from_marker does, and the screen has to draw it right.
  • spec 3 (Y18): 「ここから次のマーカーまでをループに」 "(for the last marker, up to the end of the song)", and markers snap to beats, not bars: the range is meant to end at the marker. The doc comment of Rust's bars ("The bars the range touches … its first bar, and the bar line at its end or after it") shows Rust already expects such ranges.
  • spec 3 line 52 / 92 ("Dragging on the ruler sets the loop range in whole bars", "The loop range stays in whole bars") is about what a ruler drag makes; the ruler drag still makes whole bars.

So choice (b): Rust stays, the screen changes.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/arrangeGeom.ts give the loop two readings: the place to draw (range.start / range.end in ticks, as they are) and the numbers for the heading by the rule of Play::bars (the bar that holds the start, and the bar line at or after the end; the screen's tempo map has what is needed). In ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte use the ticks for loopX, the two loop lines and the accent mark (370, 380, 572-578, 1770-1772) and the outward numbers for 「マーカー ・ ループ a–b」 (1722-1724); the same in ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte:1015. startRulerDrag (1267): when an end is grabbed, the end that stays keeps its tick; SetLoop takes bar lines, so check in crates/meloet-protocol whether the fixed end can be sent as it is; if it cannot, the fixed end goes outward to its bar line on release (say so in the fix), not to the nearest one. Add a case to ui/tests/arrange.test.ts (loop 5.3–7.2 in 4/4: drawn at the ticks, heading 5–8). No protocol or wording change expected.

Fix

Screen only (Rust stays). ui/src/components/arrangeGeom.ts: loopBars now gives a LoopRange: the numbers by the rule of core's Play::bars (the bar that holds the start, the bar line at the end or after it) and the ticks start / end as they are; barLoop gives a ruler drag's bar lines with their ticks; startRulerDrag measures the grab at the drawn ticks, and the end that stays is its outward bar line number (SetLoop takes bar lines, so a range off the bar lines becomes whole bars, outward, when one end is dragged). ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte: the loop mark on the ruler and the two loop lines over the clips are drawn at the ticks; 「マーカー ・ ループ a–b」 uses the outward numbers (the same as the status line). ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte (3 lines: shownLoop, loopX, the loop mark): the piano roll's ruler draws at the ticks too. Tests: ui/tests/arrange.geom.test.ts "a loop range off the bar lines (markers 5.3 and 7.2 in 4/4) …" (new; the old "loop range (ticks) → bar line numbers" now also expects start / end), ui/tests/arrange.test.ts "a loop range off the bar lines (5.3–7.2): the mark at its ticks, 「ループ 5–8」 …".

UI-ARRANGE-003mediumbugfixedTrack name box: any change of the song section while typing puts the old name back and selects it

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-003-track-name-box-any-change-of-the-song-section-wh.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/TrackHeads.svelte:68-81 (the $effect that fills draft and focuses the box)

What happens

The $effect that opens the track name box reads trackEdit.renaming and also song.tracks (to find the track), so it runs again every time the song section is replaced while the box is open. Each run sets draft = track.name again and calls focus() and select(). What the person has typed so far is thrown away, the old name is back in the box, and it is all selected, so the next key replaces it.

The song section is sent again without the person editing the song in several normal cases, for example:

  • Mod S while the box is open (Mod keys work while typing, WITH_MOD in ui/src/keys/attach.ts:387-399): the save turns song.file from 「changed」 to 「saved」;
  • a plugin instrument or SF2 kit finishing its load and naming its keys (TrackView.drumKeys / keyNames), the sound pack arriving (standIn cleared): typical right after adding a track and going to name it;
  • an edit made by the AI link.

What should happen

Text being typed stays until Enter, Esc or a press outside (spec 3 / 7-readings "Track name input box": decided with Enter and when pressing outside, Esc stops). The sibling boxes already guard this: the clip name box (Arrange.svelte:716-734, nameFor), the marker name box (timeline/MarkerRow.svelte:135-147, openFor) and the repeat box (Arrange.svelte:1489-1501, repeatFor) fill their draft only when the box opens.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Double click a track name, type 「ベース」 (do not press Enter), press Ctrl S. The box shows the old name again, selected; pressing Enter now keeps the old name.

Cause

The effect has song as a dependency and no "only when renaming changes" guard, so draft = track.name and select() run on every new song object.

Suggested fix

Guard it like the clip name box: remember the id the draft was made for (let draftFor: number | null = null;), return early when trackEdit.renaming === draftFor, and read the track inside untrack (or only on the first run for that id). Keep the existing "track gone → close" rule. No protocol, wording or look change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). TrackHeads.svelte:68-81: the effect reads trackEdit.renaming and song.tracks (song is $derived(current.state!.song), a new object whenever the song part of the state is replaced), and on every run sets draft = track.name and calls focus() / select(). Nothing guards "only when the box opens". Mod S does run while typing (handleKeydown in keys/attach.ts: WITH_MOD actions are taken while isTyping), and a save changes the song part. The sibling boxes named in the report do guard it (Arrange.svelte nameFor, MarkerRow.svelte openFor).

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/components/TrackHeads.svelte remember the id the draft was filled for; fill draft and focus only when trackEdit.renaming changes to a new id; keep "track gone → close" on every run; clear the remembered id when renaming goes back to null. Add a test in ui/tests/ (type, replace the song part with a new object, the typed text stays).

Fix

ui/src/components/TrackHeads.svelte: the effect remembers the track id the draft was filled for (draftFor) and fills draft and focuses only when trackEdit.renaming turns to a new id; "track gone → close" still runs on every change; the id is forgotten when the box closes. Test: ui/tests/tracks-shelf.test.ts "name input box: a new song part while typing (a save, an AI edit) keeps the typed text".

UI-ARRANGE-004mediumui-uxfixedWhile playing, a page turn of the arrange moves a held clip, marker or point a whole page away from the pointer

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-004-while-playing-a-page-turn-of-the-arrange-moves-a.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:1080-1094 (frame(): pages with followScroll whenever follow is on, also during a drag)
ui/src/components/arrangeDrag.ts:198 (dragClipTo: the move is (x - press.x) / pxPerTick, px from the press, not ticks under the pointer)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:465 and 414 (the moving picture is placed with the current view plus dragShift)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:1347-1351, 1421 (box select keeps x0 in px of the old view)
ui/src/components/timeline/MarkerRow.svelte:67, ui/src/components/automation/autoGeom.ts:204, ui/src/components/timeline/tempoGeom.ts:255 (marker, automation point and tempo point drags: also (x - downX) / pxPerTick)

What happens

Clips, markers, automation points and tempo points can be dragged while the song plays (architecture 17: moves are taken while playing). During that drag frame() still pages the arrange with the play line (follow is on from the start of play until the person scrolls sideways by hand). The drag keeps its amount as "px moved since the press", so after a page turn the held thing keeps the same tick offset while the view has moved one whole width: the clip picture (placed at tickToX(view, first) + dragShift) jumps one page to the left, usually off the screen, and the pointer is left over empty space. Releasing puts the clip where the picture is, not under the pointer. Markers, automation points and tempo points do the same. A box select started before the page turn keeps its first corner in px, so it selects clips one page away from where it was started.

The code already knows this trap for the vertical wheel: wheelRows refuses to scroll "during a clip drag or a box select (what is held would slip from under the pointer)" (Arrange.svelte:1209-1211).

What should happen

What is held stays under the pointer (spec 3: while dragging, only the screen moves first and the original place keeps its border; the drag must follow the pointer). As for the vertical wheel, the arrange should not page while something is held, or the drag should count in ticks under the pointer.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Press Space so the song plays from the left edge of the arrange, and before the play line reaches the right edge, press a clip near the right side and keep moving it slowly. When the play line crosses the right edge, the view pages: the clip picture disappears to the left while the pointer stays where it was.

Cause

frame() calls G.followScroll with no check for clipDrag, boxSel or the drags of the marker row, the tempo band and the lanes; the drags measure their move in px since the press.

Suggested fix

Skip the follow paging in frame() while a drag or box select of the arrange is going on (clipDrag, boxSel, and a small shared flag set by MarkerRow, TempoBand, AutoLane, CompBand while they hold something), the same idea as wheelRows. Paging can go on at the next frame after release. The piano roll pages the same way during note drags (ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte:894-901, outside this area). No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). frame() (Arrange.svelte:1080-1094) pages with followScroll whenever follow is true; follow is set true when play starts (1124) and false only at a sideways wheel or scrollbar move (1184, 1672), never at a pointer press. The drags count px since the press (arrangeDrag.ts:198, MarkerRow.svelte:67, autoGeom.ts:204, tempoGeom.ts:255) and place what is held with the current view, so after a page turn the held thing is one view width away from the pointer, and release puts it there. wheelRows (1209-1211) already refuses to scroll during a clip drag for this reason. Severity stays medium: it needs "drag while playing across a page turn", but then the clip (or marker, point) lands a whole page away, off the screen, without the person seeing where.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte do not page in frame() while something is held in the arrange. The smallest form that covers the clip drag, the box select and the drags of MarkerRow, TempoBand, AutoLane and CompBand without a flag in each: one "a pointer is down inside the arrange" flag on the arrange's root (capture-phase pointerdown, cleared on pointerup / pointercancel on the window), read by frame(). Paging goes on at the first frame after release. The piano roll has the same fault (pianoroll/Roll.svelte:894-901, prView.follow during note and CC drags): fix it in the same change with the same rule.

Fix

ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte: a held flag, set by a capture-phase pointerdown on the arrange's section (covers the clip drag, box select, ruler, MarkerRow, TempoBand, AutoLane, CompBand) and cleared by pointerup / pointercancel on the window (capture phase) and the window's blur; frame() does not page while it is set, and pages at the first frame after release. No hook in timeline/ or automation/. The piano roll half (pianoroll/Roll.svelte) is left to the piano roll fixer. Test: ui/tests/arrange.test.ts "while a pointer is held in the arrange (a drag), no page turn; it pages at the first frame after release".

UI-ARRANGE-005mediumparityfixedDragging a clip onto a track of the other kind looks allowed; Rust refuses it with nothing said

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-005-dragging-a-clip-onto-a-track-of-the-other-kind-l.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/arrangeDrag.ts:149-158 (groupPlaceable: checks only "inside the tracks, not before 0, no overlap")
ui/src/components/arrangeDrag.ts:260-265 (clipDragCursor: move / copy while ok)
crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:235-236 (place: if doc.tracks[to].kind.is_vocal() == c.is_keys() { return Err(EditError::WrongKind) })
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1257-1261 (apply_placed: any error other than Overlap → None, no status text)

What happens

A keyboard clip dragged up or down onto a vocal track (or an audio clip onto a keyboard track) is shown as a normal move: the moving picture sits on that track, the cursor is move (copy with Alt), and the reading says the new position. On release moveClips is sent, the model answers WrongKind, core drops it without a status text, and the clip goes back to where it was. The person is not told why. The same happens with 「貼りつけ」 on an empty place of a track of the other kind (Arrange.svelte:1653: the row is usable whenever there is a clipboard).

What should happen

Architecture 22-15: "Moving or pasting a clip to a track of the other kind returns EditError::WrongKind." Architecture 17 / B-71: where a move cannot go ("outside the tracks"), "nothing happens and nothing is said (the screen shows a 'not allowed' cursor)". The screen should treat a row of the other kind the same: ok: false, the not-allowed cursor, nothing sent.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Make a keyboard track with a clip and a vocal track under it. Drag the keyboard clip down onto the vocal track's row and release: the picture shows it there with the move cursor, then it goes back, with nothing on the status line.

Cause

groupPlaceable does not compare the clip's kind (clip.audio !== null) with the target track's kind (track.kind.kind), while the model refuses that pair.

Suggested fix

In groupPlaceable, return false when a clip would land on a track of the other kind ((clip.audio !== null) !== (track.kind.kind === "vocal")), so ok is false and the cursor is not-allowed. Optionally make the empty-place 「貼りつけ」 row unusable on a track of the other kind when the clipboard holds only clips of the other kind (that needs to know the clipboard's kinds; today only clipboardClips is sent, so this half needs a protocol field and a lead decision). No wording change for the drag half.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run), the drag half. groupPlaceable (arrangeDrag.ts:149-158) checks only "a track is there, not before 0, no overlap"; the model's place returns EditError::WrongKind for a keyboard clip on a vocal track and the other way round (crates/meloet-model/src/arrange.rs:235-236), and apply_placed (crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1257-1261) turns every error but Overlap into "nothing, no text". So the screen shows move / copy and a place that Rust will refuse. Architecture 17 gives the rule for places a move cannot go: the "not allowed" cursor, nothing sent.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/arrangeDrag.ts groupPlaceable, return false when (clip.audio !== null) !== (track.kind.kind === "vocal"); add a case to the arrange drag tests. No protocol or wording change.

The paste half (「貼りつけ」 on an empty place of a track of the other kind, Arrange.svelte:1653) is real too but is left out of this report's fix: the screen only knows clipboardClips (a count), so disabling the row needs a new protocol field. It goes to docs/bugs/later/VERIFY-UI.md.

Fix

Drag half only. ui/src/components/arrangeDrag.ts groupPlaceable: false when a clip would land on a track of the other kind ((clip.audio !== null) !== (track.kind.kind === "vocal")), so ok is false, the cursor is not-allowed and nothing is sent. The paste half stays in docs/bugs/later/VERIFY-UI.md. Test: ui/tests/arrange.group.test.ts "a keyboard clip on a vocal track, or an audio clip on a keyboard track, is 「できない」 …".

UI-MIX-001mediumbugfixedMixer and built-in param drags are not cancelled when their part goes away, so the preview sound and the held lane stay

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-001-mixer-and-built-in-param-drags-are-not-cancelled.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts:80-86 (the onMount cleanup of mixControl: off(); keys.flush();, no cancel())
ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:143-149 (the onMount cleanup: off(); stopFrame();, no cancel())
ui/src/components/params/PluginParamRow.svelte:91-97 (the sibling that does it right: if (drag) cancel();)

What happens

When a fader, pan knob or send bar (mixControl) or a row of a built-in effect or a sound knob (ParamRow) is unmounted while its mouse drag is going on, nothing is sent: neither commit: true nor cancelMixPreview. Core keeps the last commit: false value in Mixer::previews, so the engine keeps playing the dragged value while the song (and the screen, after the part comes back) has the old one. If the control has an automation lane, the commit: false intents made a grip (hand_moved in crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:715) and held the lane; the grip is never let go, so the lane stays held and the control no longer follows the automation line. mixer_changed (play/mixer.rs:738) clears the previews on the next song change, but nothing clears auto.grips except CancelMixPreview (cancel_hands) or another drag of the same control. The screen-side hand value (noteHand in automation/handValue) is also never forgotten.

What should happen

CLAUDE.md rules and the comment of mixer/mixDrag.ts: a mixer drag ends with one commit: true on release or CancelMixPreview when it is cancelled ("Cancelled (Esc, or the pointer was taken)"). A drag cut short by the part going away is a cancel. PluginParamRow.svelte already cancels its drag in its unmount cleanup.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. 1. Open the mixer band. 2. Hold the mouse button on a track's fader and move it (the sound gets louder). 3. While still holding, press M (one-letter key toggleBand mixer; keys/keys.ts:119; handleKeydown has no guard for a mouse drag). The mixer closes, the Strip unmounts, and no intent is sent. The track stays at the dragged loudness although its fader value in the song did not change. Same with U (params panel, toggleBand params) or P (the shelf takes the right side in front of the params panel) during a drag of a built-in effect row, or any other way the part unmounts mid-drag. With a volume lane on the track and playback running, the lane stays held after step 3 and the volume stops following the line.

Cause

mixControl keeps the drag in press (with its MixPreview) and ParamRow in dragging, and both clean up on unmount without calling their own cancel(). The pending requestAnimationFrame of MixPreview can even send one more commit: false after the unmount (MixPreview.stop is never called).

Suggested fix

In the unmount cleanup, cancel a running drag first, as PluginParamRow does: in control.svelte.ts return () => { off(); cancel(); keys.flush(); }; and in ParamRow.svelte return () => { off(); cancel(); stopFrame(); }; (cancel() already does nothing when no drag runs). No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). Lines corrected in Where (control.svelte.ts:80-86). The unmount cleanup of mixControl is off(); keys.flush(); and that of ParamRow.svelte:143-149 is off(); stopFrame();; neither calls its own cancel(), while PluginParamRow.svelte:91-97 does (if (drag) cancel()). One-letter keys are taken while a mouse button is held (handleKeydown has no guard), so M / U / P during a drag unmounts the part.
