Rust side: commit: false values stay in Mixer::previews (crates/meloet-core/src/play/mixer.rs:245-251) until the same control is set again, CancelMixPreview, or the next mixer change, and a grip made by hand_moved (play/automation.rs:715) is removed only by hand_let_go or the cancel path; so the heard value and the song differ, and a lane stays held.

Fix plan (as suggested): ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts cleanup → off(); cancel(); keys.flush();; ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte cleanup → off(); cancel(); stopFrame(); (cancel() does nothing with no drag). Check that MixPreview.cancel() also drops its pending animation frame (ui/src/components/mixer/mixDrag.ts). Tests: mount a strip, press and move, unmount → cancelMixPreview was sent.

Fix

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts and ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte: the unmount cleanup calls cancel() first (sends cancelMixPreview for a running drag; MixPreview.cancel() already drops its pending frame). Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "UI-MIX-001: a drag whose part goes away is cancelled".

UI-MIX-002mediumbugfixedParams panel row stays frozen at the dragged value after a drag that writes automation

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-002-params-panel-row-stays-frozen-at-the-dragged-val.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:60-72 (held, shown = held ?? auto ?? value, and the effect that clears held only when value changes)
ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:121-132 (onup keeps held = v after commit: true)

What happens

On a parameter row of a built-in effect (BuiltinList) or a sound knob (SoundKnobList) whose automation lane is armed with 「● 書く」 while playing, a drag writes the lane: core answers the release with LetGo::Written (crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:756-762) and changes only the lane points, not the parameter's own value in the song. So the value prop of the row does not change, the effect that clears held never runs, and held keeps the last dragged value. Because held comes before auto in shown, the bar, the handle and the value text stay frozen at that value while the lane goes on moving the sound. It stays so until the song value of that parameter changes for some other reason.

What should happen

Architecture 21-8: what a band moves, the screen shows at the value on the line (auto, as the header comment of ParamRow.svelte says: "While a band moves it, the value is the one on the line"). The mixer does this right: mixControl drops its drag value (live) at release, and PluginParamRow clears held whenever new values come (tick).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. 1. Put a built-in reverb on a track insert and select it. 2. Press the row's automation mark to add a lane for 「ミックス」, arm it for writing (「● 書く」) and start playback. 3. Drag the 「ミックス」 bar from 30 % to 80 % and let go. 4. The lane now holds the written movement and plays it back, but the row keeps showing 80 % and the handle does not move.

Cause

held is cleared only by $effect(() => { void value; ... held = null }). A release that writes automation is not a change of value (Svelte does not re-run the effect for the same number), so nothing ever clears it.

Suggested fix

Clear held at the end of a drag when the answer cannot be the song value, or simply also when auto changes: e.g. make the effect read auto as well (void value; void auto;), or set held = null in onup when auto !== null. No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). ParamRow.svelte:60-72: shown = held ?? auto ?? value, and held is cleared only by the effect on value; onup (121-132) leaves held = v after commit: true. Rust: a release on an armed lane while playing ends in LetGo::Written (crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:756-762), and play/params.rs:210 does nothing more for Written, so the parameter's own value in the song does not change, the value prop stays the same number and the effect never runs. PluginParamRow and mixControl do not keep the value past the release this way.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte drop held when the answer to the release has come, not only when value changes: set(v, true) goes through send(), which returns the rev; clear held when that promise resolves and no new drag has started (this also covers a release whose value Rust refuses), and keep the existing effect. Simpler but weaker: also read auto in the clearing effect. Test: auto set, drag, release with value unchanged → the row shows auto again.

Fix

ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte: a step sent from the row (release of a drag, arrow keys, ◂ ▸) keeps held only until the state has reached the rev that answers it (commitStep, answer), so a release that only writes the lane goes back to auto; the clearing on value stays. Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "after a drag whose answer leaves the song value as it was, the row follows the lane again".

UI-MIX-004mediumui-uxfixedEsc does not close the preset list of a built-in effect unless focus is inside it

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-004-esc-does-not-close-the-preset-list-of-a-built-in.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/params/PresetMenu.svelte:58-68, 72 (Esc is read only by onkeydown on the .menu div)
ui/src/components/params/PresetMenu.svelte:62 (the first Esc in the name box removes the box, and focus leaves the menu)
ui/src/components/params/BuiltinList.svelte:132 (the list is opened here; the 「プリセット ▾」 button that opens it sits outside the menu)

What happens

The preset list of a built-in effect closes with Esc only while the keyboard focus is inside the list. Pressing 「プリセット ▾」 leaves the focus on that button (or where it was), which is outside the list, so Esc goes to the app's key handler instead: no drag, no menu, so it sends the general back intent and the list stays open. In the name box of 「いまの設定を残す…」, the first Esc puts the row back (naming = false), which removes the focused input, so the focus falls to the page and the second Esc also misses the list.

What should happen

Spec 3 (round 12 M1, 3-bands.md:409): 「Esc puts the row back, Esc again closes the list」; the header of PresetMenu.svelte: "Esc closes the list". Sibling small windows register with menuOpened (lib/menu.ts), so the global Esc order closes them wherever the focus is: the arpeggio window (InputMeter.svelte:96), the mute set name box (MuteSetBox.svelte:65).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. 1. Select a built-in reverb in the mixer. 2. Press 「プリセット ▾」 in the params panel. 3. Press Esc: the list stays open (and back is sent). Or: 2. open the list, press 「いまの設定を残す…」, press Esc (the row comes back), press Esc again: the list stays open.

Cause

Esc handling is a keydown handler on the list's own element; the list is not registered in the shared Esc order and does not take the focus when it opens or after the name box goes away.

Suggested fix

Register the open list with menuOpened(onclose) (lib/menu.ts) while it is open, as the arpeggio window does, so Esc closes it through closeMenus first; or focus the list (tabindex=-1) on open and again after the name box closes. No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). Lines corrected: Esc is read only by onkeydown on the .menu div (PresetMenu.svelte:58-68, 72); the list is opened from BuiltinList.svelte:132 by a button outside it; nothing focuses the list on open, and naming = false (62) removes the focused name box. The list is not registered with menuOpened (lib/menu.ts), so closeMenus() in handleKeydown does not know it and Esc goes on to back (which folds a band or leaves the place the person was in). Spec 3 line 409: "Esc puts the row back, Esc again closes the list". Not checked in WebKitGTK whether a press on 「プリセット ▾」 leaves the focus on the button or on the body; in both cases it is outside the list.

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys.

Fix plan for this report (as suggested): in ui/src/components/params/PresetMenu.svelte register the open list with menuOpened(onclose) while it is mounted, as InputMeter.svelte:96 and MuteSetBox.svelte:65 do, so the third turn of Esc closes it wherever the focus is; keep "Esc in the name box puts the row back" in the list's own handler, and after naming = false focus the list (tabindex −1) so the second Esc is the list's.

Fix

ui/src/components/params/PresetMenu.svelte: the open list registers with menuOpened (lib/menu.ts) while mounted, so the menus' turn of Esc (closeMenus in keys/attach.ts) closes it wherever the focus is. Esc in the name box still puts the row back first (the list's own handler stops it); the next Esc closes the list through closeMenus, so the list itself is not focused. Test: ui/tests/round12.presets.test.ts "UI-MIX-004: Esc closes the list wherever the focus is; in the name box the first Esc puts the row back".

UI-PARITY-003mediumparityfixedEdit > Delete in the menu bar ignores a marker, tempo change or automation point chosen last, unlike the Delete key it shows

docs/bugs/reports/UI-PARITY-003-edit-delete-in-the-menu-bar-ignores-a-marker-tem.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/menu/menus.ts:145 (「消す」 row: act("menu.delete", { kind: "deleteSelected" }, t("key.delete"), toArrange ? { usable: state.arrange.selectedClips.length > 0 && !busy } : {}))
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:205-206 (deleteSelected → { type: "deleteSelected" }, clips only)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:476-501 (the Delete key first goes through laneDeleteCommand and timelineKeyCommand; the menu row's runKeyAction never does)
ui/src/components/automation/laneKeys.ts:15-18, ui/src/components/timeline/keys.ts:62-66 (what the key does when lastPick is "lane" / "timeline")
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1132-1139 (delete_selected deletes only the chosen clips)

What happens

When the keys go to the arrange and the thing pressed last is a marker, a tempo point, a time signature flag or automation points (lastPick "timeline" or "lane"):

  • The Delete key deletes that marker / point / flag / those points (deleteTimeline, deleteAutoPoints).
  • 「編集 ▸ 消す」, which shows the key 「Delete」 on its right, is disabled when no clip is chosen. When clips are also still chosen (pressing a marker or a lane does not clear the clip choice), the row is usable and deletes the chosen clips instead of the marker or points.

What should happen

ui/src/components/menu/menus.ts:1 and attach.ts:2: "Rows with a key go the same way as the key". 7-readings A-21: "Delete works on the thing pressed and chosen last (clips, tempo points and flags, markers, automation points)". The menu row should be usable and act exactly when the Delete key would.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Choose a clip in the arrange, then press a marker in the marker row (it becomes chosen; the clip stays chosen). Open 「編集」 and press 「消す」: the clip is deleted, the marker stays. Pressing Delete instead deletes the marker. With no clip chosen, the 「消す」 row is disabled although Delete works.

Cause

The key path asks laneDeleteCommand and timelineKeyCommand (which look at lastPick) before keyAction; the menu row calls runKeyAction({ kind: "deleteSelected" }) directly, and its usable looks only at state.arrange.selectedClips.

Suggested fix

Give the menu row the same choice as the key: when the keys go to the arrange, build the command from laneDeleteCommand / timelineKeyCommand (with a synthetic plain Delete input and the same context) before falling back to deleteSelected, and base usable on whichever of them applies (a chosen timeline item that is not the start of the song, chosen automation points, or chosen clips). Best placed in keyCommand / keyActionUsable in attach.ts so the menu and the key share it. No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). The Delete key goes through laneDeleteCommand and timelineKeyCommand first (keys/attach.ts:476-501, they look at what was pressed last), then keyAction; the menu row (menus.ts:145) calls runKeyAction({ kind: "deleteSelected" }), which is { type: "deleteSelected" } (attach.ts:205-206, clips only; crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs delete_selected), and its usable looks only at selectedClips. 7-readings A-21: "Delete | Works on the thing pressed and chosen last (clips, tempo points and flags, markers, automation points)." The header rule of menus.ts / attach.ts: rows with a key go the same way as the key. Medium is right: with a clip still chosen and a marker pressed last, the row deletes the clip, not the marker the person is looking at (one undo puts it back).

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/keys/attach.ts make one function "what Delete does now" (lane → timeline → the old deleteSelected path) used by both handleKeydown and runKeyAction / the usable check of the menu row; ui/src/components/menu/menus.ts:145 takes usable from it. Tests in ui/tests/menu.test.ts: marker pressed last + a clip chosen → the row sends deleteTimeline; no clip chosen → the row is usable.

Fix

ui/src/keys/attach.ts: pickedDelete(state) asks the same rules as the Delete key (laneDeleteCommand, then timelineKeyCommand, with a plain Delete) when the keys go to the arrange; command() uses it for deleteSelected (so the menu row sends deleteAutoPoints / deleteTimeline as the key does), and arrangeDeleteUsable(state) gives the row's usable (points chosen, a timeline item that is not the start of the song, or chosen clips while not busy). ui/src/components/menu/menus.ts: 「消す」 takes usable from it. Tests: ui/tests/menu.test.ts "「消す」 in the menu bar goes the same way as Delete (UI-PARITY-003)" (three cases).

UI-PARITY-011mediumui-uxfixedLeft and right arrows do not move the parameter row chosen with up and down; they go on to move the selected notes

docs/bugs/reports/UI-PARITY-011-left-and-right-arrows-do-not-move-the-parameter.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/params/BuiltinList.svelte:75-78 (pick focuses the list box .list, not the row's control) and :108-123 (onkeydown handles only ↑ ↓ Enter; ← → are let through)
ui/src/components/params/SoundKnobList.svelte:43-46 and :48-66 (the same)
ui/src/components/params/PluginList.svelte:116-119 and :156-172 (the same)
ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:157-165 and params/PluginParamRow.svelte:105-116 (← → are handled only on the row's own slider / steps control, which has the focus only after Tab)
ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:103-105 (a press on the bar calls preventDefault, so pressing the bar does not focus it either)
ui/src/keys/keys.ts:208-209 (with no one else taking them, ← → become the nudge action: move the selected notes one grid step)

What happens

Choose a parameter row by pressing its name, or move the choice with ↑ ↓: the row is chosen (SelectParam) and the focus is on the panel's list box. Now ← → do not move the chosen row's value. The list's key handler lets them pass, they reach the document's key handler, and they become "move notes by one grid step": with the piano roll open and notes selected, the notes move (one undoable step each press). Only after pressing Tab until the slider itself has the focus do ← → move the value.

What should happen

Spec 3 (E3, the knobs of a built-in sound): "← → move the chosen row, ↑ ↓ choose, Enter types". Spec 4: "In the parameters: choose the row above / below ↑ ↓", "move the value / move it finely ← → / Shift ← →", "type a value Enter" are one group; ↑ ↓ and Enter already act on the chosen row from the list box (enter() in BuiltinList and PluginList, paramUi.typing = knobs.row in SoundKnobList), ← → do not.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open the piano roll and select some notes. Put a compressor on the track and choose it so the params panel shows it.
  2. Press the name 「しきい値」 in the panel (or press a row, then ↓): pick focuses .list.
  3. Press →: BuiltinList.onkeydown returns at line 117 without stopping it; the document handler (keys/attach.ts handleKeydown, context "main") gets nudge and the selected notes move right by one grid step. The threshold does not change.

The same with a plugin's rows and with 「明るさ」 … of a built-in sound.

Cause

← → are handled by the control element of each row (ParamRow / PluginParamRow), but choosing a row puts the focus on the list around the rows; the list handlers implement only ↑ ↓ and Enter of the group.

Suggested fix

In the three list onkeydowns, take ← → (and Shift ← →) when a row is chosen and send them to that row's step: for example move the focus to the chosen row's control in pick ([data-param="…"] [role=slider]), or let the list call the same keyStep / plugin step the row uses. Stop the key either way so it does not reach the document. No protocol, wording or look change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). BuiltinList.svelte:75-78 pick focuses the list box; its onkeydown (108-123) takes only Enter and ↑ ↓ and returns for everything else without stopping it; ← → then reach handleKeydown and become nudge (the same path that UI-SHELL-004's test showed sending moveNotes). SoundKnobList.svelte and PluginList.svelte have the same shape. Spec 3 (E3): "← → move the chosen row, ↑ ↓ choose, Enter types"; spec 4 lists the three as one group "In the parameters".

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys. For this report the key is not taken by anyone, so the early return on defaultPrevented does not help by itself: the lists must take ← →.

Fix plan for this report: in the onkeydown of ui/src/components/params/BuiltinList.svelte, SoundKnobList.svelte and PluginList.svelte, when a row is chosen, take ← → and Shift ← → (the handler returns early on shiftKey today) and run the same step the row's control runs (keyStep of ParamRow.svelte:157-165 / the step of PluginParamRow.svelte:105-116; share the function rather than write a second one); preventDefault + stopPropagation. Tests per list: choose a row, → sends setParam and no moveNotes.

Fix

ui/src/components/params/rowKeys.ts (new): arrowToChosenRow takes ← → and Shift ← → on a parameter list when a row is chosen, stops them, and hands them to that row's own control ([data-param] [role=slider]), so the row's own step (keyStep / the plugin step) is the one that runs. Called first in the onkeydown of ui/src/components/params/BuiltinList.svelte, SoundKnobList.svelte, PluginList.svelte. ParamRow.svelte and PluginParamRow.svelte are not changed. Tests: ui/tests/params.test.ts "UI-PARITY-011: with the focus on the list, ← → and Shift ← → …", ui/tests/e3.knobs.test.ts "UI-PARITY-011: …", ui/tests/round5.step3.test.ts "UI-PARITY-011: … plugin row …".

UI-ROLL-001mediumbugfixedPiano roll toolbar take buttons switch the arrange's chosen clip, not the clip the piano roll shows

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ROLL-001-piano-roll-toolbar-take-buttons-switch-the-arran.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/Toolbar.svelte:77-81 — takeTag is made from shown.clip (the clip the piano roll shows, arrange.pianoRollClip).
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/Toolbar.svelte:225 and :234 — ◂ ▸ send { type: "stepTake", delta }, which names no clip; they are disabled only by takeTag.recording.
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1295-1299 — Play::step_take acts on self.selected (arrange.selectedClip).
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1055-1060 — select_clip(None) (a press on an empty place of the arrange) clears selected but keeps piano_roll.
  • crates/meloet-protocol/src/lib.rs:2810-2816 — selected_clip and piano_roll_clip are kept apart on purpose ("kept after it is unselected").

What happens

The take tag 「◂ テイク 2 / 3 ▸ T」 on the piano roll toolbar shows the take of the clip the piano roll shows, but its ◂ ▸ switch the take of the clip chosen in the arrange. The two are often not the same clip:

  • After a press on an empty place of the arrange, no clip is chosen but the piano roll keeps its clip and its tag. ◂ ▸ then do nothing at all.
  • After a box in the arrange that leaves one other clip chosen (core makes it the clicked clip, but not the shown one; see the test in crates/meloet-core/src/play/arrange_tests.rs:289-295), ◂ ▸ switch the take of that other clip: its sound changes and an undo step is made, while the piano roll and its tag stay as they were.
  • During the count-in, or while recording on another track, ◂ ▸ look usable but core does nothing (step_take returns at self.busy()); the arrange's 「次のテイク」「前のテイク」 rows are disabled then.

What should happen

Spec 3, "Rules for round 3": "When a clip with 2 or more takes is opened in the piano roll, the same switching tab (「◂ テイク 2 / 3 ▸ T」) is shown next to the track name on the toolbar." The tab belongs to the clip the piano roll shows, so ◂ ▸ should switch that clip's take (the arrange's own tab acts on the clip it is drawn on, Arrange.svelte switcher from selected). Readings A-8: next take / previous take are disabled while recording.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Record two takes into clip A on track 1. Press A in the arrange: the piano roll shows A and 「◂ テイク 2 / 2 ▸ T」.
  2. Press an empty place of the arrange lanes (no drag). selectClip { clip: null } is sent (Arrange.svelte:1424); the piano roll still shows A with the tag.
  3. Press ▸ on the piano roll toolbar: nothing changes.

Variant: in step 2 draw a box around only clip B (2 or more takes) on track 2, then press ▸ in the piano roll: B's take changes, A does not.

Cause

The toolbar decides what it shows from shown.clip but sends StepTake, which has no clip id; core reads self.selected, which is not the piano roll's clip after a press on an empty place, a box, Mod A or a box of one clip. The buttons' disabled state looks only at takeTag.recording (recording into this clip), not at the count-in or a recording elsewhere.

Suggested fix

Make ◂ ▸ act on the shown clip. Either (a) give StepTake an optional clip id (or add a command with the clip, like DeleteTake { clip }) and send shown.clip.id from the toolbar — a protocol change; or (b) without a protocol change, disable ◂ ▸ (and drop the 「T」 hint) unless arrange.selectedClip === shown.clip.id, and also while counting in or recording. (b) leaves the tag unusable in the cases above. Which one is a decision for the lead.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). The tag is made from the shown clip (Toolbar.svelte:77-81), its buttons send stepTake with no clip (225, 234), and step_take acts on self.selected (crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1299); select_clip(None) clears selected and keeps the piano roll's clip (1055-1060). So after a press on an empty place of the arrange the buttons do nothing, and after a box that leaves another clip chosen they change that other clip. The buttons are also not disabled while counting in or recording elsewhere (disabled={takeTag.recording} only), where step_take returns at busy(); that half is the same root as UI-ROLL-008 / UI-ARRANGE-009.

A fix without a protocol change exists, so this is confirmed, not needs-lead:

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/pianoroll/Toolbar.svelte, when current.state.arrange.selectedClip !== clip.id, send { type: "selectClip", clip: clip.id } before stepTake (both through send, which keeps the order; selectClip is not an undo step and the piano roll already shows that clip, so only the arrange's choice moves to the clip whose take is being switched, which is also what the arrange's own tag needs to show the result). Disable ◂ ▸ also while transport.state is recording or countIn. Add a test: shown clip ≠ chosen clip → the two intents in this order.

The other way, if the lead does not want the arrange's choice to move: give StepTake a clip id (protocol version change, the T key and the menu rows then send the chosen clip). Not needed for the fix.

Fix

No protocol change.

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/Toolbar.svelte: ◂ ▸ send selectClip for the shown clip first when the arrange has another clip (or none) chosen, then stepTake; they are disabled while recording or counting in (songBusy), the rule of 「次のテイク」「前のテイク」 in the menu.
  • Tests: ui/tests/pianoroll.band.test.ts "◂ ▸ switch the take of the shown clip: when the arrange chose another clip or none, the shown one is chosen first (UI-ROLL-001)" and "◂ ▸ cannot be used while counting in or recording, as the menu's 「次のテイク」「前のテイク」 (UI-ROLL-001)".
UI-ROLL-002mediumbugfixedNote list and CC list: Tab after a value that reorders the take opens the next field on another note and edits it

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ROLL-002-note-list-and-cc-list-tab-after-a-value-that-reo.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/NoteListPanel.svelte:149-164 — finish sends the decided value, then opens the next field at row e.at + next.row with open(), whose draft is read from the take shown before the answer.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/NoteListPanel.svelte:118-123 — the box is kept by row number (editing.at); editSpec is made again from whatever note is at that row in the new take.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/NoteListPanel.svelte:153 — the command goes to indexes[e.at] of the take shown at that moment.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/noteFields.ts:315-317 — commitCommand compares the typed value with spec.current of the note now at that row, so an unchanged box still sends a change.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/CcListPanel.svelte:102-106, :132-146 and ui/src/components/pianoroll/ccFields.ts:176-182 — the same in 「CC の一覧」, where the box is kept by the index in the take.

What happens

Read from the code. The note list has row 0 C3 at 1.1.0000, row 1 D3 at 1.2.0000, row 2 E3 at 1.3.0000. Open 「位置」 of row 0, type 1.4, press Tab.

  1. finish sends setNotesField (start) for index 0 and at once opens 「音」 of row 0 with the text of the old take: C3.
  2. Rust answers with a new take in start order: D3, E3, C3. Row 0 is now D3 (its position, length and velocity are shown in the row), and the moved C3 is selected on row 2. The open box on row 0 still says C3.
  3. Pressing Enter or Tab without typing sends setNotesField key = 48 for index 0 of the new take: D3 becomes C3. Typing a new note name for the moved note changes D3 instead.

The same happens with Shift Tab, and with 「音」 among notes that start together (the order is start, then key). In 「CC の一覧」: two sustain points, index 0 at 1.1 (127) and index 1 at 1.2 (0); type a later position for index 0 and press Tab: the 「値」 box opens for index 0, which after the answer is the other point, on the same row, with 127 in it; Enter sends moveControls value +127 for that point, so the pedal never comes up there.

What should happen

Spec 3, "Note list": "Tab goes to the field on the right" — the field on the right of the same note. A box that was not changed must not change anything (commitCommand is meant to return null then). Architecture 16: notes are pointed to by take id and index; an index taken from an earlier take must not edit another note.

How to trigger

As above, read from the code, not run. Any typed 「位置」 that moves a note past the start of another note, or a typed 「音」 among notes with the same start, followed by Tab or Shift Tab, then Enter.

Cause

The box remembers a row number (note list) or a take index (CC list), and its draft is filled from the take before the answer. When the answer's take arrives, editSpec follows whatever note or point is now at that row or index, but the text in the box does not; deciding then aims at the note now at that place. The panel's header comment ("The row being typed in is remembered by its row number in the list (deciding changes the take id)") covers the new id but not the new order.

Suggested fix

Follow the edited note, not the row: Rust selects the edited notes after an edit (architecture 16), so the note list can open the next field on the row of the selected note once the new take is there, with its text from the new take. Simpler, and also for the CC list: close the box after a value that can move the note or point (position, note; position in the CC list) instead of moving on with Tab. No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). finish (NoteListPanel.svelte:149-164) sends the value and at once opens the next field at the same row number with draft from the take shown before the answer; editSpec (123) is made again from whatever note is at that row in the new take; commitCommand (noteFields.ts:315-317) compares the typed number with spec.current of that other note, so the old text, unchanged, is sent as a change to the other note with the new take's id (Rust takes it: the id is the shown take). Nothing closes or refills the box when the take id changes (the header comment says the row number is kept on purpose across the new id). CcListPanel.svelte:132-146 does the same by index. The worked example is right: the model keeps notes in start, then key order.

Fix plan (the smaller of the two in the report, no new mechanism): in ui/src/components/pianoroll/NoteListPanel.svelte and CcListPanel.svelte, when a decided value was really sent (command !== null) and the field can move the row (note list: position, key; CC list: position), do not open the next field from the old take: wait for the reply (send() returns the rev) and for the take with the new id, then open the next field on the row of the note Rust selected (architecture 16: edited notes are selected after an edit; pr.selectedNotes → row through indexes), with its text from the new take; if that row cannot be found, close the box. For fields that cannot reorder (length, velocity, CC value) today's path stays. Tests: the report's example for both lists (Tab after a position that reorders, then Enter: no second intent; the box is on the moved note).

Fix

No protocol change.

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/NoteListPanel.svelte: after Tab (or Shift Tab) decided a 「位置」 or 「音」 that was really sent, the box closes and the next field waits (after) for the reply's rev and for the take Rust now shows; it then opens on the row of the note Rust selected (the edited note), with its text from that take. If Rust kept the same take, it opens on the same row as before; if the row is not found, nothing opens. Other fields move on at once as before.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/CcListPanel.svelte: the same for 「位置」 in 「CC の一覧」 (the moved point is the selected point).
  • Tests: ui/tests/pianoroll.panels.test.ts "the next field opens on the moved note once Rust's take is shown, with its text. Deciding it unchanged sends nothing" and "when Rust changed nothing (the same take), the next field opens on the same row"; ui/tests/pianoroll.r5-notes.test.ts "Tab after a position that changes the order opens 「値」 of the moved point once Rust's take is shown; unchanged it sends nothing (UI-ROLL-002)". Changed to the right behavior: pianoroll.panels.test.ts "↑ ↓ by 1 …" now waits for the answer after Shift Tab from a changed pitch.
UI-ROLL-003mediumbugfixedA note or CC drag sends the take id shown at release with the indexes picked at the press

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ROLL-003-a-note-or-cc-drag-sends-the-take-id-shown-at-rel.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/drag.ts:191-255 — down picks notes, grab and hits as indexes of the take shown at the press; the drag does not keep that take's id.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/drag.ts:375-381 — up reads this.takeId() (the take shown at the release) and passes it to finish.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/drag.ts:452-473 — finish puts that id together with the press-time indexes into moveNotes, resizeNotes, deleteNotes, changeVelocity, selectNotes.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/cc.ts:352-357 — LaneMouse.up does the same for moveControls, deleteControls, selectControls.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte:280 and CcLanes.svelte:85 — shown() returns the take shown now; nothing ends or marks a drag when it changes.

What happens

If the shown take changes while the mouse button is held, the release sends the new take's id with indexes taken from the old take. Core accepts it, because the id is the take shown now, and edits whatever notes have those indexes in the new take: other notes are moved, resized, given another velocity, or erased, and the wrong notes are selected. While the button is held the take can change by keys (the key handler does not wait for a drag: Mod Z, Mod Shift Z, Delete, ↑ ↓ ← →, Q, T), by an edit from the AI link, or when the new take of the edit just before (a drag let go a moment ago, a ↑ ↓) arrives after the press.

What should happen

Architecture 16, "Note order and how notes are pointed to": "An intent points by take id and index in that take. If the id is not the shown take, Rust does nothing (so an old index does not edit another note, e.g. when a key is pressed right after letting go of a drag)." This guard works only when the id sent is the id of the take the indexes were read from.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. In a clip with notes A, B, C (indexes 0, 1, 2), draw a new note X before A (one undo step; the take is now X, A, B, C, and B is index 2).
  2. Press B and start dragging it to the right; keep the button down.
  3. Press Mod Z: the take goes back to A, B, C (the earlier id), where index 2 is C.
  4. Let go: moveNotes { take: <earlier id>, notes: [2] } is sent and C moves instead of B. With the erase tool the same steps delete C.

Cause

RollDrag.up() and LaneMouse.up() take the id from input.shown() at the release, not from the press. The indexes in the drag belong to the take of the press.

Suggested fix

Keep the take id in the drag at the press (a take field in Drag and LaneDrag) and use that id in finish / finishLane; core then does nothing for a drag whose take is gone. Optionally cancel the drag (as Esc does) when n.take.id changes during it. No protocol change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). RollDrag.down keeps indexes of the take shown at the press and no take id; up() reads this.takeId() at release (drag.ts:375-381) and finish(d, take) joins that id with the old indexes (452-473); LaneMouse.up the same (cc.ts:352-357). Nothing cancels a drag when the shown take changes (grep in Roll.svelte / CcLanes.svelte: cancel() only from Esc and pointercancel), and handleKeydown (keys/attach.ts) does not look at a held drag, so Mod Z during a held drag reaches core. Rust's guard (architecture 16: "If the id is not the shown take, Rust does nothing") is passed because the id sent is the new one.

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/components/pianoroll/drag.ts keep the take id in the drag at down and use it in up / finish; the same for LaneDrag in cc.ts; core then does nothing for a drag whose take is gone (the drawn look goes back by itself). Also cancel the held drag when n.take.id changes (the moving picture would otherwise be drawn with old indexes on the new take's notes, the same kind of fault as UI-ARRANGE-001). Tests in ui/tests/pianoroll*.test.ts: press, change the shown take, release → the intent carries the press-time id.

Fix

No protocol change.

  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/drag.ts: RollDrag keeps the take id shown at the press (pressTake) and sends it on release; dropIfTakeChanged() cancels a held drag when another take is shown.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/cc.ts: the same for LaneMouse.
  • ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte, ui/src/components/pianoroll/CcLanes.svelte: an effect on the shown take id calls dropIfTakeChanged().
  • Tests: ui/tests/pianoroll.drag.test.ts "a drag keeps the take of the press (UI-ROLL-003)" (3 tests); ui/tests/pianoroll.cc.test.ts "CC drags: the take of the press, and while recording (UI-ROLL-003, UI-ROLL-008)"; ui/tests/pianoroll.hold-mount.test.ts "a drag of the take shown at the press (UI-ROLL-003)".
UI-SHELL-001mediumbugfixedArrow keys and Enter go to the output chooser even while a dialog is open

docs/bugs/reports/UI-SHELL-001-arrow-keys-and-enter-go-to-the-output-chooser-ev.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/keys/attach.ts:467-473 (the context is "outputChooser" whenever state.workspace.chooser is set, before any dialog is looked at)
ui/src/keys/keys.ts:376-380 (in that context ↑ ↓ Enter become chooserUp / chooserDown / chooserConfirm)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:511-541 (the plain-Enter branches for the dialogs run only when keyAction returned null)
crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:3018-3020 (chooser_shown does not look at dialogs, so the chooser stays in the state while a dialog is open)

What happens

While the output chooser is shown (no output chosen yet and a keyboard is there; flow 2), the person can still open the sound settings (Mod ,), a song dialog (Mod N / Mod O with changes), the export dialog, or the scan-stopped dialog. In any of them:

  • Enter sends chooserConfirm instead of the dialog's bright button. In the sound settings this plays from the output highlighted in the chooser behind, not the output picked in the dialog, and the dialog stays open. In a song dialog («保存しますか» …) Enter does not answer the dialog at all.
  • ↑ ↓ move the highlight of the chooser behind the dialog.

What should happen

Spec 3 (dialogs about saving): "Enter is the button at the right end". The sound settings: Enter = 「OK」 (settingsApply, attach.ts:538-540). A dialog is in front, so its keys come first (the Esc order of spec 3 puts the dialog before the parts behind it). The sibling contexts already do this: shelfHasKeys (ui/src/components/tracks.svelte.ts:58-67) and historyKeys (attach.ts:424-435) both return false while songDialog, settings or the scan-stopped dialog is open.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Start with no output decided and one keyboard (or meloet-vkeys appear): the output chooser shows.
  2. Press Mod , : the sound settings open. Click an output row other than the one highlighted in the chooser.
  3. Press Enter: chooserConfirm is sent; the chooser's highlighted output is chosen, the dialog stays, and the draft output is not used.

Also: with the chooser shown, make a change, press Mod N, press Enter: the 「保存しますか」 dialog is not answered.

Cause

handleKeydown picks context = state.workspace.chooser ? "outputChooser" : … without checking songDialog, settings, export.dialog, exportedOpen(state) or plugins.stoppedOpen. keyAction then returns a chooser action for ↑ ↓ Enter, so action is not null and the plain-Enter dialog branches (attach.ts:511-541) are never reached.

Suggested fix

Use the "outputChooser" context only when no dialog is open (the same checks as historyKeys: songDialog, settings, export.dialog, exportedOpen, plugins.stoppedOpen, and about.open / keyList.open). No protocol, wording or looks change.

Verification

Confirmed with a test that was run (scratch worktree, not kept): handleKeydown with a state that has workspace.chooser set and workspace.settings set sent chooserConfirm for Enter (and the chooser's step for ↓); with songDialog set instead, Enter did not send answerSongDialog. Read: attach.ts:467-473 picks the "outputChooser" context from state.workspace.chooser alone, and chooser_shown in core (crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:3018-3020) does not look at dialogs. Not checked in the real app that every dialog named can be opened while the chooser is shown (the menu rows and Mod , are not disabled by the chooser as far as I read).

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys.

Fix plan for this report: in ui/src/keys/attach.ts use the "outputChooser" context only when no dialog is in front (songDialog, settings, latencyDialog, export.dialog, exportedOpen, plugins.stoppedOpen, about.open, keyList.open); add the case to ui/tests/keys*.test.ts.

Fix

ui/src/keys/front.ts (new): dialogInFront(state) names the dialog that has the keys (About, the key list, the scan-stopped dialog, a song dialog, the export dialog, 「書き出しました」, the latency dialog, the sound settings; in the Esc order: the screen's own dialogs first, then the order core's Back closes them). ui/src/keys/attach.ts: handleKeydown uses the "outputChooser" (and "shelf", "history") context only when no dialog is in front; the plain-Enter branches go by dialogInFront. Test: ui/tests/keys.front.test.ts "UI-SHELL-001: …" (two cases); ui/tests/screen.test.ts "↑ ↓ Enter in the output chooser, Enter in sound settings" still passes unchanged.

UI-SHELL-002mediumbugfixedKeys stay with the shelf while the export dialog or the exported dialog is open

docs/bugs/reports/UI-SHELL-002-keys-stay-with-the-shelf-while-the-export-dialog.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/tracks.svelte.ts:58-67 (shelfHasKeys checks songDialog, settings and the scan-stopped dialog only)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:469-470 (the "shelf" context comes from shelfHasKeys)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:530-537 (the Enter branches of the export dialog and 「書き出しました」 run only when keyAction returned null)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:618-619 (a press inside a dialog does not clear shelfUi.keys: dialogs are outside .app)

What happens

When the shelf had the keys (a shelf row was clicked) and the export dialog is opened with Mod Shift E, the keys still go to the shelf:

  • Enter sends shelfConfirm (makes a new track from the sound being auditioned, or puts in the chosen effect) instead of 「書き出す」. With nothing chosen in the shelf, Enter does nothing at all.
  • ↑ ↓ change the audition in the shelf behind the dialog; Space plays the shelf sample; F sets a favorite.

The same happens in the 「書き出しました」 dialog: Enter does not close it. Clicking inside the dialog does not help, because handlePointerdown clears shelfUi.keys only for presses inside .app, and dialogs are outside it.

What should happen

A dialog in front takes the keys. Spec 4: 「書き出しました」 closes with Esc / Enter; attach.ts:530-533 says Enter in the export dialog is 「書き出す」. The sibling check historyKeys (attach.ts:424-435) already leaves out export.dialog and exportedOpen(state); shelfHasKeys should do the same.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open the shelf (P) and click an instrument row: the audition starts and the shelf has the keys.
  2. Press Mod Shift E: the export dialog opens (core does not end the audition on OpenExport, crates/meloet-core/src/export.rs:413).
  3. Press Enter: shelfConfirm is sent and a new track is made behind the dialog; the export does not start.

Cause

shelfHasKeys returns true while export.dialog or the exported dialog is open, so handleKeydown uses the "shelf" context; keyAction maps Enter to shelfConfirm (an action, even when its command is null), so the plain-Enter branch for the export dialog is skipped.

Suggested fix

In shelfHasKeys also return false when state.export.dialog !== null or exportedOpen(state) !== null (as historyKeys does). No protocol, wording or looks change. (About and the key list are already listed as known in docs/progress.md, "one-letter keys still work"; they could get the same check.)

Verification

Confirmed with a test that was run (scratch worktree, not kept): with shelfUi.keys = true, the shelf in front and export.dialog set, Enter did not send startExport; with shelfUi.keys = false it did. Read: shelfHasKeys (tracks.svelte.ts:58-67) leaves out export.dialog and the exported dialog, which historyKeys (attach.ts:424-435) has; handlePointerdown clears shelfUi.keys only for presses inside .app, and dialogs are outside it (App.svelte).

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys.

Fix plan for this report: in ui/src/components/tracks.svelte.ts shelfHasKeys also returns false for state.export.dialog !== null, exportedOpen(state) !== null, the latency dialog, About and the key list (the shared "dialog in front" check). docs/progress.md lists "one-letter keys still work" under About and the key list as known; that note is about letters, not about the shelf's Enter and arrows.

Fix

ui/src/components/tracks.svelte.ts: shelfHasKeys is false while any dialog is in front (dialogInFront of ui/src/keys/front.ts: also the export dialog, 「書き出しました」, the latency dialog, About and the key list). historyKeys and auditionToCancel in ui/src/keys/attach.ts use the same check. Test: ui/tests/keys.front.test.ts "UI-SHELL-002: the shelf behind the export dialog or 「書き出しました」 does not take Enter or ↓".

UI-SHELL-003mediumbugfixedEsc and Enter in the latency dialog act on the sound settings dialog under it

docs/bugs/reports/UI-SHELL-003-esc-and-enter-in-the-latency-dialog-act-on-the-s.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/keys/attach.ts:538-540 (plain Enter with workspace.settings sends settingsApply)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:555-564 (Esc sends back; nothing looks at workspace.latencyDialog)
ui/src/components/LatencyDialog.svelte:49-50 (cancel is written for Esc but only the button calls it)
crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:1702-1710 (Back closes the settings dialog, self.dialog, and never the latency dialog)
ui/src/App.svelte:98-107 (the latency dialog is shown whenever latencyDialog is set, with or without the settings)

What happens

The latency dialog is opened from 「遅れを測る…」 in the advanced sound settings, so the settings dialog is still open in core under it.

  • Esc sends back; core closes the hidden sound settings, and the latency dialog stays on screen (measuring goes on if it was measuring). A second Esc folds a band or leaves the arrange; the latency dialog never closes with Esc.
  • Enter sends settingsApply: the hidden settings dialog is applied and closed (a different draft output is chosen), and the latency dialog stays.

What should happen

Spec 4: Esc closes a dialog / goes back one, and the dialog in front comes first (spec 3 Esc order). The latency dialog's own comment (LatencyDialog.svelte:49) says Esc is 「やめる」: while measuring it stops measuring (stopLatencyMeasure), otherwise it closes the dialog (closeLatencyMeasure). Enter should do nothing to the settings under it (the bright button of this dialog, or nothing).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Mod , → 「くわしい設定」 → 「遅れを測る…」: the latency dialog opens over the settings.
  2. Press Esc: the settings close in core; the latency dialog stays open. Or press Enter: settingsApply is sent; the latency dialog stays open.

Cause

handleKeydown has no branch for state.workspace.latencyDialog. Esc falls through to { type: "back" }, whose core handler closes self.dialog (the settings) first; plain Enter matches the state.workspace.settings branch because the settings are still open under the latency dialog.

Suggested fix

In attach.ts, before the settings branches: when state.workspace.latencyDialog is set, Esc sends stopLatencyMeasure while measuring and closeLatencyMeasure otherwise (the same as the dialog's cancel), and plain Enter does not send settingsApply (it can press the dialog's bright button, or do nothing). No protocol, wording or looks change.

Verification

Confirmed with a test that was run (scratch worktree, not kept): with workspace.settings and workspace.latencyDialog both set, handleKeydown sent exactly { type: "back" } for Esc and { type: "settingsApply" } for Enter. Read: core's Back (crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs:1702-1710) closes the scan-stopped dialog, the song dialog, then self.dialog (the sound settings); it never touches the latency dialog; App.svelte:98-107 draws the latency dialog in place of the settings while both are set. LatencyDialog.svelte:49-50 has cancel "(Esc)", called only by the button.

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys.

Fix plan for this report: in ui/src/keys/attach.ts, when state.workspace.latencyDialog is set: Esc sends stopLatencyMeasure while measuring, else closeLatencyMeasure (what cancel does; share one function with the dialog); plain Enter sends nothing to the settings (spec 3 gives this dialog no Enter rule, so nothing is the safe choice). No protocol or wording change.

Fix

ui/src/keys/attach.ts: with the latency dialog in front, Esc sends latencyCancel(measure) (stopLatencyMeasure while measuring, else closeLatencyMeasure) and plain Enter sends nothing. latencyCancel lives in ui/src/keys/front.ts; cancel of ui/src/components/LatencyDialog.svelte uses it too. Test: ui/tests/keys.front.test.ts "UI-SHELL-003: …".

UI-SHELL-004mediumbugfixedArrow keys in a menu bar list also move the selected notes in the piano roll

docs/bugs/reports/UI-SHELL-004-arrow-keys-in-a-menu-bar-list-also-move-the-sele.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/menu/MenuList.svelte:90-118 (onkeydown ends with ev.stopPropagation(), not stopImmediatePropagation())
ui/src/components/menu/MenuBar.svelte:31-34, 103 (the list is moved to body) and ui/src/components/menu/ContextMenu.svelte (same)
ui/src/keys/attach.ts:452-583 (handleKeydown on document does not look at ev.defaultPrevented or at whether the focus is in a menu)
ui/src/keys/keys.ts:402-403 (↑ ↓ ← → become nudge)

What happens

The menu bar list and the context menu are moved to body. Svelte 5.57.3 hears delegated keydown for such nodes with its listener on document (node_modules/svelte/src/internal/client/render.js:230-250), which was added at mount, before attachKeys added its own listener on document. stopPropagation() in MenuList does not stop another listener on the same node, so every ↑ ↓ ← → and Enter used to move in the list also runs handleKeydown:

  • With the piano roll open, pressed last, and notes selected, each ↑ ↓ in the list moves the selected notes a semitone and each ← → moves them a grid step (a moveNotes step each, plus the audition of the moved key).
  • With the shelf having the keys, ↑ ↓ change the audition; with the output chooser shown, they move its highlight.
  • Enter on a row while the sound settings are open also sends settingsApply.

What should happen

Keys used inside an open list belong to the list (spec 2 "List": ↑ ↓ move between rows, Enter acts, ← → open or close child lists). They must not edit the song. MetronomeWindow.svelte:130-134 shows the way that works for a node in body: stopImmediatePropagation().

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open the piano roll (E), click in it, select some notes.
  2. Press F10 (or click 「ファイル」) and press ↓ three times: the focus walks down the rows, and the selected notes go down three semitones (three undo steps).
  3. Press → to step to the next menu: the notes also move one grid step right.

The test in ui/tests/menu.test.ts:581-630 does not see it because the piano roll is closed there (a nudge then has no command).

Cause

Order of listeners on document: Svelte's delegated handler (calls MenuList's onkeydown, which only calls stopPropagation) runs first, then handleKeydown from keys/attach.ts runs anyway, because both listeners are on the same node. handleKeydown does not check ev.defaultPrevented. docs/progress.md lists the same mechanism for ChoiceMenu and the zoom lists as "not checked whether it does harm"; this is the harm, in the menu bar and context menu lists.

Suggested fix

In MenuList's onkeydown, call ev.stopImmediatePropagation() (as MetronomeWindow does) for the keys it takes. Or make handleKeydown return early when ev.defaultPrevented is true (this would also cover ChoiceMenu and the zoom lists). No protocol, wording or looks change.

Verification

Confirmed with a test that was run (scratch worktree, not kept): MenuBar mounted in a #app div as in the real app (its list moves itself to body), handleKeydown on document, the piano roll open with one note selected and pressed last. F10, then ↓: the focus moved to the next row of the list and a moveNotes intent was sent. The existing test (ui/tests/menu.test.ts:581-630) mounts into body and has the piano roll closed, so it sees neither.

Why (read in ui/node_modules/svelte 5.57.3, src/internal/client/render.js): delegated events are heard on the mount target and on document; a node outside the mount target is served by the document listener, and stopPropagation() there cannot stop handleKeydown, which is on the same node.

This is more than the known note in docs/progress.md ("Lists moved to body … stop key events with stopPropagation, which does not stop the document key handler on the same node (R3 found it; not checked whether it does harm)"): here is the harm, an undo step in the song per arrow key, in the menu bar and context menu lists. The fix should close that note too (ChoiceMenu, the zoom lists).

One root with UI-SHELL-001, -002, -003, -004, -005, UI-PARITY-011, UI-PARITY-015 and UI-MIX-004: a key that belongs to the part in front (a dialog, an open list, a focused control) falls through to the document key handler (handleKeydown in ui/src/keys/attach.ts), which does not know that part is in front. Fix them as one piece of work with one rule in handleKeydown: (1) one "which dialog is in front" check used by every context (outputChooser, shelf, history) and by the Enter / Esc branches, in the order the dialogs are drawn in App.svelte; (2) return early when ev.defaultPrevented (a part that took the key says so), which covers lists moved to body; then each part only has to take its own keys.

Fix plan for this report: handleKeydown returns early when ev.defaultPrevented (MenuList.svelte:117 already calls preventDefault for the keys it takes), placed after the typedKeys / hint lines if those must still see the key. Check the other preventDefault callers in ui/src so no key that the document handler must still see is prevented earlier (grep preventDefault in keydown handlers). Add the test above to ui/tests/menu.test.ts (mount into a child of body).

Fix

ui/src/keys/attach.ts: handleKeydown returns at once when the key is already defaultPrevented (after the 「キーで弾く」 keys and the hint), so keys taken by a list moved to body (MenuList, ChoiceMenu, zoom lists; all preventDefault what they take) are not used again. The other preventDefault callers in keydown handlers under ui/src were checked: all of them also stop the key (stopPropagation). docs/architecture.md 25-8 says so now. Tests: ui/tests/menu.test.ts "UI-SHELL-004: ↑ ↓ ← → used in the list do not move the selected notes of the piano roll" (MenuBar mounted in a child of body, piano roll open with a note selected); ui/tests/keys.front.test.ts "UI-SHELL-004: a key a part already took is not used again".

UI-TEXT-001mediumparityfixedEnglish vocal recording status says monitor on while monitoring is off

docs/bugs/reports/UI-TEXT-001-english-vocal-recording-status-says-monitor-on-w.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/StatusLine.svelte:259
ui/src/components/StatusLine.svelte:264
ui/src/text/en.ts:1154 (msg.recordingVocal)
ui/src/text/en.ts:1567 (msg.recordingVocals)
ui/src/text/en.ts:1295 (meter.monitorOn)

What happens

With the page in English, recording a vocal track while its monitor is off still shows "● Recording · 6 Vocal (In 1) · monitor on · Space to stop" (and the same for two or more vocal tracks). The status line says the monitor is on when it is off. In Japanese the part 「モニター オン ・ 」 is taken out as meant.

What should happen

The part "monitor on · " is left out when monitoring is off, as the comment at StatusLine.svelte:244 says (「モニター オン ・ 」 is left out when monitoring is off) and as the Japanese page does (spec 6 "Recording vocals", "Recording 2 or more vocal tracks").

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Language English; add a vocal track, turn its 「モニター」 off, press record. recordingVocalsParts(keys, vocals, monitor=false) builds monitorOn = "Monitor on · " and calls text.replace("Monitor on · ", "") on "… · monitor on · …": no match, nothing is removed.

Cause

The code finds the piece to remove by the text of another key: t("meter.monitorOn") + STATUS_JOIN. In Japanese both say 「モニター オン」; in English meter.monitorOn is "Monitor on" (capital M, a label) while the sentences have "monitor on" (small m, mid-sentence). String.replace is case-sensitive.

Suggested fix

Smallest: in en.ts make the piece inside msg.recordingVocal and msg.recordingVocals match what the code removes, or (better, not tied to letter case) give the sentence without the monitor part its own way: for example compare case-insensitively, or have the code remove the text between the hole before it and STATUS_JOIN by a separate key used only for this (meter.monitorOnInline: 「モニター オン」 / "monitor on"). A new key needs English only (the Japanese is the existing 「モニター オン」); no protocol change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). StatusLine.svelte:259-264 removes t("meter.monitorOn") + STATUS_JOIN from the sentence with String.replace; in English that is "Monitor on · " (en.ts:1295), while msg.recordingVocal / msg.recordingVocals have "monitor on · " (en.ts:1154, 1567). The match is case-sensitive, so nothing is removed and the status line says "monitor on" with the monitor off. Japanese has 「モニター オン」 in both. Medium is right: the line says something false about what the person hears.

Fix plan (no new wording): in ui/src/components/StatusLine.svelte do not find the piece by another key's text. Split the sentence on its own joints instead: the text part that follows the last hole before {stopKey} is 「…・ モニター オン ・ 止めるには 」 / " · monitor on · " + the lead of the next piece; remove the segment between two STATUS_JOINs that holds no hole (the monitor piece is the only such segment in these two texts in both tables). If the lead prefers the plain way: compare without case (toLowerCase on both) — one line, but still tied to two keys saying the same words. Add a test in ui/tests/language.test.ts: English, monitor off → no "monitor on".

Fix

ui/src/components/StatusLine.svelte: the monitor piece is no longer found by the text of meter.monitorOn. With the monitor off, withoutMonitorPiece splits each text part of the sentence on the joint mark (STATUS_JOIN without its spaces, as Japanese writes 「）・ 」) and drops the piece between two marks (the only piece with no hole on either side, in both tables). Japanese output is unchanged. Test: ui/tests/round7.vocal.test.ts "English: with monitor off, 「monitor on · 」 is removed too, and the inputs are in English brackets".

AI-001lowbugfixedToggle tools (set_mute, set_solo, bypass_effect, set_loop_on) read, then send a toggle, so two calls at once flip back

docs/bugs/reports/AI-001-toggle-tools-set-mute-set-solo-bypass-effect-set.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/mixer.rs:450-472 (set_mute: reads muted, then sends Command::ToggleMute / ToggleReturnMute)
crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/mixer.rs:489-494 (set_solo: reads soloed, then sends ToggleSolo)
crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/mixer.rs:597-609 (bypass_effect: reads bypassed, then sends ToggleBypass)
crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/timing.rs:293-300 (set_loop_on: reads loop_on, then sends ToggleLoop)

What happens

These four tools take the wanted state (on: true/false) but change the song with a toggle intent. The tool reads the song, compares, and when it differs sends the toggle. Between the read and the moment the core runs the toggle, anything else that flips the same switch makes the toggle go the wrong way. Two set_mute {track: 2, on: true} calls that run at the same time both read "not muted" and both send ToggleMute: the track ends unmuted, and both answers say changed: true ("Done"). The same happens when the person presses M (or S, the bypass button, L) in that window. The tools are marked idempotent_hint = true, and an AI app may send the same call again or several calls at once.

What should happen

set_mute {on: true} leaves the track muted, whatever ran at the same time; a second call is "no change" (docs/development/ai-tools.md, "No change": "Tools that turn a value into a toggle (set_mute, set_solo, bypass_effect, set_loop_on …) answer the same when it is so already"), and the tool's idempotent_hint says so.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. One connection: send two tools/call requests set_mute {"track": 1, "on": true} without waiting for the first answer (rmcp 3.11 service.rs runs every request of a connection as its own task, spawn_service_task, so both read the song before either toggle reaches the core). Or two AI apps connected at once (up to 8 connections). Or the person presses M on that track while the call is between its read and its ask.

Cause

The state is read on the AI thread (self.read()) and the change is a toggle that the core applies later on its own thread; there is no check that the state is still the one read. Outside a batch nothing holds the person's intents or other connections' asks between the read and the toggle.

Suggested fix

Let the core decide with the wanted value: an AI ask such as AiAsk::SetToggle { what, on } (or the existing screen command path with a guard) that, on the core thread, does nothing (Unchanged) when the switch is already on and otherwise sends the toggle. No protocol change for the screen (AiAsk is core-internal); no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). set_mute, set_solo, bypass_effect and set_loop_on each read the song on the AI side (self.read()), compare with on, and then send a plain toggle through AiAsk::Screen(Command::Toggle…). The core's dry run for these (crates/meloet-core/src/play/ai.rs:279-341) does not know the wanted value, so nothing checks that the switch is still as read when the toggle runs. Two calls of the same tool at once, or the person's M / S / L between the read and the ask, flip it the wrong way, and both answers say changed: true. The tools carry idempotent_hint = true. Severity low stands: it needs two asks inside a few milliseconds.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: let the core thread decide. crates/meloet-core/src/ai.rs: an ask that carries the wanted value with the toggle (for example AiAsk::ScreenWhen { command, on: bool }, core-internal, not part of the screen's contract); crates/meloet-core/src/play/ai.rs / state/ai.rs: on the core thread read the switch the command would flip (track or return mute, solo, bypass of the insert, loop_on) and answer AiRefusal::Unchanged when it is on already, else run the toggle as now. crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/mixer.rs and tools/timing.rs send it and keep their early "no change" for the common case. Test in crates/meloet-ai/tests/link.rs: the ask with on: true twice in a row leaves the track muted and the second is "no change".

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/ai.rs: a core-internal ask AiAsk::ScreenWhen { command, on } (a toggle for the state on). crates/meloet-core/src/state/ai.rs (ai_write): on the core thread it answers AiRefusal::Unchanged when the switch is on already, else it runs the toggle as AiAsk::Screen does. crates/meloet-core/src/play/ai.rs: Play::ai_switch reads the switch a toggle would flip (track mute, return mute, solo, bypass, loop_on). crates/meloet-ai/src/tools.rs (screen_when), tools/mixer.rs (set_mute, set_solo, bypass_effect) and tools/timing.rs (set_loop_on) send it and keep their early "no change". No protocol change. Docs: docs/development/ai-tools.md, docs/architecture.md (24-16).

Test: a_toggle_for_a_state_ends_in_that_state in crates/meloet-ai/tests/link.rs (each of the four toggles asked twice for on, as two calls that both read before either reached meloet: the first flips it, the second is Unchanged, the state ends as asked, for on true and false).

AI-004lowui-uxfixedget_automation gives at most 2000 points of a lane with no paging; later points cannot be seen or named

docs/bugs/reports/AI-004-get-automation-gives-at-most-2000-points-of-a-la.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/automation.rs:217 (lane.points.iter().enumerate().take(MAX_NOTES))
crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/automation.rs:108-111 (TrackArgs: no offset / limit)

What happens

get_automation gives only the first 2000 points of each lane, with total beside them, and has no offset or limit to get the rest. move_automation and delete_automation take the points by their index from get_automation, so a point at index 2000 or later can be neither seen nor named with knowledge of where it is. A lane has no limit on its points (meloet_model::automation::put_points has none), and a lane written while playing (「● 書く」) or by write_automation calls one after another can pass 2000.

What should happen

Every point can be read: "Long lists come in pages: limit, offset, and total, count, next_offset" (docs/development/ai-tools.md, "How every tool answers"; the tool INSTRUCTIONS say the same). The sibling get_notes has first and max for the same reason.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Write 2001 or more points into one lane (two write_automation calls of 2000 points at different ticks), then call get_automation: total is over 2000 but the last points are missing and there is no way to ask for them.

Cause

The cap of 2000 (MAX_NOTES) was put on the list without paging arguments.

Suggested fix

Add optional offset (or first) and limit (default and most 2000) to get_automation, applied per lane or to one lane chosen by target, and give next_offset when points are left. Indexes stay the indexes in the lane. No protocol change; docs/development/ai-tools.md gains the two arguments.

Verification

Confirmed by reading. get_automation takes only track (TrackArgs) and gives lane.points…take(MAX_NOTES) (2000) with total; there is no way to read the points after the first 2000 of a lane, while move_automation and delete_automation name points by the index this tool gives. I kept it as a fault and not a wish because the sibling has the missing half: get_notes takes first and max for the same cap (tools.rs:705-710), and the tool instructions and docs/development/ai-tools.md say "Long lists come in pages". A lane has no limit on its points. Severity low stands (a lane of more than 2000 points is rare).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/automation.rs: get_automation gets its own arguments (track, optional first and max as get_notes names them, default and most 2000), applied to every lane's points; each lane keeps total and gains next_first (null when none are left); indexes stay the indexes in the lane. docs/development/ai-tools.md names the two arguments. Test in crates/meloet-ai/tests/: a lane of 2001 points, the second page holds index 2000.

Fix

crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/automation.rs: get_automation takes optional first and max (default and most 2000), as get_notes names them, applied to every lane's points; each lane answers first and next_first (null when none are left); indexes stay the indexes in the lane. The tool's description names them. docs/development/ai-tools.md: a get_automation section.

Test: get_automation_gives_every_point_in_pages in crates/meloet-ai/tests/link.rs (a lane of 2001 points: the first page ends with next_first 2000, the second page holds index 2000).

CORE-AUDIO-002lowbugfixedA voice file starts half a quarter note before the roll, not half a beat as the keys do: in 6/8 and 2/2 the voice and key windows differ

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-002-a-voice-file-starts-half-a-quarter-note-before-t.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs:407-411 (vocal_mark: half_beat_us = 30e6 / map.tempo_at(tick))
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:2712 (the key rule it should match: t.record_from - t.record_beat / 2, record_beat = Meter::ticks_per_beat() at record_from, engine.rs:1130-1132)

What happens

The disk writer is told to start the voice files half_beat_us before the moment the song rolls. tempo_at is quarter notes per minute (docs/architecture.md 22-2), so 30e6 / tempo µs is always half a quarter note (960 ticks), whatever the time signature.

The keys keep what was played in the last half beat of the count-in, where a beat is the note of the lower number (Meter::ticks_per_beat = 7680 / den): 960 ticks in x/4, but 480 ticks in x/8 and 1920 ticks in x/2.

  • In 2/2 (and other x/2) the voice file starts only an eighth note before the roll, while the keys keep a quarter note: a sung pickup that starts between a quarter and an eighth note early is not in the file, so pulling the clip's left edge out shows silence there.
  • In 6/8, 3/8, x/16 the voice file keeps twice (four times) as much as the keys.

The comment above the line says "Half a beat before the song rolls, as the keys keep."

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 22-6: "the file is written from the head of the last half beat of the count-in (same length as the key rule of 12)", and 22-7 / 22-16 say the same ("from the count-in's last half beat"). The key rule is half of Meter::ticks_per_beat() (engine.rs:2712).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Set the time signature to 2/2 at tempo 120 (a quarter note is 0.5 s).
  2. Arm a vocal track, record with the count-in, and start singing 0.4 s before the first bar (between an eighth and a quarter note early).
  3. Stop, pull the clip's left edge out to before the start: the first part of the pickup is not there (the file begins 0.25 s before the roll). A key played at the same moment is kept.

Cause

The half beat is computed from the tempo alone. When tempo became quarter notes per minute in every time signature (22-2), this line kept meaning "half a quarter note" and was not tied to the meter at the record start, as the engine's record_beat is.

Suggested fix

Take the half beat from the meter at the roll tick, as the engine does: let beat = map.meter_at(tick.max(0.0) as u64).meter.ticks_per_beat() as f64; then half_beat_us = beat / 2.0 / TICKS_PER_QUARTER as f64 * 60e6 / map.tempo_at(tick).max(1.0). No protocol, file or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). vocal_mark (service/input.rs:407-411): half_beat_us = 30e6 / map.tempo_at(tick); the tempo is quarter notes a minute whatever the meter, so this is always half a quarter note. The keys' rule in the engine is record_from - record_beat / 2 with record_beat = Meter::ticks_per_beat() = TICKS_PER_QUARTER * 4 / den (engine.rs:1130-1132, 2712). Architecture 22-6: "the same length as the key rule". In x/2 the voice file starts half as early as the keys are kept, in x/8 twice as early. Severity low stands (only meters whose lower number is not 4, and only the part before the clip's start).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs vocal_mark: take the beat from the meter at the roll tick: let beat = map.meter_at(tick.max(0.0) as u64).meter.ticks_per_beat() as f64; let half_beat_us = beat / 2.0 / TICKS_PER_QUARTER as f64 * 60e6 / map.tempo_at(tick).max(1.0);. A unit test of the small calculation (pull it into a function): 4/4, 2/2 and 6/8 at one tempo.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/service/input.rs: vocal_mark takes the half beat from the new half_beat_us(map, tick): half of Meter::ticks_per_beat() of the meter at the roll tick, at the tempo there (the engine's key rule). Only that line of vocal_mark changed. Test: service::input::tests::the_files_begin_half_a_beat_of_the_meter_before_the_roll (4/4, 2/2 and 6/8 at 120; it fails without the fix).

CORE-AUDIO-005lowui-uxfixedAn audio file that could not be copied in (full disk, no work place) is reported as a kind meloet does not read

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-005-an-audio-file-that-could-not-be-copied-in-full-d.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs:392-393 (Work::bring: an error of bring_in becomes Unread::NotAudio)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs:418-420, 431-441 (Work::found: no work place, or a copy that fails, becomes Finding::Unreadable)
crates/meloet-core/src/state/audio.rs:245, 291 (Unreadable / NotAudio → StatusMessage::AudioUnreadable)

What happens

Bringing in a file first checks what it is (decode::facts), then copies it into the work place (AudioStore::bring_in). When the copy fails (the disk is full, the work place cannot be written, there is no state folder), the keeper answers Brought::Unreadable(name, Unread::NotAudio), and the status line says 「「声.wav」は読めません（読めるのは wav ・ mp3 ・ flac ・ ogg）。」 about a wav file that reads well. 「さがす…」 does the same when its copy fails (Finding::Unreadable → the same message). The person is told to use another file kind, which does not help; the real cause (no room, no right to write) is only on stderr.

What should happen

The message must match the cause (docs/bugs brief: no wrong or misleading wording). facts already tells "not a kind meloet reads" (NotAudio), "broken" (Broken) and "too many channels" apart; a failure to write is none of these. Spec 6 has a message that names the disk for a song that cannot be saved (「ここには保存できませんでした（書きこむ権利がないか、空きが足りません）。…」), but none for audio that cannot be brought in.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Fill the disk that holds ~/.local/state/meloet (or make work/音 unwritable).
  2. Drag a good wav file onto the arrange.
  3. The status line says the file cannot be read and lists wav as a kind that reads.

Cause

Work::bring maps every error of store.bring_in to Unread::NotAudio, and Work::found maps a failed copy to Finding::Unreadable; Unread has no case for "could not be copied".

Suggested fix

Add a case to Unread (and Finding) for "could not be written into the work place" and show its own message. Needs new wording (the lead or the owner decides) and a new StatusMessage variant (a protocol addition). A smaller step with no new wording is not possible, since every existing message names a different cause.

Verification

Real, checked by reading (no test run). Work::bring (audiofiles/keeper.rs:389-393) first reads the file's facts, then maps every error of store.bring_in (the copy into the work place) to Unread::NotAudio; Work::found (keeper.rs:414-442) gives Finding::Unreadable when there is no work place or the copy fails. state/audio.rs:245, 291 turn both into StatusMessage::AudioUnreadable: 「「声.wav」は読めません（読めるのは wav ・ mp3 ・ flac ・ ogg）。」 for a file that reads well.

Looked for an existing message: AudioUnreadable, AudioBroken, AudioTooManyChannels, AudioFoundMismatch each name another cause; ClipWorkFailed (「{count} つのクリップは、音のファイルを読めないか書けないので、そのままにしました。」) is about clips that are already in the song; msg.bounceFailedWrite and msg.exportFailedWrite are about their own jobs. None says "the file could not be copied in".

Why needs-lead: new wording and a new StatusMessage variant (protocol addition), plus a new case in Unread and Finding. Choices:

  1. A new variant (for example AudioNotCopied { file }) with the owner's text, for example 「「声.wav」を入れられませんでした。作業の場所に書けません。」 (the second sentence is the one of msg.bounceFailedWrite). Recommended, and best decided together with CORE-AUDIO-003 and -004, which need the same kind of sentence.
  2. Leave it and list it in docs/progress.md.

Severity low stands (a full disk or a work place that cannot be written).

Fix

Protocol 38 (the lead's decision): StatusMessage::AudioNotCopied { file }. Unread::NotCopied (crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/decode.rs) and Finding::NotCopied (audiofiles/keeper.rs): Work::bring gives it when bring_in fails, Work::found when there is no work place or the copy fails; crates/meloet-core/src/state/audio.rs turns both into AudioNotCopied. Text msg.audioNotCopied (builder:2026-11-25), StatusLine.svelte. Tests: audiofiles::tests::a_file_that_cannot_be_copied_in_says_so, a new row in state::audio::tests::files_that_do_not_read_bring_nothing_in, a new step in state::audio::tests::a_missing_file_is_said_looked_for_and_taken_out, tests38::the_messages, the row of 「知らせ」 in ui/tests/connect-components.test.ts.

CORE-AUDIO-006lowbugfixedA long vocal track name makes the recorded file name longer than the file system allows, and the voice is not recorded

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-006-a-long-vocal-track-name-makes-the-recorded-file.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs:68-77 (file_name: the whole track name goes into the file name, with no limit on its length)
crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs:101 (WavFile::create fails on such a name)
crates/meloet-model/src/edit.rs:387-405 (rename_track: a track name has no length limit; the screen's name field has no maxlength either)

What happens

A recorded file is named <track name> <audio id>.wav. Track names have no length limit, and a file name on Linux file systems (ext4, btrfs, xfs) is at most 255 bytes; a Japanese character takes 3 bytes in UTF-8. With a vocal track name of about 82 Japanese characters or more (about 245 ASCII characters), the name is too long: WavFile::create fails with "File name too long", start_vocals answers VocalsNotStarted, and the recording goes on without the voice (and without a word, CORE-AUDIO-003).
