The same function names the files of 「逆にする」 (play/reverse.rs:43) and 「音のクリップにする」 (play/bounce.rs:68-72, with a suffix added), which then fail too.

What should happen

A track name the song accepts must not keep its track from being recorded. The other file names meloet makes from what a person typed are made safe for the file system: vocalrec::file_name already takes out /, \, NUL and control characters and handles an empty name (vocalrec.rs:70-75), and songfile::folder_name does the same for a song's folder; only the length is not handled.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Rename a vocal track to a name of 90 Japanese characters (by typing it, or with the AI link's rename).
  2. Arm it and record: the count-in and the recording run, no waveform grows, and no take is made for the voice.

Cause

file_name joins the cleaned track name, a space, the id and .wav without looking at the byte length; start_vocals adds « (n)» for a taken name, which makes it longer still.

Suggested fix

In file_name, cut the cleaned track name at a character boundary so that the whole name, with room for « (99)» or « 99» (the suffixes start_vocals, bring_in and put_in_place add), stays well under 255 bytes (for example at most 200 bytes for the track-name part). No protocol, file-shape or wording change; names of existing files are not touched.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). vocalrec::file_name (vocalrec.rs:68-77) puts the whole cleaned track name into <name> <id>.wav; rename_track only trims a name. Past about 82 Japanese characters the file name is over 255 bytes, WavFile::create fails, start_vocals answers VocalsNotStarted, and the voice is not recorded (silently, CORE-AUDIO-003). The same function names the files of 「逆にする」 and 「音のクリップにする」. The same fault for exported files is CORE-EXPORT-003. Severity low stands (a very long track name).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs file_name: cut the cleaned name at a character boundary to at most 200 bytes (then trim spaces and dots again) before the id and .wav are added; that leaves room for the « (n)» and suffixes added later. Share the cut with export/job.rs free_name (one small helper) when CORE-EXPORT-003 is fixed. Test next to the file_name tests: 100 Japanese characters give a name of at most 255 bytes that ends in <id>.wav.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/vocalrec.rs file_name: the cleaned track name is cut with crate::export::job::cut_name (200 bytes, on a character boundary, no space or dot left at its end) before the id and .wav; the same function names the files of 「逆にする」 and 「音のクリップにする」. cut_name stays where it is (one function); nothing changed in meloet-model. Test: vocalrec::tests::a_long_track_name_is_cut_to_fit_a_file_name (it fails without the fix).

CORE-AUDIO-007lowbugfixedAfter 「さがす…」 takes a file with other contents, the clip keeps playing the old decoded copy and showing the old waveform

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-AUDIO-007-after-takes-a-file-with-other-contents-the-clip.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs:414-443 (Work::found: a file of the same rate and channels is copied in under the source's name, whatever it holds; nothing kept for the old contents is let go)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles.rs:246-262 (cached / playable: the decoded copy audio/<uid>-<rate>.wav is taken as ready when it opens at the rate)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles.rs:429-439 (PcmStore::open: the same copy is what plays)
crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs:465-467 (watch: the peaks file is kept when its frame count equals source.frames)

What happens

「さがす…」 accepts a file with another SHA-256 when its rate and channel count match (docs/architecture.md 22-18 A2, B-94-30), and copies it into the work place under the missing source's name. The source keeps its uid, and everything meloet made from the old file is keyed by that uid and is still in the cache (a source in the table keeps its cache "used"):

  • If the file is not a plain wav at the engine's rate (an mp3, a flac, an ogg, or a wav at another rate), playable and PcmStore::open find the old decoded copy audio/<uid>-<rate>.wav and use it. The clip plays the old sound, not the file that was found, and nothing decodes the found file.
  • The old peaks file peaks/<uid>.peaks says frames == source.frames (the table's old count), so watch does not make peaks again: the waveform is the old file's, also for a plain wav that does play the new contents.

On a save the found file goes into 音/; on another PC, or after the cache is emptied, the same song then plays the found file. So the same song sounds different by PC.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 22-18 A2: "The found file goes to the work place and moves to 音/ on the next save" — that file is the song's audio from then on, and what plays and what is drawn must come from it. 22-8: the decoded copy "can be rebuilt"; 22-9: "Missing cache files are rebuilt". The cache is a copy of the file, never a second source of truth.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Bring an mp3 into a song and play it once (the keeper decodes it into ~/.cache/meloet/audio/<uid>-48000.wav and writes its peaks). Save.
  2. Close meloet. In another program change the mp3 (for example make it quieter, or cut a part out of its middle) and save it under another name at the same rate and channel count; move the original away.
  3. Open the song: the clip is missing. Use 「さがす…」 and pick the changed file: the status line says it was found, but the clip plays and draws the old contents.

Cause

Work::found only copies the file and calls file_arrived. The caches are looked up by uid alone (cache_path(uid, rate), peaks_path(uid)), and the uid of brought-in audio is the hash of the old contents, so the old copies stay valid for the new file.

Suggested fix

In Work::found, when the found file's SHA-256 differs from source.sha256 (or source.sha256 is None), remove the decoded copies of that uid (audio/<uid>-*.wav) and its peaks file before file_arrived, so watch makes them again from the found file; when the SHA-256 is the same, keep them as now. No protocol, file-shape or wording change. (Giving the found source a new uid would also work but changes the song's table.)

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Work::found (audiofiles/keeper.rs:414-443) copies a file of the same rate and channel count into the work place under the missing source's name and calls file_arrived; nothing made from the old contents is let go. AudioStore::playable and PcmStore::open (audiofiles.rs:251-262, 429-439) take the decoded copy audio/<uid>-<rate>.wav whenever the found file is not itself a wav at the engine's rate, and watch (keeper.rs:465-467) keeps the peaks file while its frame count equals source.frames. The uid does not change, so the old decoded copy plays and the old waveform is drawn. Architecture 22-18 A2 accepts a file with another SHA-256 on purpose ("maybe the same song saved again by another app"), and 22-8 / 22-9 call the cache a copy that can be made again. Severity low stands (only after 「さがす…」 with a file of other contents, and for the decoded copy only when the file is not a wav at the engine's rate).

Corrections: none. I did not check when the cache of a missing source is cleaned; if it is already gone, only the peaks half is left.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs Work::found: after the copy, when the found file's SHA-256 is not source.sha256 (or there is none), remove the decoded copies of that uid and its peaks file (a small method on AudioStore, which is the one place that knows the cache paths) and take the uid out of peaks_made, before file_arrived. Test in audiofiles/tests.rs: a cached copy and peaks for a uid, found with a file of other bytes → both are gone and playable says NeedsCache.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles/keeper.rs Work::found: after the copy, when the found file's SHA-256 is not source.sha256 (or there is none), the new AudioStore::drop_cache (crates/meloet-core/src/audiofiles.rs) deletes the decoded copies of the uid at every rate and its peaks file, and the uid leaves peaks_made; watch then makes them again from the found file. The same bytes keep them. Test: audiofiles::tests::a_found_file_with_other_contents_is_made_ready_again_from_itself (it fails without the fix).

CORE-EXPORT-003lowbugfixedPer-track export fails as a whole when a track name is longer than a file name can be

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-EXPORT-003-per-track-export-fails-as-a-whole-when-a-track-n.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/export/job.rs:299-313 (free_name)
crates/meloet-core/src/export/job.rs:259-264, 286 (the name joined to the folder, then move_to)
crates/meloet-core/src/export/midi.rs:342-348 (MIDI 「トラックごと」 uses the same free_name)

What happens

「トラックごと」 names each file after its track with free_name, which takes out /, \, NUL and control characters but does not limit the length. A track name has no length limit (meloet_model::edit::rename_track only trims it). On Linux file systems a file name is at most 255 bytes; a Japanese character is 3 bytes in UTF-8, so a track name of about 84 Japanese characters or more makes move_to (audio) or write_beside (MIDI) fail with ENAMETOOLONG. The whole export then ends as 「書き出せませんでした」 (ExportFailure::CannotWrite, "the disk is full, or the place does not take it"), the files already moved are taken away again, and nothing tells the person that one track's name is the reason.

What should happen

free_name says it gives <name>.<ending> "with what a file name cannot hold taken out"; a name too long for a file is such a thing. Every track should get a file (spec 3 "Per track makes one file for each track, named after the track").

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Rename a track to a name of 90 Japanese characters.
  2. 「書き出し…」, 「トラックごと」, any folder, 「書き出す」.
  3. The run ends with the failure 「書き出せませんでした」; no file is left.

Cause

No byte length limit in free_name; the format's ending and the " 2" number are added after the name.

Suggested fix

In free_name, cut clean at a character boundary so that clean, the number part and .<ending> together stay within 255 bytes (for example cut clean to 200 bytes before adding the number and the ending). Both the audio and the MIDI 「トラックごと」 use it. No protocol, wording or looks change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). free_name (export/job.rs:299-313) takes out /, \, NUL and control characters and has no limit on the length; rename_track only trims a name. A track name of more than about 84 Japanese characters gives a file name over 255 bytes, move_to (or the MIDI writer, export/midi.rs:342-348) fails, and the whole per-track export ends as ExportFailure::CannotWrite. Severity low stands (a very long track name).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/export/job.rs free_name: cut clean at a character boundary so that the name with the number and .<ending> stays within 255 bytes (cut clean to at most 200 bytes, then trim spaces and dots again). Test next to the free_name tests: 100 Japanese characters give a name of at most 255 bytes that ends in the ending, and two such tracks get different names.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/export/job.rs: a shared helper cut_name(name: &str) -> &str with NAME_BYTES = 200 (cut on a character boundary, no space or dot left at the end); free_name cuts the cleaned track name with it before the " 2" number and the ending are added, so the name stays within 255 bytes and names still differ. Audio and MIDI 「トラックごと」 (and the AI link's file list, state/ai.rs) all use free_name.

Test: export::job::tests::a_track_name_too_long_for_a_file_name_is_cut_between_characters (export/job/tests.rs).

CORE-FILES-001lowbugfixedA song file's notes and control points are read with any tick; Note::end then overflows

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-FILES-001-a-song-file-s-notes-and-control-points-are-read.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1909 (to_take: a note is checked for length >= 1, key and velocity only; start and length are any u64)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1922-1931 (a control point's tick is any u64)
crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1683 (only the take's length is held to MAX_TICK, not what is inside it)
crates/meloet-model/src/song.rs:58-60 (Note::end is self.start + self.length)
crates/meloet-model/src/playback.rs:46,60 (at(tick) = zero + tick as i64, at(n.end()))

What happens

A song file whose take holds a note such as [1, 18446744073709551615, 60, 100], or [0, 9223372036854775808, 60, 100], opens without a word. When the song's events are made for the engine (playback::track_events, after every open), n.end() overflows u64 in the first case and zero + tick as i64 overflows i64 in the second. A debug build (the one the owner runs from VS Code) panics on the core thread. A release build wraps: the note-off lands at a negative tick, and .min(end) as u64 turns it into a huge tick, so the note never ends (or ends before it starts). Control points with ticks above i64::MAX wrap the same way in at(c.tick) and land before the clip.

What should happen

A song that breaks a rule the model keeps does not read (docs/architecture.md 14, "Does not read: ... a break of a model rule"; the brief: bad or hostile JSON must not panic). The model's own edits never make such notes: meloet-model/src/notes.rs:190 refuses a note starting at or past MAX_TICK, and notes.rs:211 caps a length to MAX_TICK - start. songfile.rs already refuses a clip end, a clip offset, a marker, a tempo point and an automation point past MAX_TICK; notes and control points are the gap.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Save any song with one keys clip, edit the .meloet file by hand so that one note of the take the clip shows is [1, 18446744073709551615, 60, 100], and open it in a debug build: the core thread panics with "attempt to add with overflow" in Note::end. With [0, 9223372036854775808, 60, 100] the panic is in playback.rs:46 (zero + tick as i64). In a release build the second note sounds until play stops.

Cause

to_take (songfile.rs:1896-1944) checks the order of the notes and control points, the key, the velocity and a length of at least 1, but not that start, start + length and a control point's tick stay within MAX_TICK (i64::MAX / 2). Every later use assumes they do: Note::end adds without a check, and playback.rs casts ticks to i64.

Suggested fix

In to_take, refuse the take (return None, so the song does not read as with every other broken rule) when a note has start >= MAX_TICK or start.checked_add(length) is None or above MAX_TICK, and when a control point's tick > MAX_TICK. No protocol or file-shape change; a song meloet wrote never has such values, so no real song stops reading.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_core_files_001): a song with one clip, encoded, the note [37,200,60,90] changed in the JSON to [37,18446744073709551615,60,90]; songfile::decode gives Ok, and meloet_model::playback::track_events then panics at crates/meloet-model/src/song.rs:59 ("attempt to add with overflow", debug build). Release builds wrap, as the report says (read, not run). Severity low stands: only a file changed by hand or damaged has such a note; meloet never writes one.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs to_take: a note is taken only when start < MAX_TICK and start.checked_add(length) is Some(end) with end <= MAX_TICK; a control point only when tick <= MAX_TICK; else the take is None and the song does not read, as for the other broken rules. Tests in songfile/tests.rs (both notes of the report and a control point past MAX_TICK give Err(Broken)).

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs to_take: a take with a note whose start + length overflows or is above MAX_TICK, or with a control point above MAX_TICK, does not read (ReadError::Broken), as the other broken rules. docs/architecture.md 14 says so.

Test: songfile::tests::ticks_past_max_tick_do_not_read (meloet-core; both notes of the report, a note ending past MAX_TICK and a control point past it are broken; the same values at MAX_TICK read and track_events does not panic).

CORE-FILES-002lowbugfixedA song file's loop range is read past MAX_TICK, which set_loop refuses

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-FILES-002-a-song-file-s-loop-range-is-read-past-max-tick-w.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:1823-1826 (loop_range: Some(TickRange::new(r.start, r.end)?), no MAX_TICK check)
crates/meloet-model/src/timeline.rs:306-308 (set_loop refuses range.end() > MAX_TICK)
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:437 (ceil: r.end() as i64)

What happens

A song file with "loop": {"start": 0, "end": 18446744073709551615} opens. The loop's end is then larger than any tick the model allows. Where the core takes it as i64 it wraps: play.rs:437 gives a ceiling of -1 for an end of 2^63 or more; then, while recording with the loop on, press (play.rs:470) puts every live note at tick 0 (.min(ceil - 1).max(0)) and release (play.rs:462) gives it length 1, and play.rs:2162 sends the raw u64 to the screen, where a JS number cannot hold it exactly. The engine is told the same end (play.rs:1031-1037, EngineCommand::SetLoop) and turns it into frames of the tempo map.

What should happen

The loop range keeps the rule set_loop keeps: its end is at most MAX_TICK (meloet-model/src/timeline.rs:306). A song that breaks a rule of the model does not read (docs/architecture.md 14). The decoder already holds clip ends, markers, tempo points and automation points to MAX_TICK; the loop range is the one tick field it lets through.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Edit a saved song's "loop" to {"start": 0, "end": 18446744073709551615}, open it, turn the loop on, record and play a few keys: the live notes shown while recording sit at tick 0 with length 1.

Cause

to_song builds the loop with TickRange::new(r.start, r.end), which only checks end > start. The MAX_TICK test that timeline::set_loop does is missing here.

Suggested fix

In to_song, also require r.end <= MAX_TICK (TickRange::new(r.start, r.end).filter(|_| r.end <= MAX_TICK)?, as for a clip at songfile.rs:1665). No protocol or file-shape change; meloet never writes such a loop.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-core --features dev-hooks --lib verify_core_files_002): a song with a loop, encoded, the loop's "end" changed in the JSON to 18446744073709551615; songfile::decode gives Ok with loop_range: Some(TickRange { start: 0, end: 18446744073709551615 }). timeline::set_loop refuses an end past MAX_TICK, and play.rs:437 casts the end to i64. What the recording and the engine then do with it I read only. Severity low stands (a file changed by hand).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs to_song: Some(r) => Some(TickRange::new(r.start, r.end).filter(|_| r.end <= MAX_TICK)?). Test in songfile/tests.rs.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs to_song: a loop whose end is above MAX_TICK does not read, as timeline::set_loop refuses it. docs/architecture.md 14 says so.

Test: songfile::tests::ticks_past_max_tick_do_not_read (meloet-core; loop ends u64::MAX and MAX_TICK + 1 are broken, MAX_TICK reads).

CORE-PLAY-A-003lowui-uxfixedHistory rows of 「アルペジオを変える」 and 「音のつまみを変える」 show the track name twice, against spec 3 (94-6)

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-A-003-history-rows-of-and-show-the-track-name-twice-ag.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • crates/meloet-core/src/play/history.rs:1136-1142 (StepKind::SetArp: what StepWhat::Track { track }, place on_track(track))
  • crates/meloet-core/src/play/history.rs:1143-1160 (StepKind::SetSoundKnob: what StepWhat::SoundKnob { track, .. }, place on_track(track))
  • ui/src/components/history/stepText.ts:199-203 (rowWhatText drops the track only for soundReplaced)
  • ui/src/bridge/fakeWave3.ts:27 (the fake copies the same row)

What happens

In the history band, a 「アルペジオを変える」 row reads 「「アルペジオを変える」・ 1 ピアノ」 with 「1 ピアノ」 again in the right column, and a 「音のつまみを変える」 row reads 「「音のつまみを変える」・ 1 ピアノ ・ 明るさ 50 → 25 %」 with 「1 ピアノ」 again in the right column.

What should happen

Spec 3 (94-6, 3-bands.md line 272): "for steps whose row shows the track name in the right column, 「（何を）」 does not include the track name." Spec 7 readings line 396 applies it to 「音源を入れかえる」 too (only the window row keeps its words twice, by the owner's choice). The sibling soundReplaced row already follows it: rowWhatText writes only 「グランドピアノ → オルガン」 when the place is the track.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Change the arpeggio of track 1 (「アルペジオ ▾」), or drag a knob of a built-in sound; open the history band and look at the newest row.

Cause

Both step kinds put the track into 「（何を）」 and also name the track as the place. rowWhatText (stepText.ts 201) removes the track only for what.kind === "soundReplaced"; track (SetArp) and soundKnob pass through stepWhatText with the track label.

Suggested fix

Screen side, as soundReplaced is done: in rowWhatText, when row.place?.kind === "track" and row.what.kind === "track" (the same track), give history.rowBare; for soundKnob, a form without the track (「明るさ 50 → 25 %」). The status line can keep the track (it has no right column). The soundKnob form without the track needs a wording key in ja.ts / en.ts (a lead decision, or reuse of an existing key); the SetArp half needs none. Alternatively Rust gives StepWhat::None for SetArp, but then the undo status line loses the track name too.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). play/history.rs:1136-1160 gives StepWhat::Track (SetArp) and StepWhat::SoundKnob with the place on_track(track); rowWhatText (ui/src/components/history/stepText.ts:199-203) drops the track only for soundReplaced, so both rows name the track in 「（何を）」 and again in the right column. Spec 3 (94-6): "for steps whose row shows the track name in the right column, 「（何を）」 does not include the track name"; 7-readings.md line 396 keeps the name only for rows whose right column is 「アレンジ」 or 「ミキサー」. Severity low stands.

On the wording: no new text key is needed, so this is confirmed, not needs-lead. 「アルペジオを変える」 with the track as its place becomes the bare row (history.rowBare). The knob row without the track is 「明るさ 50 → 25 %」, which existing keys already say: history.changeAt ({place} {from} → {to}, spec:3) with the knob's name as place, from the number, and to through param.value.percent ({value} %, spec:3). Both keys exist in ja.ts and en.ts.

Fix plan (screen only): ui/src/components/history/stepText.ts rowWhatText: when row.place?.kind === "track": for what.kind === "track" return null (bare row); for what.kind === "soundKnob" return the history.changeAt form above. The status line keeps stepWhatText with the track. ui/src/bridge/fakeWave3.ts:27 follows. Tests in the history text tests next to the soundReplaced case.

Fix

ui/src/components/history/stepText.ts rowWhatText: with the track as the place, a track 「（何を）」 of the same track gives the bare row, and soundKnob gives history.changeAt with the knob name and param.value.percent (「明るさ 50 → 25 %」). The status line keeps stepWhatText. The fake (fakeWave3.ts) gives the same row as Rust and needed no change. Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "CORE-PLAY-A-003: rows whose right column is the track do not name it again".

CORE-PLAY-A-004lowbugfixedKeep what was just played: past 4096 events with one key in the phrase, the key's release is dropped and the note lasts to the phrase end

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-A-004-keep-what-was-just-played-past-4096-events-with.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

  • crates/meloet-core/src/play/recent.rs:156-164 (Phrase::push: self.events.remove(1) when notes <= 1)

What happens

A phrase of 「さっきのを残す」 that has one key press and then more than 4095 other events (pitch bend, modulation wheel, knobs sending CC) drops events[1] each time it is full. When the key was already let go, events[1] is its release (NoteOff, or NoteOn with velocity 0), so the release is the first thing dropped. The kept clip then has that note held until the end of the phrase (keep_played lets every key go at placed.end, recent.rs 465-471), for example a short note becomes many seconds long.

What should happen

The comment at recent.rs 160-161 says the rule: "One key held under a long run of controller moves: the key stays, and the oldest of the moves goes." Only controller moves are meant to be dropped; the key's own release must stay so the note keeps the length it was played with (architecture 25-16: whole notes are dropped from the old side, not cut in two). The test one_key_held_under_thousands_of_controller_moves_stays (recent/tests.rs 590) only covers a key still held, where events[1] is a controller move.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Standing still: press and release C4 (two events), then move the pitch bend or modulation wheel for a few seconds (more than 4095 messages; a wheel sends one about every ms while it moves). Press 「さっきのを残す」: the clip's C4 lasts from its press to the end of the phrase. As a test: still(0, S, on(60)), still(0, S + 1000, off(60)), then 5000 Cc { number: 1 } items, keep, and the note length is not the 1000 µs played.

Cause

With notes <= 1 the code removes index 1 without looking at what it is. After the single press, index 1 is whatever came next; once the key is released, that is the release.

Suggested fix

Drop the oldest controller move after index 0 instead of index 1 blindly: find the first i >= 1 whose kind is Cc or PitchBend and remove it (dropping a middle controller move is what the code does today, so head needs no change); only when there is none fall back to remove(1). No protocol, file or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Phrase::push (play/recent.rs:152-169): when the phrase is full and holds at most one key press, it calls self.events.remove(1) without looking at the event. With the press at index 0 and its release right after it, the release is the first event dropped, and keep_played then ends the key at the end of the phrase. The comment there says only controller moves are meant to go. Severity low stands (one key and more than 4095 controller moves in one phrase).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/recent.rs Phrase::push: in the else branch remove the first event at index ≥ 1 that is a Cc or PitchBend; fall back to remove(1) only when there is none. Test next to one_key_held_under_thousands_of_controller_moves_stays in recent/tests.rs: press, release after 1000 µs, 5000 CC moves, keep; the note keeps its played length.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/recent.rs Phrase::push: with one key press in a full phrase, the oldest controller move (Cc or PitchBend) after the press goes; only when there is none does the event after the press go, as before.

Test (in play/recent/tests.rs): one_key_let_go_under_thousands_of_controller_moves_keeps_its_length.

CORE-PLAY-C-001lowbugfixedA stopped plugin loaded again after the sample rate changed runs at the old sample rate

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-001-a-stopped-plugin-loaded-again-after-the-sample-r.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:668 (load_again passes over Phase::Stopped | Phase::Reloading)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:855-858 (reload_plugin: Effect::ReloadPlugin { ticket } carries no rate)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/aistate.rs:362-365 (reload_at, the same effect)
crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/hosts.rs:1165-1172 (Run::reload loads with slot.spec, the spec of the first load)
crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/hosts.rs:1110-1117 (Request::Load { sample_rate: spec.sample_rate, .. })
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3364-3373 (Effect::ReloadPlugin → Hosts::reload(id, block, ..))

What happens

A plugin whose process stopped (or is being loaded again, Phase::Reloading) while the output runs at one sample rate, and that is loaded again with 「もう一度読みこむ」 (or by the AI link's revive) after the output was opened at another rate, is loaded at the OLD rate. Its plugin host is told the old rate in Request::Load, while the engine runs at the new one. An instrument then plays out of tune and out of time (told 48 kHz while the engine runs at 44.1 kHz: about 1.5 semitones low and slower; tempo-synced LFOs and delays wrong); an effect's delays, filters and reverb times are wrong. Nothing tells the person; the plugin shows as ready.

What should happen

Every plugin runs at the rate of the stream: Play::set_plug_rate says "a plugin is made for one rate, so every one is loaded again when it is another than before" (plugs.rs:692-699), and want_plug / load_again always send sample_rate: self.plugs.rate. docs/architecture.md 20-10: "Reload: a new process, restoring the last known state plus parameters moved since" — with the rate the engine runs at now. The sibling path for a plugin that never loaded (Phase::Failed in reload_plugin) does it right: it goes through want_plug, which uses the present rate.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A song with a plugin instrument (e.g. Surge XT) plays through an output at 48 kHz.
  2. The plugin's process stops (crash, or a hang killed after 5 s): the track shows 「止まりました」 with 「もう一度読みこむ」.
  3. Choose another output that runs at 44.1 kHz (Input::AudioOpened { sample_rate: 44_100 } → set_plug_rate → load_again(true), which passes the stopped plugin over).
  4. Press 「もう一度読みこむ」: Effect::ReloadPlugin → Hosts::reload → place(&slot.spec, ..) with sample_rate: 48_000. Play: the track sounds about 1.5 semitones too low.

The same for a plugin in Phase::Reloading at step 3 (its reload, already asked, answers at 48 kHz).

Cause

Hosts keeps the LoadSpec of the first load in Slot::spec and Run::reload loads with it unchanged; only SetOutputs ever updates the spec (hosts.rs:752-759). The core's load_again reloads every Ready/Loading plugin at the new rate but continues on Stopped/Reloading, and the later Effect::ReloadPlugin { ticket } has no rate to give, so nothing tells Hosts the rate changed.

Suggested fix

Give the reload the present rate: Effect::ReloadPlugin { ticket, sample_rate: self.plugs.rate } and Hosts::reload(id, sample_rate, block, done), which sets slot.spec.sample_rate = sample_rate before place. A plugin in Phase::Reloading when the rate changes can be asked to reload again (or marked so plugin_loaded reloads it once more). No protocol, wire or file change. See also CORE-PLAY-C-006 (the same continue in load_again, another fault).

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). load_again (play/plugs.rs:668) passes over Phase::Stopped | Phase::Reloading | Phase::Failed; 「もう一度読みこむ」 of a stopped plugin sends Effect::ReloadPlugin { ticket } (plugs.rs:855-858, also aistate.rs:365), the service calls Hosts::reload(id, block, ..) (service.rs:3364-3373), and Run::reload (hosts.rs:1165-1172) loads with slot.spec, whose sample_rate is the one of the first load (Request::Load { sample_rate: spec.sample_rate }, hosts.rs:1110-1117). Nothing gives Hosts the new rate. A Failed plugin goes through want_plug and gets the present rate.

Severity changed from medium to low: it needs a plugin whose process stopped and, before it is loaded again, an output at another sample rate. The sound is wrong when it happens, but it is a rare corner, and taking the plugin out and in again is the way around.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs / play/plugs.rs / play/plugs/aistate.rs: Effect::ReloadPlugin { ticket, sample_rate: self.plugs.rate }; crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs passes it on; crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/hosts.rs reload(id, sample_rate, block, done) writes slot.spec.sample_rate before place. For Phase::Reloading at the moment of a rate change: remember in the plug that the rate changed and, in plugin_loaded, load it again once through the normal path (the plugin is Ready then, so load_again's rule applies). Tests in state/added_tests.rs next to plugins_are_loaded_again_for_another_sample_rate_and_for_a_new_engine (the reload effect carries the new rate) and in the hosts' tests with the fake host (the Load of a reload names the new rate).

Fix

Effect::ReloadPlugin carries sample_rate (crates/meloet-core/src/state.rs); reload_plugin (play/plugs.rs) and reload_at (play/plugs/aistate.rs) give it the rate of now, the service passes it on (service.rs), and Hosts::reload(id, sample_rate, block, done) writes it into the slot's spec before the new process loads the plugin (plugins/hosts.rs). A plug remembers the rate it was asked at (Plug::rate); one that was being loaded again when the rate changed is loaded once more, with the state it has, when it is there (plugin_loaded → load_again_at). The callers of Hosts::reload in tests/plugin_hosts.rs and tests/plugin_windows.rs name the rate.

Tests: play::plugs::tests::a_stopped_plugin_is_loaded_again_at_the_rate_the_stream_runs_at_now (the reload effect has the new rate; a reload overtaken by another rate is loaded again at it), tests/plugin_state_put.rs a_reload_is_at_the_rate_asked_for_and_without_a_state_tried_and_never_read_back (the Load of a reload names the new rate, with a stand-in plugin host).

CORE-PLAY-C-004lowbugfixedA track's plugin that fails to load during a replace audition leaves the track silent after Esc, not on the sine stand-in

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-004-a-track-s-plugin-that-fails-to-load-during-a-rep.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:607-624 (plugin_unreadable: under a trial on the seat, no EngineOp::Instrument { SINE })
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:509-520 (place_waiting only places a Ready plugin)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/shelf.rs:364-375 (end_audition in replace mode: EndTrial { keep: false }, then place_waiting)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:446-459 (seat_instrument: a plugin that is loading is sent to the engine as InstrumentRef::Plugin, which instruments::make makes Silent)

What happens

If a track's plugin instrument is still loading when the person starts trying other sounds on that track in 「入れかえる」 mode, and the load then fails, the track is silent after the audition is cancelled (Esc, or the shelf closed): the engine's own instrument of the seat is still the silent stand-in for a plugin that loads. The status line said 「曲で使っている … を読みこめませんでした。1 本のトラックはサイン波で鳴らしています。」 (PluginUnreadable), and the instrument row says the sine wave stands in, but nothing sounds when it is played.

What should happen

Spec 3-bands "A plugin that could not be loaded (89-12)": "When a plugin of the song is there but could not be loaded, that track plays with a sine wave, and the instrument row says 「サイン波で代わり」." plugin_unreadable's own doc: "A track's instrument that did not load sounds as the sine wave (spec 3, 89-12)". The sibling sync_plugs (plugs.rs:724-731) keeps this under a trial because the sine is what was set aside there; here what was set aside is the silent stand-in.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open a song whose track 1 has a plugin instrument that takes long and then fails to load (its plugin host does not answer Load within 30 s, or the load is refused late).
  2. While it loads (the track is silent), open the shelf on track 1 to replace its sound and choose a row (a trial on the seat).
  3. The load fails: plugin_unreadable → Phase::Failed, trial_seat == Some(seat) so no sine is sent; the status line says the track plays a sine wave.
  4. Press Esc: end_audition → EndTrial { keep: false } brings back the seat's own Silent; place_waiting does nothing for Failed. Play track 1: silence.

Cause

plugin_unreadable leaves out the sine while a trial is on the seat (the engine would take it as the tried instrument), and nothing gives it later: end_audition → place_waiting handles only a Ready plugin.

Suggested fix

In place_waiting (or in end_audition for replace mode), when the seat's own plugin is Phase::Failed, push EngineOp::Instrument { seat, instrument: InstrumentRef::SINE }. No protocol, wording or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). seat_instrument (play/plugs.rs:440-459) gives the engine InstrumentRef::Plugin for a plugin that is loading (the silent stand-in). When the load fails while a trial is on that seat, plugin_unreadable (plugs.rs:607-624) sets Phase::Failed and leaves the sine out (trial_seat != Some(seat) is false), but still returns StatusMessage::PluginUnreadable. On Esc, end_audition (play/shelf.rs:364-375) sends EndTrial { keep: false } and calls place_waiting, which acts only on a Ready plug. The seat's own instrument stays the silent stand-in, against spec 3 (89-12) and the status line just shown. Severity low stands (a load that fails late, during a replace audition on that very track).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs place_waiting: when the seat's own plug is Phase::Failed, push EngineOp::Instrument { seat, instrument: InstrumentRef::SINE }. Check the other end of a replace audition too (the tried sound is kept: then the own plug is dropped and nothing is needed). Test in the shelf or plugs tests: load fails under a trial, Esc, the seat gets the sine.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs place_waiting: when the seat's own plugin did not load (Phase::Failed), the engine gets EngineOp::Instrument { SINE } for the seat after EndTrial. When the tried sound is kept, the own plugin is dropped and nothing more is needed (play/shelf.rs unchanged).

Test: play::plugs::tests::a_tracks_plugin_that_does_not_load_under_a_trial_sounds_as_the_sine_wave_after_it.

CORE-PLAY-C-005lowbugfixedLoading again a failed plugin that this PC no longer has leaves its track silent, not on the sine stand-in

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-005-loading-again-a-failed-plugin-that-this-pc-no-lo.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:860-873 (reload_plugin, Phase::Failed: the return of want_plug is not looked at)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:402-405 (want_plug → Want::Missing when known has no bundle for the key)
crates/meloet-core/src/instruments.rs:127 (InstrumentRef::Plugin(_) → Silent)

What happens

「もう一度読みこむ」 on a track whose plugin instrument did not load (Phase::Failed, the track plays the sine wave) drops the failed entry and asks want_plug to load the plugin, then sends EngineOp::Instrument { instrument: Plugin(..) } whatever want_plug answered. When this PC no longer has the plugin (a later scan, e.g. 「もう一度スキャン」 after it was removed or moved, took it out of known), want_plug answers Missing and loads nothing, but the seat still gets the plugin stand-in, which is Silent. The track goes from the sine wave to silence and stays so; the screen shows the plugin as missing, which says the sine wave stands in.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 20-11 "Missing things": "No plugin → instrument sine"; engine_instrument (plugs.rs:343-350) gives the engine the sine for a plugin this PC does not have. The sibling load_from_song (aistate.rs:375-384) sends the instrument only when want_plug(..) == Want::Loading, and seat_instrument maps Want::Missing to InstrumentRef::SINE (plugs.rs:446-449).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A song's track uses a plugin instrument that fails to load (PluginUnreadable): it plays the sine wave and shows 「もう一度読みこむ」.
  2. The plugin is taken off the PC (or its bundle moved) and the scan runs again (「もう一度スキャン」): set_known_plugins drops the key.
  3. Press 「もう一度読みこむ」 on the track: reload_plugin → drop_plug → want_plug = Missing → EngineOp::Instrument { Plugin } → the seat is Silent.

Cause

In the Phase::Failed arm of reload_plugin, the Want that want_plug returns is thrown away and the plugin is always sent to the engine.

Suggested fix

Look at the answer as load_from_song does: send InstrumentRef::Plugin(plugin) only for Want::Loading, and InstrumentRef::SINE for Want::Missing (Kept cannot happen after the drop). No protocol or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). In the Phase::Failed arm of reload_plugin (play/plugs.rs:860-873) the answer of want_plug is thrown away and EngineOp::Instrument { instrument: InstrumentRef::Plugin(plugin) } is always sent (outside a trial). want_plug answers Want::Missing and loads nothing when plugs.known has no bundle for the key (plugs.rs:402-405), and the engine's stand-in for a plugin is silent. seat_instrument (plugs.rs:446-449) maps Want::Missing to the sine. Severity low stands (the plugin must leave the PC between the failed load and the press).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs reload_plugin: match self.want_plug(..): Want::Loading → the plugin stand-in as now; Want::Missing → InstrumentRef::SINE (the seat already plays the sine, so sending nothing is as good; choose the one that reads clearer). Test in the plugs tests: failed plug, the key leaves known, reload → no EngineOp::Instrument { Plugin }.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs reload_plugin, Phase::Failed: the silent stand-in for the plugin goes to the engine only when want_plug answers Want::Loading, as load_from_song does; for Want::Missing nothing is sent and the seat keeps the sine wave it plays. instruments.rs is unchanged.

Test: play::plugs::tests::loading_again_a_plugin_this_pc_no_longer_has_leaves_the_sine_wave.

CORE-PLAY-C-006lowbugfixedAfter the engine is made anew, a stopped plugin can no longer be loaded again

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-006-after-the-engine-is-made-anew-a-stopped-plugin-c.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:701-707 (plugs_lost → load_again(false))
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:668 (Phase::Stopped | Phase::Reloading | Phase::Failed => continue)
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:2587-2598 (unload_gone: the stopped slot handed back with the lost engine is taken out of ids and unloaded in Hosts)
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3364-3373 (Effect::ReloadPlugin with no ids[ticket] → Input::PluginUnreadable)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs:603-604 (Phase::Reloading → back to Phase::Stopped)

What happens

When the engine is lost with its stream and made anew (service.rs Audio::open → rebuild → Input::PluginsLost), every plugin that sounds is loaded again under a new ticket, but a plugin that had stopped is passed over. The lost engine's slots are dropped, and each one's Gone makes unload_gone take the stopped plugin's ticket out of PluginSide::ids and unload its slot in Hosts (with the state and parameters Hosts remembered for its reload). The core still lists the plugin as stopped and the screen still offers 「もう一度読みこむ」, but pressing it does nothing: ReloadPlugin finds no id, answers PluginUnreadable, and the plugin goes back to Stopped. An instrument stays silent, an effect stays passed by, until the person removes it or opens the song again. The AI link's revive (reload_at) ends the same way.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 20-10: "Reload: a new process, restoring the last known state plus parameters moved since." stopped_plugins / plugin_is_down keep offering 「もう一度読みこむ」 for such a plugin, so it should work (or the plugin should be loaded again with the others, from what the song has).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A plugin of the song stops (its process crashed): 「…が止まりました」 with 「もう一度読みこむ」.
  2. The output is lost in a way that loses the engine with the stream (cpal's failure paths, recover gets nothing back), and an output opens again: rebuild, PluginsLost.
  3. Press 「もう一度読みこむ」: Effect::ReloadPlugin → no ids[ticket] → PluginUnreadable → still stopped.

Cause

load_again does not load stopped plugins, and the reload path depends on the old slot still being known to PluginSide::ids and Hosts, which the drop of the lost engine removes. ReloadPlugin has no way to fall back to a load from the song.

Suggested fix

In load_again(false), load a Stopped plugin anew like the others (PlugState::Bytes(old.plugin.state) or Fresh, new ticket, removed from plugs.stopped), or in the service turn a ReloadPlugin whose ticket has no id into Input::PluginUnreadable only after trying a load from the song (the rules could do this in plugin_unreadable for Phase::Reloading: load_from_song(spot)). Related, but another fault: CORE-PLAY-C-001 (the same continue keeps the old sample rate for a later reload). No protocol or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). plugs_lost (play/plugs.rs:701-707) calls load_again(false), which passes over Phase::Stopped (plugs.rs:668). The engine lost with its stream held the stopped plugin's slot (HousedInstrument / HousedEffect, plugins/housed.rs); when it is dropped its Gone tells the service, and unload_gone (service.rs:2587-2598) takes the ticket out of ids and unloads the slot in Hosts. A later Effect::ReloadPlugin finds no id (service.rs:3364-3373) and answers Input::PluginUnreadable, which puts Reloading back to Stopped (plugs.rs:603-604). 「もう一度読みこむ」 stays offered and does nothing. Severity low stands (it needs a stopped plugin and an engine lost with its stream, which the code itself treats as a failure path: "the engine was lost with an earlier stream; making a new one").

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs load_again: when not live (the engine was made anew), load a Stopped plug anew like the others, from the song's state (PlugState::Bytes(old.plugin.state) or Fresh), with a new ticket, and take its old ticket out of plugs.stopped. Reloading and Failed stay as they are. What Hosts remembered of parameters moved since the last state is lost in this corner; that is acceptable against a plugin that cannot come back. Test next to plugins_are_loaded_again_for_another_sample_rate_and_for_a_new_engine.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs.rs load_again_at (the body of load_again): with a new engine (live false) a Phase::Stopped plugin is loaded anew like the others, with the song's state (Bytes, Fresh when empty), under a new ticket, and its old ticket leaves plugs.stopped. At another sample rate (live) it still waits for 「もう一度読みこむ」. Reloading and Failed stay as they were.

Test: play::plugs::tests::a_stopped_plugin_is_loaded_anew_as_the_song_has_it_when_the_engine_is_made_anew.

CORE-PLAY-C-007lowbugfixedDuplicate track gives the plugin states it asked for to the copy by slot number, not by plugin

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-PLAY-C-007-duplicate-track-gives-the-plugin-states-it-asked.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/copies.rs:54-72 (ask_copy_states keeps only the place, after checking the key once)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/copies.rs:106-132 (copy_state_said hands back (place, state); the key the state belongs to is not kept)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/tracks.rs:109-122 (make_copy puts each state on whatever plugin the copy has at that place now, under that plugin's key)

What happens

「トラックを複製」 on a track whose plugins sound first asks each of them for its state and makes the copy when all have answered (round 12, A3). The answers are matched to the copy by place only (instrument, or insert slot number). If the track's inserts were reordered (MoveInsert) or an insert was replaced by another plugin (ReplaceEffect, 「入れかえる」) after the asks went out and before the last answer came, the state of plugin A is given to the plugin B that is in that slot now: set_plugin_state(doc, at, &key_of_B, state_of_A, ..). The copy's B then holds A's bytes in the song (and in the file when saved); its load gives B a state that is not its own, which the plugin host refuses (the slot then stays empty: plugin_unreadable → Failed) or which B reads as something else.

What should happen

copies.rs doc: "the copy's plugins then have the state the song has for them" — the state of the same plugin. A plugin state belongs to its key (docs/architecture.md 20-5: "Parameters and state differ between formats"; 24-16 G: "The two formats of one plugin cannot take each other's state"). ask_copy_states itself checks q.key == p.plugin.key when it asks; the answer should be held to the same check.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A track with two different plugin effects, Zam (slot 1) and LSP (slot 2), both sounding.
  2. 「トラックを複製」: two StateAsk::Copy asks go out. Their answers come later through the plugin hosts' one thread (behind, for example, a slow Load of another plugin, or a plugin window being made on the process's main thread: 10–300 ms).
  3. Before they come, drag LSP above Zam in the mixer (MoveInsert). The intents are also served back to back when both were held behind an AI batch (state/ai.rs ai_hold): duplicate, then the reorder, then the answers.
  4. The copy is made from the song as it is now: its slot 1 is LSP, and gets Zam's state under LSP's key.

Cause

CopyAsk keeps place and puts but not the plugin's key; copy_state_said and make_copy do not check that the plugin now at that place of the source track is still the one that was asked. forget_copy is called only for another song or a history jump, not for other steps.

Suggested fix

Keep the key (or the ticket) in CopyAsk / CopyStates, and in make_copy give a state only where the copy's plugin at that place has the same key (better: find the place by the ticket's present spot, so a moved plugin still gets its own state); a state with no such plugin is dropped and the copy keeps the song's state. No protocol or file change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). ask_copy_states (play/plugs/copies.rs:46-90) checks the key once when it asks and keeps only ticket, place, puts. copy_state_said hands back (place, state), and make_copy (play/tracks.rs:109-122) puts each state on whatever plugin the copy has at that place now, under that plugin's key. forget_copy runs only for another song or a history jump, so a MoveInsert or a replace on the source track between the asks and the last answer is not noticed. Severity low stands (the two intents must fall inside the wait for the answers, tens to hundreds of milliseconds).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/copies.rs: keep the plugin's key in CopyAsk and hand it back with the state (CopyStates rows of (place, key, state)); crates/meloet-core/src/play/tracks.rs make_copy: give a state only where the copy's plugin at that place has that key, else leave the song's state. (Following a moved plugin by its ticket is more than the fix needs.) Test in the copies tests: asks out, MoveInsert, answers in → no state lands on the other plugin.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/play/plugs/copies.rs: CopyAsk keeps the key of the plugin asked; when the last answer comes, a state is kept only where the plugin of its ticket is still at the place it was asked at and the song's plugin there has that key. Any other state is dropped, and the copy keeps the song's state there. play/tracks.rs (make_copy) is unchanged.

Test: play::plugs::tests::a_copy_gets_a_state_only_where_the_plugin_that_said_it_is (asks out, MoveInsert, answers in).

CORE-STATE-003lowui-uxfixedClosing the window or Quit while a save is being written is dropped without a word

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-STATE-003-closing-the-window-or-quit-while-a-save-is-being.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs:454-457 (close_requested: if self.files.exiting || self.files.waiting.is_some() { return; })
crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3716-3785 (save_song: a song with plugins or audio is written on the meloet-save thread; the answer comes later)

What happens

While a song with plugins (up to STATE_WAIT = 3 s for their states) or with audio (copying the audio into 音/, the status line says 「保存しています ・ 音のファイルを写しています …」, can take many seconds) is being written, the window's close button and 「meloet を終わる」 (Command::Quit) do nothing: the request is thrown away. When the save ends, meloet stays open; the person has to close it again and does not know why the first one did nothing.

What should happen

A close is kept and done once the song is free, as for a recording: docs/architecture.md 14 "A close request stops recording, puts what was recorded into the song, and then decides" (close_when_recorded, songs.rs:447-452). The doc comment of Waiting (songs.rs:37) says "Song commands wait meanwhile", not that they are lost. After the save has answered, the usual flow applies: no unsaved changes → end; changes made during the save → 「保存していない変更があります」.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Open a song with audio clips (or a plugin) and press Mod S: Effect::SaveSong starts the save thread, files.waiting = Some(Waiting::Save(Stay)).
  2. While the status line still says 「保存しています …」, click the window's close button (Input::CloseRequested) or choose 「meloet を終わる」.
  3. Nothing happens, then or after Input::SongSaved comes.

Cause

close_requested returns at once when files.waiting is set, and nothing remembers the request (unlike the busy case right below it, which sets files.close_when_recorded).

Suggested fix

When files.waiting is Some(Waiting::Save(_)), set a flag (or reuse close_when_recorded) and call close_requested again once the save has answered (SongSaved / SongNotSaved in song_input; after SongNotSaved the 「保存できない」 dialog may take precedence). No protocol, wording or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). close_requested (state/songs.rs:454-457) returns at once when files.waiting.is_some(), and nothing keeps the request; the case right below it (a recording) keeps it in files.close_when_recorded. A save of a song with plugins or audio answers later (service.rs save_song on its own thread), so a close or 「meloet を終わる」 in that time is lost. Severity low stands (the way around is to close again).

Corrections: files.waiting is also set while an open or another file job waits; the fix below is for the save, and a close during the others can be kept the same way if it costs nothing more.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs: in close_requested, when something waits, set a flag (a new close_when_free, or reuse close_when_recorded if its reader is called after the save's answer too) and return; where SongSaved / SongNotSaved (and the other answers that clear files.waiting) are handled, call close_requested again when the flag is set and no dialog came up from the answer (after SongNotSaved the 「保存できない」 dialog comes first and the flag is dropped). Test in state/songs/tests.rs: CloseRequested during a waiting save, then SongSaved → Effect::Exit.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs: a new flag Files::close_when_saved, set by close_requested while a save is being written. When the save answers (SongSaved), the close is done then (it comes before a new song or an open that was to follow the save; a close that was what followed already just ends). After SongNotSaved 「保存できない」 comes first and the flag is dropped (for a save of the AI link, which puts up no dialog, the close goes on). No new wording. docs/architecture.md 14 (Flow) says so.

Test: state::songs::tests::closing_while_a_save_is_written_closes_once_it_has_answered (state/songs/tests.rs: close and Quit during the save, then the answer ends meloet; a change made during the save is asked about; a failed save shows 「保存できない」 and the close is not kept past it).

CORE-STATE-004lowbugfixedThe keyboard fader's waiting step is made in a song that replaced the one it was moved in

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-STATE-004-the-keyboard-fader-s-waiting-step-is-made-in-a-s.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/state/buttons.rs:110-115 (keyboard_fader keeps Pending { track, db, at_us } by track id only)
crates/meloet-core/src/state/buttons.rs:128-146 (fader_due / commit_fader send SetGain { commit: true } for that id, whatever song is in place by then)
crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs:536-541 (replace_song does not let go of self.fader)

What happens

The fader (CC 7) of a keyboard sets the selected track's level as a preview, and its step is made 0.5 s after the fader rests (FADER_REST_US). If the song is replaced within that half second (「新しい曲」 or 「最近ひらいた曲 ▸」 with nothing unsaved, a file the PC hands over, the AI link's new or open song), the preview goes with the old song (Play::replace_song → forget_mixer), but the pending step stays. On the next Input::Tick it is made in the new song: the track there that has the same id (track ids start again for every song, so usually track 1) gets the old fader level as a 「音量を変える」 step. The new song shows as changed and its level jumped, though nobody touched it.

What should happen

A step belongs to the song it was made in. docs/architecture.md 13: "Esc (CancelMixPreview), another step, undo, or replacing the song ends the preview" — the fader's level is such a preview until it rests; replacing the song should end it, not turn it into a step of the next song.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. A song with nothing unsaved; track 1 selected. Move the keyboard's fader (Input::KeyboardFader): fader.pending = Some(Pending { track: 1, .. }).
  2. Within 0.5 s, choose 「新しい曲」 (no question, nothing unsaved): then(Then::NewSong) puts a new song in place.
  3. 0.5 s after the last fader move, Input::Tick → fader_due → commit_fader → SetGain { strip: Track { id: 1 }, commit: true } → Play::mix makes the step on the new song's track 1 (it makes the step even with no preview, play/mixer.rs:251-260).

Cause

Model::fader (buttons::Fader) is not tied to the song: neither replace_song nor anything else clears pending (or compares a serial) when another song takes the place of this one.

Suggested fix

Keep the song's serial in Pending and drop the pending step in commit_fader when self.play.serial() differs, or reset self.fader to its default in Model::replace_song. No protocol, wording or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). keyboard_fader (state/buttons.rs:110-115) keeps Pending { track, db, at_us }; fader_due / commit_fader send SetGain { strip: Track { id }, commit: true } half a second later; Model::replace_song (state/songs.rs:537-541) does not touch self.fader. Track ids start again in every song, so the step lands on the track of the new song that has the same id. Severity low stands (the song must be replaced within 0.5 s of a fader move).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs Model::replace_song: self.fader = Default::default(); (if Fader holds something that must outlive a song, such as the pick-up position, clear only pending and set). Test in state/buttons_tests.rs or next to the fader tests: fader move, replace the song, tick after 0.5 s → no step in the new song.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/state/songs.rs Model::replace_song: self.fader = Default::default(), so the keyboard fader's waiting step (and what it caught) goes with the song it was moved in; it is never made in the new song.

Test: state::songs::tests::a_keyboard_fader_step_still_waiting_goes_with_the_song_it_was_for (state/songs/tests.rs).

CORE-STATE-005lowbugfixedThe PC's file windows start nowhere, not in the songs folder, when the song's folder is gone

docs/bugs/reports/CORE-STATE-005-the-pc-s-file-windows-start-nowhere-not-in-the-s.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs:3798-3806 (start_folder)

What happens

When the song has a file but its folder is not there any more (a USB stick pulled out, the folder moved or deleted in the file manager), start_folder returns None, so 「ひらく…」, 「名前をつけて保存…」 and the folder window of 「保存できない」 open wherever GTK starts by itself, not in the songs folder. This is exactly the moment 「保存できない」 comes up (the save over the file failed because the folder is gone), and the person is left to find the songs folder alone.

What should happen

Its own doc comment: "The folder of the song file in use, or else the songs folder." With the song's folder gone, the songs folder (<music>, docs/architecture.md 14).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Save a song, then remove its folder <songs>/<name>/ outside meloet (or save it on a USB stick and pull the stick out).
  2. Press Mod S: SaveTo::File fails, 「保存できない」 comes up.
  3. 「場所をえらぶ」: Effect::PickFolder → start_folder() is None → ShellRequest::PickFolder { folder: None }, so the shell does not call set_current_folder (crates/meloet-app/src/pcwindow.rs:124-126).

Cause

.or_else(|| self.songs_dir.clone()) comes before .filter(|d| d.is_dir()): the song's folder is taken when the song has a path at all, and is then thrown away by the filter, with nothing to fall back to.

Suggested fix

Filter the song's folder before falling back: self.model.song_path().and_then(|p| p.parent()).filter(|d| d.is_dir()).map(PathBuf::from).or_else(|| self.songs_dir.clone().filter(|d| d.is_dir())). No protocol, wording or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). start_folder (service.rs:3798-3806): song_path().and_then(parent).map(PathBuf::from).or_else(|| songs_dir.clone()).filter(|d| d.is_dir()). When the song has a path, the or_else is never taken, and a folder that is gone is then thrown away by the filter: None, not the songs folder its own comment promises. Severity low stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs start_folder: filter each choice before falling back (…parent().filter(|d| d.is_dir()).map(PathBuf::from).or_else(|| self.songs_dir.clone().filter(|d| d.is_dir()))). Do it together with CORE-STATE-002, which adds a second helper with the same shape.

Fix

crates/meloet-core/src/service.rs start_folder: each choice is filtered before the fall back (first_folder: the song's folder when it is a directory, else the songs folder when it is one). Done together with CORE-STATE-002 (place_folder uses the same filter).

Test: service::shell_tests::the_pcs_windows_start_among_the_song_folders_or_in_the_songs_folder (the part after the song's folder is removed: 「ひらく…」, 「名前をつけて保存…」 and 「保存できない」 → 「場所をえらぶ」 start in the songs folder).

ENGINE-002lowbugfixedPlugins on the returns and the master are told the transport of the last stretch of a piece, not of its first frame

docs/bugs/reports/ENGINE-002-plugins-on-the-returns-and-the-master-are-told-t.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:2964-2969 (set_transport is written for every stretch; only the built-in effects' ctx keeps the first one, piece_ctx)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:3025-3039 (the returns' inserts run after the stretch loop)
crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs:3064-3065 (the master's inserts run after the stretch loop)
crates/meloet-engine/src/plugin.rs:211-216 (submit copies self.block.transport() as it is at that moment)

What happens

Engine::render cuts a piece into stretches (at notes, clicks, tempo points, the loop's end, arpeggio steps, mute steps …) and, for each stretch, writes "where the song is at the first frame of this stretch" into the shared PluginBlock (line 2965). The tracks' instruments and inserts are run inside the stretch loop and read the right value. The returns and the master are run once for the whole piece after the loop (lines 3025-3065), so a plugin effect there submits the transport that the last stretch left in PluginBlock: its position_quarters, position_frames, bar, bar_start_quarters, tempo and flags describe a frame somewhere inside the piece, while the plugin processes the piece from its first frame.

  • While the song plays, the told position jumps forward by 0 to one block (up to 1024 frames) from block to block, depending on where the last cut fell. A plugin on a return or the master that follows the host position (an LFO, gate, tremolo or delay synced to the song position; LV2 plugins get time:Position at frame 0 of every block from this value, docs/architecture.md 21-3) wobbles in phase with it.
  • In a piece that holds the loop's end, the plugin is told the loop's start for a block whose first part is still before the loop's end.
  • In the piece where a count-in ends, it is told "recording" from the first frame.

The built-in effects on the same strips get the tempo of the first stretch (piece_ctx), so the two kinds of effect disagree.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 21-3: "Built-in delay: EffectContext::tempo_bpm = tempo_at at the head of the cut. Returns and the master are not split by cuts, so they get the value at the head of the render (at most 1024 frames)." and "To plugins (PluginTransport): the values at the head of the cut". A strip that is not cut has one cut, the whole piece, whose head is the piece's first frame; the built-in effects already get that (piece_ctx).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put a plugin effect that follows the song position (for example a tempo-synced tremolo or an LFO-driven filter in sync mode) on return A or on the master.
  2. Play a song with notes on a track, so that most blocks are cut into several stretches.
  3. The plugin is told a position that is late by a different amount in each block; its synced modulation stutters. The same plugin on a track insert runs smoothly.

Cause

PluginBlock holds one transport, overwritten at the head of every stretch. PluginSlot::submit reads it when the plugin's block is handed over, which for the returns and the master happens only after the last stretch.

Suggested fix

Keep the transport of the first stretch (told of the first iteration, next to piece_ctx) and write it back with self.plugin_block.set_transport(&first) just before the returns' inserts run (line 3025). Nothing else changes; no protocol or file change. tests/lookahead.rs plugins_are_told_where_the_song_is could be grown with a fake plugin effect on the master that records what it is told.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). In Engine::render the stretch loop writes self.plugin_block.set_transport(&told) at the head of every stretch (engine.rs:2964-2965) and keeps only the built-in effects' ctx of the first one (piece_ctx). The returns' and the master's inserts run after the loop (engine.rs:3025-3065), and a plugin effect's submit (plugin.rs:211) copies self.block.transport() at that moment, which is the last stretch's. Architecture 21-3 says the returns and the master "get the value at the head of the render". Severity low stands: the told position is off by less than one block, and only plugins on a return or the master that follow the host position can show it.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs render: keep told of the first stretch next to piece_ctx (a Copy value, no allocation) and call self.plugin_block.set_transport(&first) once before the returns' loop. Test: grow tests/lookahead.rs plugins_are_told_where_the_song_is with a fake plugin effect on the master in a block that is cut into several stretches.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-engine/src/engine.rs render: the transport of the first stretch is kept with piece_ctx and written back into PluginBlock before the returns' and the master's inserts run.
  • Test: plugins_on_the_returns_and_the_master_are_told_the_head_of_the_piece in crates/meloet-engine/tests/lookahead.rs (a stand-in plugin effect on return A and on the master reads PluginBlock as a plugin slot's submit does, in blocks cut by a key every 100 frames).
MODEL-A-004lowbugfixedAfter close_laps, a late key or pedal of the ended pass goes back into that pass when the next pass has no event yet

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-A-004-after-close-laps-a-late-key-or-pedal-of-the-ende.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs:249-281 (close_laps ends the passes but never opens the next one)
crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs:136-140 (a key is held in self.laps.len() - 1, the last pass the recorder has)
crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs:370-379 (control: a late controller move goes to last)
crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs:335-349 (enter_lap is the only place a pass is added, and only push and finish call it)

What happens

With a loop and 「重ねる」, the core calls Recorder::close_laps(upto) when the heard position enters the next pass (Play::pass_over, crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1857). The recorder adds a pass only when an event of that pass comes in. At the moment of the close, usually no key of the new pass has come yet (a person's keys are stamped with the heard position), so the last pass in laps is the one just closed.

A key pressed in the last moment of the closed pass whose event reaches the core after that close (the engine reports it a block later) then goes into the closed pass, not into "the pass that is going on":

  • A late key: the closed pass gets notes again, and the next close_laps gives it as a take of its own. With 「重ねる」 each take is its own step, so the person gets an extra 「録音」 step holding that one note (or an extra take in the clip, when 「重ねる」 was just turned on and add_recording takes the passes).
  • A late pedal up (or bend, modulation) with no late note: it goes into the closed pass's controls; that pass has no notes, so the next close_laps gives no take and clears it. The pedal-up point is lost from the song (the pass-0 take has no pedal up in its last moments). The next pass still starts with the pedal up, because the recorder's held values take the move.

When some event of the new pass came before the close, the same late key goes into the new pass, as the test closed_passes_are_given_once_and_late_keys_go_on expects. So the result depends on whether the person happened to play in the first 15 ms of the new pass.

What should happen

The doc of close_laps: "Keys that come late for an ended pass go into the pass that is going on." The pedal test a_pedal_of_a_pass_goes_there_though_notes_of_the_next_pass_came_first ("Once the pass was ended, what comes late for it goes into the pass going on") expects the same for controller moves.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. In the model:

let mut r = Recorder::new(BAR, Some(bars_2_3), HeldControls::default());
r.push(on(B + 100, 0, 60)); r.push(off(B + 200, 0, 60));
r.close_laps(0);                     // laps = [pass 0], all closed
r.push(on(2 * B - 20, 0, 65));       // late key of pass 0
r.push(off(2 * B - 10, 0, 65));
r.push(on(B + 10, 1, 64)); r.push(off(B + 60, 1, 64));
r.close_laps(1)                      // gives 2 takes: [65 alone], [64]; expected 1 take [64, 65]

In the app: loop recording with 「重ねる」 on, press a key a few ms before the loop end, play nothing else in the first moment of the next pass.

Cause

close_laps clears the closed passes but leaves them as the last entries of laps. push puts every key into self.laps.len() - 1, and control puts a move whose pass is closed into last, without checking that this pass is still open (number >= open_from).

Suggested fix

At the end of close_laps, when the last pass in laps has number <= upto, call self.enter_lap(upto + 1) so that a pass going on exists (it then starts with the held controllers, as any new pass). Then late keys and moves land in it as documented. No protocol or file-shape change. Add the case above to the recorder tests.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-model --lib verify_model_a_004): the sequence of "How to trigger" gives two takes at close_laps(1): [(7660, 10, 65)] and [(10, 50, 64)], where the doc of close_laps and the test closed_passes_are_given_once_and_late_keys_go_on promise one take holding both. The core calls close_laps from Play::pass_over (play.rs:1844) as soon as a heard position of the next pass comes, so the order is reachable.

Correction: the pedal half is smaller than written. A late pedal-up of the closed pass is dropped from that pass, but HeldControls still takes it, so the next pass does not start with the pedal down (my test verify_model_a_004_pedal passed: the head of pass 1 has no CC 64 = 127). What is lost is only the pedal-up point in the last moments of the closed pass's take. Severity low stands.

Fix plan: in crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs close_laps, after the loop, when the last entry of laps has number <= upto, call self.enter_lap(upto + 1). Add the sequence above to record/tests.rs. Check finish afterwards: an empty pass that was entered this way must not give an empty take or count as a pass with notes.

Fix

Keys half fixed, controller half left as it is. crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs Recorder::push: a key pressed late for an ended pass first enters the pass going on (enter_lap(open_from)), so it goes there also when nothing of that pass came before the close. The verifier's plan (enter_lap(upto + 1) at the end of close_laps) was not used: it writes the next pass's head while a pedal-up of the ended pass may still be on its way, so a late pedal-up would leave the whole next pass with the pedal down (Fable agreed). Controller moves are as before: with no pass going on yet, a late move stays with the ended pass and its point is lost, while the next pass starts from the moved value; with a pass going on, the move lands there at its tick and that pass's head keeps the old value (asserted as meant in a_pedal_of_a_pass_goes_there_though_notes_of_the_next_pass_came_first). Which of these is right is left to the lead (docs/bugs/later/FIX-fix-model-a.md).

Test: record::tests::late_keys_go_on_when_nothing_of_the_next_pass_came_first (meloet-model; also checks that a late pedal-up does not leave the next pass with the pedal down at its head).

MODEL-A-005lowbugfixedRecorded takes keep notes of one tick in release order, so an edit that changes nothing still makes a step

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-A-005-recorded-takes-keep-notes-of-one-tick-in-release.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs:301 (Recorder::take: notes.sort_by_key(|n| n.0), by start only; lap.notes is filled in the order keys are let go, record.rs:359)
crates/meloet-model/src/notes.rs:873 and 876-885 (put_take_reaching sorts the new notes by (start, key), then calls the edit Unchanged only when they equal the old notes in the old order)

What happens

A recorded take holds the notes that start on one tick in the order the keys were let go (stable sort by start only). A chord whose key events reach the PC together (one tick is 0.26 ms at 120.00; events of one USB MIDI packet are read microseconds apart) can get one tick for all of them; the arpeggio and 「さっきの演奏を残す」 go through the same Recorder. Then C-E-G released G, E, C is stored G, E, C.

Every note edit builds its new notes and sorts them by (start, key) (C, E, G), then compares them with the old notes in the take's order (G, E, C). They differ, so an edit that changes no note is not Unchanged: it makes a new take and an undo step that does nothing to the song. Examples: 「ベロシティをまとめて変える」 「割合」 100 %, 「次の音までのばす」 when the notes already reach the next start, typing the velocity a note already has, a velocity step up at 127. (A quantized take is already sorted, so it is only a recorded take not yet edited.) The person sees a history row (「ベロシティを割合で変える」 …) and needs one more Mod Z.

What should happen

Architecture 13: "An action that changes nothing makes no step"; architecture 25-10: for the velocity and length tools "no change → no step". Architecture 16: "a take's notes are ordered 'start → key → earlier first' (stable sort)". Every other maker of keys takes follows that order (write_in_new_clip notes.rs:354, join_clips arrange.rs:592, overdub_recording record/overdub.rs:127, put_take_reaching).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Record a chord whose notes get the same tick and are let go top key first (or any recorded take with two notes on one tick, higher key first).
  2. Select the chord, 「ベロシティをまとめて変える」 → 「割合」 100 → apply.
  3. A step 「ベロシティを割合で変える」 appears in the history; the notes are unchanged.

Cause

Recorder::take sorts by start only, so the take breaks the (start, key) order the edits assume; put_take_reaching compares the sorted new list with the unsorted old list.

Suggested fix

In Recorder::take, sort by (start, key) (notes.sort_by_key(|n| (n.0, n.2)), stable), as the other makers of takes do. Optionally also sort live_notes the same way so the live picture matches. No protocol or file-shape change; takes already saved keep their order and behave as now.

Verification

Confirmed by a test I ran (scratch worktree, cargo test -p meloet-model --lib verify_model_a_005): three keys pressed on one tick and let go top first give a take with keys [67, 64, 60]. put_take_reaching (notes.rs:873-885) sorts the new notes by (start, key) and compares them with the old notes in the take's order, so for such a take an edit that changes nothing is not Unchanged (read, not run end to end). Severity low stands: it needs two keys on the same tick, and the cost is one empty history row.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs Recorder::take: notes.sort_by_key(|n| (n.0, n.2)). Test in record/tests.rs (the order of a same-tick chord). Leave live_notes alone unless the screen's indexes into a live take must match the final take; check that while fixing.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/record.rs Recorder::take: the notes are sorted by (start, key) (stable). live_notes is left as it is (only drawn; no intent points into it).

Test: record::tests::notes_of_one_tick_are_in_the_order_of_key (meloet-model; a chord let go top first gives keys [60, 64, 67], and change_velocity by 0 is Unchanged).

MODEL-B-003lowbugfixedTyped effect values read full-width digits but not full-width unit letters, so 500ms in full width becomes 500 s

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-B-003-typed-effect-values-read-full-width-digits-but-n.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/effects.rs:122-130 (ParamSpec::read: only ０-９, −, －, ＋, ． are turned into ASCII)
crates/meloet-model/src/effects.rs:146-152 (the unit is matched against ASCII ms, s, k only)
ui/src/components/params/ParamRow.svelte:174 (the screen sends the typed text as it is)

What happens

Typing into a built-in effect's value box with the Japanese input method in full-width mode gives full-width letters for the unit. 「５００ｍｓ」 in the reverb's 「響きの長さ」 (seconds) reads as 500 (the unit 「ｍｓ」 is not seen), is clamped to 10 s, and the status line says the value was put at the end of its range. 「１.５ｓ」 in the compressor's attack (ms) becomes 1.5 ms instead of 1500 ms, and 「２ｋＨｚ」 in an EQ frequency becomes 2 Hz (clamped to the lowest). The same full-width text without the unit, or with an ASCII unit, reads right.

What should happen

The unit is followed whatever width it is typed in: docs/architecture.md 20-13 ("Typing for built-in effects ... converting 「500 ms」, 「1.5 s」, 「2 kHz」"), and read already takes full-width digits, signs and the full-width point (its own comment: "１２０"). The screen's other typed fields normalize the whole text with NFKC first (ui/src/components/valueClamp.ts:23, ui/src/components/time.ts:64, ui/src/components/params/clipFields.ts:23), so full-width letters work there.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put a reverb on a track, open the params panel, double-click 「響きの長さ」.
  2. With the input method on (full-width alphanumerics), type 500ms and press Enter: the box holds 「５００ｍｓ」, sent as TypeParam { text }.
  3. Play::type_param (crates/meloet-core/src/play/mixer.rs:498) calls spec.read: the number is 500, unit is 「ｍｓ」 (lower case of full-width is still full-width), starts_with("ms") is false, scale 1. The value becomes 10.0 s (clamped) with ParamClamped.

Cause
