The character map in read covers digits and a few signs but not the full-width Latin letters U+FF21..U+FF3A / U+FF41..U+FF5A, so unit.starts_with("ms"), starts_with('s') and starts_with('k') never match a full-width unit.

Suggested fix

In the character map of ParamSpec::read, also turn full-width Latin letters ('Ａ'..='Ｚ', 'ａ'..='ｚ') into their ASCII letters (subtract 0xFEE0), or have the screen send typed.normalize("NFKC") as its sibling fields do (that would also cover plugin values typed in the same box). No protocol or song-file change; a case like 「５００ｍｓ」 in a_typed_value_reads_with_or_without_its_unit would watch it.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). ParamSpec::read maps only full-width digits, −, －, ＋ and ． to ASCII; the unit is compared with ASCII ms, s, k after to_lowercase(), which leaves full-width letters full-width. ParamRow.svelte:174 sends the typed text as it is. So 「５００ｍｓ」 for a parameter in seconds reads as 500. Severity low stands (needs the input method in full-width alphanumerics and a typed unit of another scale).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-model/src/effects.rs ParamSpec::read: add 'Ａ'..='Ｚ' | 'ａ'..='ｚ' to the character map (to the ASCII letter, code − 0xFEE0), and the full-width space '\u{3000}' to a plain space while there. Rust side only, so the AI link's typed values get it too. Cases 「５００ｍｓ」, 「１．５ｓ」, 「２ｋＨｚ」 in the test a_typed_value_reads_with_or_without_its_unit.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/effects.rs ParamSpec::read: full-width Latin letters (Ａ-Ｚ, ａ-ｚ) become their ASCII letters before the unit is read (Rust side, so the AI link's typed values get it too). The full-width space of the plan needed no line: trim and trim_start already take it as a space (the case 「５００　ＭＳ」 shows it). Test: cases 「５００ｍｓ」, 「５００　ＭＳ」, 「１．５ｓ」, 「２ｋＨｚ」 added to effects::tests::a_typed_value_reads_with_or_without_its_unit.

MODEL-B-004lowbugfixedA vocal track accepts lanes of the built-in sound knobs, which move nothing

docs/bugs/reports/MODEL-B-004-a-vocal-track-accepts-lanes-of-the-built-in-soun.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-model/src/automation.rs:163-167 (Track::has_target, AutoTarget::Instrument on a track whose instrument is not a plugin)
crates/meloet-model/src/knobs.rs:84-91 (knob_usable does not look at the track's kind)

What happens

add_lane(doc, vocal_track, AutoTarget::Instrument { param: 0..=3 }, …) succeeds: a vocal track gets a lane of 「明るさ」, 「立ち上がり」, 「のび」 or 「ゆれ」, with an undo step 「帯を足す」 and a lane row in the arrange. Its points are sent to the vocal track's seat as knob values, but a vocal track has no sound that uses knobs, so the lane changes nothing heard. Doc::check_lanes also accepts such a lane (from a song file too). The AI link reaches this: add_lane with target instrument and param 0..=3 on a vocal track is not refused (crates/meloet-ai/src/tools/automation.rs:137-143 → Command::AddLane → Play::add_lane, crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:508-521; the AI's dry run in crates/meloet-core/src/play/ai.rs:420-430 also says yes), so the AI is told it worked.

What should happen

has_target's own comment: the track "has what target moves: a plugin as its instrument, a knob its sound that comes with meloet uses". A vocal track has no instrument: crates/meloet-model/src/audio.rs:14-15 ("Track::instrument of one is the sine wave, which nothing reads"). Knobs belong to built-in sounds of keys tracks (docs/architecture.md 25-27; Play::set_sound_knob only acts on a track whose sound is the one named, and the screen shows knobs only for keys tracks, crates/meloet-core/src/play/mixer.rs:598). So has_target(Instrument { .. }) should be false on a vocal track, and the AI should get "no such lane" (AiRefusal::NoSuchLane for NoSuchInsert, crates/meloet-core/src/play/ai.rs:451).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. With the AI link on: add a vocal track, then call the tool add_lane with {"track": <the vocal track>, "target": "instrument", "param": 0}. The song gets a 「明るさ」 lane on the vocal track; put_points on it also works; nothing in the sound changes.

Cause

For AutoTarget::Instrument, has_target asks only knob_usable(&self.instrument, knob). A vocal track's instrument is InstrumentRef::SINE, a built-in sound, and knob_usable(SINE, _) is true for every knob, so the track kind is never looked at.

Suggested fix

In Track::has_target, return false for AutoTarget::Instrument { .. } when self.kind.is_vocal() (and, for the same reason, let knobs::set_sound_knob refuse a vocal track). The song file reader already asks has_target for every lane and leaves out one without a target (crates/meloet-core/src/songfile.rs:684), so a file that holds such a lane still opens, without it. No protocol or song-file shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Track::has_target(Instrument { param }) looks only at self.instrument; a vocal track's instrument is InstrumentRef::SINE ("which nothing reads"), so params 0 to 3 pass. auto::add_lane (automation.rs:323) and the AI's dry run (play/ai.rs:422-430) both ask only has_lane_target, so the AI link's add_lane with instrument on a vocal track makes a lane and is told it worked. The screen does not offer knob lanes on a vocal track, so a person reaches it only through the AI link or a song file. Severity low stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-model/src/automation.rs Track::has_target: AutoTarget::Instrument { .. } is false when self.kind.is_vocal(). Then add_lane gives NoSuchInsert (the AI gets NoSuchLane), and the song file reader leaves such a lane out. Test in the automation tests (add_lane on a vocal track is refused). Do not touch knobs::set_sound_knob unless it is reachable for a vocal track; check while fixing.

Fix

crates/meloet-model/src/automation.rs Track::has_target: AutoTarget::Instrument is false on a vocal track. add_lane then gives NoSuchInsert (the AI's add_lane is answered NoSuchLane), check_lanes refuses such a lane, and the song file reader leaves it out, so a file that holds one still opens without it. knobs::set_sound_knob was not touched: the screen offers no knobs for a vocal track (picked_at in play/mixer.rs) and no AI tool sets knobs. Tests: automation::tests::a_vocal_track_has_no_lanes_of_an_instrument (model) and songfile::vocal_tests::a_lane_of_an_instrument_on_a_vocal_track_is_left_out (core: the file opens, the lane is left out, the volume lane stays).

PLUG-002lowbugfixedScan stamp of a plugin bundle that is a symbolic link never changes, so a changed or removed linked plugin is never scanned again

docs/bugs/reports/PLUG-002-scan-stamp-of-a-plugin-bundle-that-is-a-symbolic.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-plugin/src/scan.rs:127-155 (BundleStamp::read / add: std::fs::symlink_metadata on the bundle itself)
crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/scanner.rs:252-253 (bundles_in: a bundle is found by the ending of its name, a link included)
crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/scanner.rs:269-276 (places_todo: an unchanged stamp leaves the record as it is)

What happens

A bundle in a plugin place that is itself a symbolic link (for example ~/.vst3/Foo.vst3 -> ~/Downloads/Foo/Foo.vst3, or ~/.lv2/foo.lv2 -> /opt/foo/foo.lv2) is found and scanned once, but its stamp is only the link's own modified time and the length of the link text. Whatever happens to the folder the link points to, the stamp stays the same, so:

  • after the person updates the plugin in place (new plugins in the bundle, a plugin removed, a new version with other parameters or another kind), meloet keeps the old record: new plugins never reach the shelf, removed ones stay on it;
  • after the target is deleted (the link now points nowhere), symlink_metadata still succeeds on the link, bundles_in still finds the name, and the plugin stays on the shelf; choosing it fails to load.

Only 「もう一度スキャン」 of a stopped plugin or deleting the scan cache would read it again.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 20-4: "Results are remembered by path, modified time and size; later scans look only at changed bundles." A bundle whose contents changed must count as changed. The note "Links are not followed" (scan.rs:126) is about links inside the bundle while walking it; the bundle the scan actually reads through the link is the target folder.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Put a plugin folder somewhere and link it into a place: ln -s /opt/x/Foo.vst3 ~/.vst3/Foo.vst3. Start meloet; it is scanned.
  2. Replace the contents of /opt/x/Foo.vst3 with another version that adds a second plugin class (or delete /opt/x/Foo.vst3).
  3. Start meloet again: BundleStamp::read(~/.vst3/Foo.vst3) gives the same stamp (symlink_metadata of the link: not a dir, size = link length, link mtime), places_todo returns None, the old record stays.

Cause

BundleStamp::add calls std::fs::symlink_metadata(path) also for the first path, the bundle. For a link that is not a directory, so the walk stops there and the stamp is the link's own. bundles_in accepts a link by its name ending without looking at what it is.

Suggested fix

Read the bundle itself with std::fs::metadata (following a link at the top only) and keep symlink_metadata for what is inside, for example by passing a flag for the first level in BundleStamp::read. A dangling link then gives an error, and places_todo would leave its old record in place, so it must also count as gone: in bundles_in (scanner.rs, another area) skip an entry whose std::fs::metadata fails, which puts it in gone. No protocol or file-shape change; SCAN_CACHE_VERSION need not change (only links' stamps differ, and they then read as changed once).

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). BundleStamp::add calls std::fs::symlink_metadata on the bundle path itself; for a bundle that is a symbolic link this is the link (not a directory), so the stamp is the link's own modified time and the length of its text. bundles_in (scanner.rs:245-256) takes the entry by its name ending, link or not, and places_todo returns None for an unchanged stamp. A changed target is not scanned again, and a link that points nowhere still stamps the same and stays on the shelf. Severity low stands (only linked bundles; 「もう一度スキャン」 is not offered for a plugin that did not stop).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-plugin/src/scan.rs BundleStamp::read: read the bundle itself with std::fs::metadata (follow the link at the top only), keep symlink_metadata below it. crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/scanner.rs bundles_in: pass over an entry whose std::fs::metadata fails (a link to nowhere), so its record goes with the bundles that are gone. Tests: scan.rs (a linked bundle's stamp changes when a file in the target changes) and the scanner's tests (a dangling link is not a bundle). SCAN_CACHE_VERSION stays; linked bundles are scanned once more after the change.

Fix

crates/meloet-plugin/src/scan.rs BundleStamp::read: the bundle itself is read with std::fs::metadata (a link is followed at the top only); what is in it is read with symlink_metadata as before. crates/meloet-core/src/plugins/scanner.rs bundles_in: an entry with a bundle's name that points nowhere is passed over, so its record goes with the bundles that are gone. SCAN_CACHE_VERSION stays.

Tests: scan::tests::a_bundle_that_is_a_link_is_stamped_by_what_it_points_to (meloet-plugin), plugins::scanner::tests::a_bundle_that_is_a_link_is_found_while_it_points_somewhere (meloet-core).

PLUG-003lowbugfixedVST3: SetParam can answer with the value set before, because param() replays older audio-thread values over the new one

docs/bugs/reports/PLUG-003-vst3-setparam-can-answer-with-the-value-set-befo.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-plughost/src/control.rs:809-824 (Request::SetParam: controller.set_param, then audio.set_param, then c.param(id) for the answer)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs:1410-1414 (Vst3Controller::param calls hear_audio() after the value was set)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs:1167-1176 (hear_audio applies every queued value, oldest first, with setParamNormalized)
crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs:987-997 (every block pushes the last value of each Param event, also the ones meloet set, to to_controller)

What happens

For a VST3 plugin, SetParam {value: v2} sets v2 on the controller, then reads the answer with param(). param() first runs hear_audio(), which empties the ring from the audio thread and calls setParamNormalized with each value in it. If a block has processed the previous SetParam {value: v1} for the same parameter since the main thread last emptied the ring, the ring still holds (id, v1), and it is applied over v2. The answer then says v1 (value and text), and the plugin's controller, and its window if open, shows v1 until the next block's echo of v2 is heard (a block plus up to 10 ms later).

The answer goes to the params panel row (param_now in crates/meloet-core/src/play/params.rs, the dragged row's value and text) and to an AI set. During a drag in the panel the row's text jumps back one step now and then; when the mouse is let go right after the last move, the commit: true answer can be the value before, and nothing reads the row again afterwards, so the panel keeps showing a value one step off from what the plugin has.

What should happen

docs/architecture.md 20-12: "Even false returns value and text in paramValues (the plugin writes the dragged row's text)"; crates/meloet-plugin/src/wire.rs:89-90: SetParam is "Answered by Param: the value as the plugin took it, with its text." The value just set is the newest; values the audio thread reports from before it must not be put over it. LV2 does not have this: Lv2Controller::param reads the value kept in Shared and replays nothing (lv2.rs:2233-2236).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. It needs an engine block longer than the host main thread's 10 ms tick (TICK, control.rs:33), so it shows with a quantum of 512 frames and more (about 30 % of the steps of a drag at 1024 frames / 48 kHz), rarely at 256.

  1. Load a VST3 effect or instrument (ZamComp, Surge XT) and pick it so its parameters show in the params panel.
  2. Drag a continuous parameter in the panel; the screen sends SetPluginParam {commit: false} about every 16.7 ms, then commit: true on release.
  3. When the block that took v1 ends after the main loop's last poll and before SetParam {v2} arrives, the answer to v2 carries v1.

Cause

hear_audio is meant to bring the controller up to date with what the audio thread gave the processor (automation). It does not know that the main thread has set a newer value for the same parameter after those values were queued, and param() calls it after set_param. The ring holds echoes of meloet's own SetParam values too (the block's told loop takes every ToPlugin::Param).

Suggested fix

Answer SetParam from the controller without replaying the ring after the value was set: have Vst3Controller::set_param call self.hear_audio() before setParamNormalized, and read the answer with a form of param() that does not call hear_audio() (a flag or a small separate method used only by the SetParam arm). An echo of v1 from a block that was running at that moment can still pass over the controller later for a moment, but the answer and the panel row are right, and the echo of v2 follows. No protocol or file-shape change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Request::SetParam (control.rs:804-824) calls controller.set_param(id, v2) (vst3.rs:1459, a plain setParamNormalized), then answers with c.param(id), and Vst3Controller::param (vst3.rs:1410-1414) runs hear_audio() first, which applies every (id, value) the audio thread queued (vst3.rs:987-997 queues the last value of every ToPlugin::Param of a block, meloet's own included). An echo of the value set before, still in the ring, lands over v2 and is what the answer reads. LV2's param reads the kept value and replays nothing. Severity low stands (needs a block that ends between the host's 10 ms poll and the next SetParam; the row is one step off until the next reading).

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs: in Vst3Controller::set_param call self.hear_audio() before setParamNormalized, and give the SetParam arm an answer that does not replay the ring after the set (a private param_now(id) without hear_audio, used by param() after its own hear_audio()); crates/meloet-plughost/src/control.rs uses it through the controller trait only if the trait needs a new method, else keep the change inside vst3.rs by remembering "just set" for the next param call. Test with the fake VST3-like controller if one exists in control.rs tests; else a unit test of the order in vst3.rs.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-plughost/src/formats/vst3.rs: Vst3Controller::set_param hears the ring first (values from before land before the new one), sets the value and keeps its id (just_set); the next hear_audio (the one inside the param() that answers SetParam) passes over that parameter's values in the ring, which were given before the set (a block that ran with the value before while Handle::set_param waited for the seat). Other parameters are heard as before. The trait and control.rs are unchanged.
  • Test: formats::vst3::tests::a_value_set_is_answered_over_the_one_before_it_heard_from_the_audio_thread (#[ignore], needs Surge XT (VST3)); written but not run in this fix (the request said not to run ignored tests). There is no fake VST3 controller to test it without a real plugin.
SOUND-001lowbugfixedElectro kit envelopes decay into denormal numbers and stay there for the rest of the hit

docs/bugs/reports/SOUND-001-electro-kit-envelopes-decay-into-denormal-number.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-sound/src/synth/kit.rs:271-276 (Voice::tick: env[0..3] *= coef[..] with no flush)
crates/meloet-sound/src/synth/kit.rs:439 (render: the voice ends only on env[0] + env[1] < SILENT)
crates/meloet-sound/src/synth/kit.rs:125, 141, 180-185 (the short sub-envelopes: click ring(0.0015), pitch dc(0.030) / dc(0.012), cymbal hit ring(0.06) / ring(0.025))

What happens

On the audio thread, inside the per-sample loop of the electro kit, some sub-envelopes of a hit (the click of kick and toms, the pitch fall of kick, toms and snare, the hit part of crash and ride) keep being multiplied by their decay factor after they are far below anything audible. They pass under the smallest normal f32 (1.18e-38) and then get stuck at a denormal number that never reaches 0: once x * coef is less than half a step away from x, rounding gives x back. Checked with numpy f32 at 48 kHz (computed, not run in meloet):

Sub-envelopecoefdenormal fromstuck at
kick / tom click, ring(0.0015)0.986210.13 s5e-44
snare pitch, dc(0.012)0.998271.05 s4.0e-43
kick pitch, dc(0.030)0.999312.62 s1.0e-42
ride hit, ring(0.025)0.999172.18 s8.4e-43
crash hit, ring(0.06)0.999655.24 s2.0e-42

The voice itself lives on, because it ends only when env[0] + env[1] < 1e-4, and env[0] rings longer: kick to 1.84 s, toms to 1.5–2.0 s, snare (velocity 127) to 1.29 s, ride to 5.1 s, crash (velocity 127) to 9.2 s, all four times longer with 「のび」 at 100 %. So for every kick that rings past 0.13 s (on a beat of 0.5 s that is 0.37 s of each beat), every crash that rings past 5.2 s, every ride hit that rings past 2.2 s, each sample does several operations on denormal numbers (env[k] *= coef[k], then rng.bipolar() * env[1] * click or hit * env[1]). On CPUs that slow down on denormals this is tens to hundreds of cycles each.

What should happen

CLAUDE.md rules: "Stop NaN where signals are summed, and set denormal small values to 0 in loops (do not set FTZ or DAZ)". docs/architecture.md 19-2: "Each effect and instrument guards denormals itself (values below 1e-18 in feedback and filter state become 0; envelopes stop at −80 dB)". The sibling sound makers do it: the sampler ends every envelope at SILENT (sampler/player.rs:176, 181, 190), the SF2 filter flushes at TINY (sampler/shape.rs:339), the effects call dsp::flush.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Put the electro kit on a track, play one kick (key 36) or one crash (key 49) and let it ring. From 0.13 s after the kick (5.2 s after the crash) every sample of that voice works on denormal values until the voice ends. A song with a crash at the start of each phrase or a kick on every beat does this all the time.

Cause

Voice::tick multiplies the three envelopes every sample and never sets a tiny value to 0 (kit.rs:271-276). Only env[0] + env[1] decides when the voice ends (kit.rs:439), so a short sub-envelope (env[1] of a kick, env[2] of a kick or snare, env[1] of crash and ride) keeps decaying long after it is silent. The test denormals_stay_finite_and_small covers only the effects; nothing watches the kit.

Suggested fix

In Voice::tick, after the three multiplies, set each env[k] below about 1e-18 to 0 (the same flush the effects use, crate::effects::dsp::flush, or a local copy). No change of looks, wording, protocol or file shape. The sound changes only by values far under 1e-38 in single samples; the stored hashes of the knob tests (architecture 25-27, "hashing the output of all 31 built-in sounds") may need to be looked at in case one such tiny value was in them.

Verification

Confirmed by reading and by a calculation I ran (python with numpy float32, the same multiply as Voice::tick at 48 kHz): the click envelope (ring(0.0015), coef 0.9862071) is denormal from 0.131 s and sticks at 5e-44 from 0.149 s; the kick's pitch envelope (dc(0.030), coef 0.9993058) is denormal from 2.62 s and sticks at 1.009e-42. These match the report's table. Voice::tick (kit.rs:271-276) has no flush, and the voice ends only on env[0] + env[1] < SILENT (kit.rs:439), so the short envelopes stay denormal while env[0] rings. I did not measure the CPU cost in meloet; the fault is against the written rule (CLAUDE.md: "set denormal small values to 0 in loops"; architecture 19-2), and the cost depends on the CPU. Severity low stands.

Corrections: none.

Fix plan: crates/meloet-sound/src/synth/kit.rs Voice::tick: after the multiplies, set each env[k] below 1e-18 to 0 (use the flush of crate::effects::dsp if it is reachable from synth, else a two-line local one). Add the kit to the denormal test (denormals_stay_finite_and_small or a new test next to the kit's tests: after 3 s of a kick, env[1] and env[2] are exactly 0). Run the hash tests of the built-in sounds after the change; update a stored hash only if the kit's changed and only after checking the difference is below 1e-18.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-sound/src/synth/kit.rs Voice::tick: after the multiplies, each envelope below 1e-18 is set to 0 (effects::dsp::flush).
  • Test: synth::kit::tests::no_envelope_rings_on_below_hearing (all twelve pieces at velocity 127 for 10 s: every envelope of a voice that rings is 0 or at least 1e-18). No stored hash changed (tests/knobs.rs and the other kit tests pass as they were).
SOUND-002lowbugfixedSF2 modulation envelope jumps when a key is let go during its attack

docs/bugs/reports/SOUND-002-sf2-modulation-envelope-jumps-when-a-key-is-let.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

crates/meloet-sound/src/sampler/shape.rs:222-227 (Adsr::release_amount)
crates/meloet-sound/src/sampler/shape.rs:198-208 (Adsr::amount, the convex attack it should continue from)

What happens

For an SF2 zone whose modulation envelope has an attack (attackModEnv longer than one frame) and moves the pitch or the filter (modEnvToPitch, modEnvToFilterFc), letting the key go before that attack is over makes the pitch or the filter cutoff jump at once.

During the attack amount() is the convex curve 1 + 400/960 · log10(rise). On release, release_amount() sets fall = 1 - rise, so the next amount() is 1 - fall = rise, the straight-line value. The two are not the same (computed, not run):

rise at key upamount beforeamount afterjump with modEnvToFilterFc = 2400 cents
0.100.5830.10−1160 cents
0.250.7490.25−1198 cents
0.500.8750.50−899 cents
0.750.9480.75−475 cents

The filter cutoff drops by about an octave inside one 32-frame chunk (the filter numbers are moved in a straight line over that chunk, Filter::run), and with modEnvToPitch the pitch drops by the same share of its cents in one chunk: heard as a click or a sudden darker or lower sound at the key up.

What should happen

The release goes on from where the envelope was. The volume envelope right beside it does so: release_level() (shape.rs:214-220) turns the attack's level into the matching fall, with the comment "The release goes on from the loudness the attack had reached". Architecture 20-7 says playback is to match FluidSynth; from memory (not checked in its source in this sweep) FluidSynth also turns the convex attack value into the matching linear one at note-off, so the verifier may want to read fluid_voice.c to be sure.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Load an SF2 preset that has a modulation envelope with an attack of, say, 0.5 s and modEnvToFilterFc of a few thousand cents (a swept synth brass or pad). Press a key and let it go after 0.1–0.3 s: the cutoff falls by about an octave at the key up instead of going down smoothly from where it was.

Cause

release_amount uses 1 - rise, the value of a straight-line attack, while amount() gives the convex value during the attack. The jump is convex(rise) - rise.

Suggested fix

In release_amount, when rising(), set self.fall = 1.0 - self.amount() (taken before seg changes), so the first released value equals the last attack value. Sound only; no protocol, file shape, wording or looks change. A test like the_modulation_envelope_moves_the_pitch in crates/meloet-sound/tests/sf2.rs with a key up in the attack would watch it.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (no test run). Adsr::amount() gives the convex value 1 + 400/960 · log10(rise) during the attack; release_amount() sets fall = 1 - rise, so the first value after the key up is rise, the straight-line value: at rise = 0.25 the amount goes from 0.749 to 0.25 at once. I checked the report's table by hand (0.10 → 0.583, 0.50 → 0.875).

What I relied on: (1) the neighbour release_level() in the same file, which turns the attack's level into the matching fall ("The release goes on from the loudness the attack had reached"); (2) the SF2 text for generator 30 releaseModEnv, as I remember it (not read from a file in this job, so confidence C): "the release phase linearly ramps toward zero from the current level", which means the level the envelope had, not the straight-line one; (3) FluidSynth, also from memory (confidence C): at note-off inside the modulation envelope's attack it turns the convex value into the linear one so the release starts from the same value. All three point the same way; no primary source was opened.

Corrections: none. Severity low stands (needs an SF2 preset with a modulation-envelope attack and a key let go inside it).

Fix plan: crates/meloet-sound/src/sampler/shape.rs release_amount: when rising(), self.fall = 1.0 - self.amount() (read before seg changes). Test in crates/meloet-sound/tests/sf2.rs next to the_modulation_envelope_moves_the_pitch, or a unit test of Adsr: amount() just before and just after release_amount() in the attack are equal.

Fix

  • crates/meloet-sound/src/sampler/shape.rs Adsr::release_amount: in the attack, fall = 1 - amount() (the convex value), so the release starts where the attack was.
  • Test: sampler::shape::tests::the_modulation_release_goes_on_from_the_attack. No SF2 test expectation changed (tests/sf2.rs, tests/sf2_fluidr3.rs pass as they were).
UI-ARRANGE-006lowparityfixedMarker and tempo point drags with Shift show an unsnapped place, but Rust always snaps them to the beat

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-006-marker-and-tempo-point-drags-with-shift-show-an.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/timeline/MarkerRow.svelte:67-68 (toTick = e.shiftKey ? Math.round(at) : nearestBeatTick(map, at))
ui/src/components/timeline/tempoGeom.ts:252-258 (dragPoint: shift ? Math.round(at) : nearestBeatTick(map, at))
crates/meloet-core/src/play/timeline.rs:427-434 (move_marker: let tick = self.snapped(tick), always)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/timeline.rs:262-286 (move_tempo_point: self.snapped(to_tick), always, when the point moves)

What happens

While a marker tag or a tempo point is dragged with Shift held, the screen does not snap: the tag or point follows the pointer tick by tick and the reading in place shows that position (for example 「9. 2. 0480」). On release the intent carries that tick, but Rust snaps it to the nearest beat, so the marker or point jumps to the beat the person did not pick. Shift does nothing for these two drags, though the screen says it does.

What should happen

The screen and Rust should agree. Spec 4 lists "While dragging, do not snap to the grid: Shift + drag — Works" for every drag; spec 3 (Y18, Y17) says marker and tempo point moves snap to the beat. Either Rust takes the tick as sent when the person held Shift, or the screen does not offer Shift for these two drags (always snaps), so what is shown is what is put. The other drags of the arrange agree on both sides (clip moves: Rust uses the sent amount; automation point moves: Rust uses the sent dTick).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Open 「テンポ ・ 拍子の変更」, hold Shift and drag a tempo point sideways a little off a beat; the reading shows the off-beat place; release: the point lands on the nearest beat. The same with a marker tag in the marker row.

Cause

move_marker and move_tempo_point call snapped() whatever the screen sent, while the screen leaves out snapping with Shift.

Suggested fix

Needs a decision by the lead. (a) Drop the Shift branch on the screen (always nearestBeatTick), matching what Rust does today; no protocol change. Or (b) let these intents carry the "no snap" wish (a protocol field such as snap: bool on MoveMarker and MoveTempoPoint) and skip snapped() when it is false (architecture 21-15 says "the core snaps again by the same rule"; that line would change).

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). The screen leaves out snapping with Shift (MarkerRow.svelte:68, tempoGeom.ts:255), Rust always snaps (play/timeline.rs move_marker: let tick = self.snapped(tick); move_tempo_point the same; snapped = nearest beat).

Which side to follow is decided by the documents (so not needs-lead): Rust is right, the screen changes.

  • spec 3 (Y17): "A tempo can change at the start of a beat."
  • spec 3 (Y18): "Move: drag sideways (snaps to the beat; the reading is the position)."
  • architecture 21-15: "Snapping to beats is done by both: the screen snaps with the map (nextBeat) for the look while dragging, and the core snaps again by the same rule (nearest beat; at exactly half, the later)."
  • spec 4's row "While dragging, do not snap to the grid — Shift + drag" speaks of the grid; these two drags do not use the grid but the beat rule above. 7-readings has no line that gives Shift to marker or tempo point drags (A-20), while A-21 gives it to automation points in words ("The start of a beat. With Shift, ticks.").

Fix plan (choice (a)): drop the Shift branch in ui/src/components/timeline/MarkerRow.svelte:68 and in dragPoint of ui/src/components/timeline/tempoGeom.ts:255 (always nearestBeatTick), and fix the comment above dragPoint ("ticks with Shift"). Check ui/tests/ for a test that expects the Shift form (tempoGeom tests) and change it. No protocol change.

Fix

Not committed yet (the lead commits).

  • ui/src/components/timeline/MarkerRow.svelte: a marker drag always snaps to the nearest beat (the Shift branch is gone).
  • ui/src/components/timeline/tempoGeom.ts: dragPoint always snaps to the nearest beat; its shift parameter and the "ticks with Shift" comment are gone; TempoBand.svelte no longer passes it.

Test: ui/tests/round6.timeline.test.ts — "with Shift, a tempo point and a marker still go to the start of a beat, as core puts them (spec 3 Y17, Y18; architecture 21-15)". The shape test "drag: sideways to beat starts, …" only lost the false argument.

UI-ARRANGE-007lowparityfixedAdding an automation point: the screen snaps to the beat, Rust snaps again to the piano roll's grid and ignores Shift

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-007-adding-an-automation-point-the-screen-snaps-to-t.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/automation/autoGeom.ts:348-352 (addTick: the start of the beat; the tick as it is with Shift)
ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:109-115 (addReading: the reading shows that place and the line's value there)
ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte:172-173 (addAutoPoint with that tick)
crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:587-615 (add_auto_point → on_grid: snaps again to session.roll.grid, the piano roll's grid, always)

What happens

Pressing an empty place of an automation lane shows the reading 「位置 ・ 値」 for the start of the beat there (or, with Shift, the exact tick) and sends addAutoPoint with that tick. Rust then puts the point on the piano roll's grid instead. Whenever that grid is coarser than a beat or does not hold the beat (grid 1 bar, 1/2, 1/4 triplet; or x/8 with a 1/4 grid), the point lands somewhere else than the reading said, with a value taken there; and Shift never adds a point between grid steps. Moving points in the same lane follows beats (dragPoints, and Rust uses the sent dTick as it is), so adding and moving in one lane use two different grids.

What should happen

One rule on both sides, so the reading is where the point goes. Spec 3 (Y16): "pressing an empty place in the band adds a point (with the value of the line at the pressed position) … sideways for the position (snaps to the grid)"; spec 4: Shift + drag does not snap. The screen's rule (beats, ticks with Shift) is the one its tests check (ui/tests/automation.test.ts:112, :140).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Set the piano roll's grid to 1 bar (「ノート ▸ グリッド ▸」). In the arrange, add a volume lane and press an empty place of it on beat 3 of bar 5: the reading says 「5. 3. 0000 …」, the point appears on a bar line (bar 5 or 6). Hold Shift and press between beats: the point still lands on the grid.

Cause

add_auto_point always runs on_grid with the piano roll's grid, while the screen already snapped by its own (beat) rule and offers Shift for "no snap".

Suggested fix

Needs a decision by the lead on which grid automation points use. The smallest change that keeps the screen's tested rule: make add_auto_point take the tick as sent (the screen has snapped it), like move_auto_points takes dTick; the doc comment on add_auto_point changes with it. No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). add_auto_point (crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs:587-604) always runs on_grid, which uses self.song.session.roll.grid (the piano roll's grid); the screen has already put the tick on the start of a beat, or left it as it is with Shift (autoGeom.ts:348-352), and shows that place in the reading.

Which side to follow is decided by 7-readings A-21 (so not needs-lead): "Snapping of added and moved points | The start of a beat. With Shift, ticks. | mock R10-AutoEdit". The screen follows it, and move_auto_points already takes the screen's dTick as sent; only add_auto_point differs.

Fix plan: in crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs make add_auto_point take the tick as sent (only min(MAX_TICK)), remove on_grid if nothing else uses it (grep first: the AI link's add may call the same function; if the AI tool relies on the grid, keep the snap on that path only), and change the doc comment of add_auto_point and any Rust test that expects the piano roll's grid. No protocol or wording change.

Fix

Not committed yet (the lead commits).

  • crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation.rs: add_auto_point takes the tick as sent (the model's add_point still clamps it to MAX_TICK), like move_auto_points takes dTick; on_grid is removed (nothing else used it) and the doc comment says so. Other callers checked: the AI link writes points through play/ai.rs (Put), not this intent; the Rust tests that send AddAutoPoint use ticks on beats, except the one below.

Test: crates/meloet-core/src/play/automation/tests.rs lanes_and_points_are_steps_and_the_selection_comes_back_with_undo now expects the point at Q / 4 + 10 as sent (it expected the piano roll's 1/16 grid, Q / 4, before).

Where

What happens

Commit 7120b19 moved the track heads into TrackHeads.svelte and took the .tog, .tog.step, .tog.step:hover and .tog.step:active rules out of Arrange.svelte with them; the switcher buttons stayed in Arrange.svelte with the same class names.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Record two takes on a keyboard track, click the clip: the ◂ and ▸ in the tag above it look like default WebKitGTK buttons.

Cause

Suggested fix

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run; no picture taken). Arrange.svelte:1990, 1996 use class="tog step"; the style block of Arrange.svelte has no .tog or .step rule (grep: none), .tog exists only in TrackHeads.svelte and mixer/Strip.svelte (scoped, they do not reach this markup), and ui/src has no global rule for button other than :focus-visible in components/common.css. git show 7120b19 -- ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte shows the .tog, .tog.step, .tog.step:hover, .tog.step:active rules being removed. So the two buttons have the browser's default button look.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte give the two buttons the classes the piano roll's tag uses (m-plain step, pianoroll/Toolbar.svelte:225-234) with the same small .step rules, or put the removed .tog.step rules back. Compare with mock F-Takes (tools/design/shoot_ui.py). No new colors or sizes. Fix together with UI-ARRANGE-009 (same two buttons).

Fix

ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte: the .tog.step rules are back in the style block (size part.toggle-size, border gray.6, bg gray.1, text gray.8; hover border gray.8 / text gray.10; pressed bg gray.2 / border gray.8), plus the disabled look for UI-ARRANGE-009 (border gray.5, text gray.7). No new values. Not compared with mock F-Takes in a picture (pure CSS, no test).

UI-ARRANGE-010lowparityfixedTime signature flag box: an unreadable bottom number is reported as the top number

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-010-time-signature-flag-box-an-unreadable-bottom-num.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/timeline/TempoBand.svelte:302 (reject={() => send({ type: "rejectValue", field: "meterNum", kept: String(editFlag.num), range: null })})

What happens

In the open 「テンポ ・ 拍子の変更」 band, a time signature flag's box (double press on a flag, or 「拍子を打ちこむ…」) sends rejectValue with field: "meterNum" and the top number for every text it cannot read. When the top number is fine and the bottom is not (「4/x」, 「4/」), the status line says 「拍子の上は数字で打ちます。もとの 4 のままです。」, blaming the top number. ValueInput passes the typed text to reject, but the band ignores it.

What should happen

The transport's time signature box tells which half is wrong: rejectMeter(draft) in ui/src/components/Transport.svelte:303-306 uses meterFieldOf(draft) (bottom when the top is 1 to 12) and keeps that half's number. The flag box should say the same for the same text (spec 6 "Not readable as a number": 「{field}は数字で打ちます。もとの {kept} のままです。」).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Open the band, double press the 4/4 flag, type 「4/x」, press Enter: the status line names 「拍子の上」. The same text in the transport's time signature box names 「拍子の下」.

Cause

The reject callback is written with a fixed field: "meterNum" instead of choosing the field from the typed text.

Suggested fix

Use the typed text as the transport does: reject={(draft) => { const field = meterFieldOf(draft); send({ type: "rejectValue", field, kept: String(field === "meterNum" ? editFlag.num : editFlag.den), range: null }); }} (meterFieldOf is in components/time.ts). No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). TempoBand.svelte:302 sends a fixed field: "meterNum" and ignores the draft; ValueInput.svelte:39 does pass the typed text (reject(draft)); the transport's box uses meterFieldOf(draft) (Transport.svelte:303-306, time.ts:88-92: bottom when the top reads as 1 to 12). Both boxes read with the same readMeter.

Also reported as UI-PARITY-004 (the same line, the same fix; that report was merged into this one and deleted). From it: the header of timeline/ValueInput.svelte says "The look and rules are the same as the transport's number box (NumberField.svelte)", and spec 6 "Not readable as a number" names the field that could not be read and its old value.

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/components/timeline/TempoBand.svelte:302 use the draft, reject={(draft) => { const field = meterFieldOf(draft); send({ type: "rejectValue", field, kept: String(field === "meterNum" ? editFlag.num : editFlag.den), range: null }); }}; add a case to ui/tests/round6.timeline.test.ts (「4/x」 on a flag → field: "meterDen"). No protocol or wording change.

Fix

Not committed yet (the lead commits).

  • ui/src/components/timeline/TempoBand.svelte: the flag box's reject uses the typed text: meterFieldOf(draft) picks the half, and that half's old number is kept (as the transport's box). Also covers the old UI-PARITY-004.

Test: ui/tests/round6.timeline.test.ts — "an unreadable lower number typed on a flag is told as the lower number, as the transport's box does (spec 6)".

What happens

Spec 3 (29-A): 「持ってきている間、入る所に gray.7 の枠」 — the frame shows where the file will go. The comment of dropFrameBox says "A new track … is just below the last row". The bottom of all track blocks is rows.tracksHeight from arrangeRows (automation/autoGeom.ts:96).

UI-ARRANGE-013lowui-uxfixedWhile recording, a drag on the arrange ruler still shows a new loop range that Rust then refuses

docs/bugs/reports/UI-ARRANGE-013-while-recording-a-drag-on-the-arrange-ruler-stil.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:1263-1286 (rulerDown / rulerMove / rulerUp: no check of busy)
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1012-1015 (set_loop: if self.busy() { return; })
crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:928-931 (locate: the same)

What happens

While recording or counting in, a press and drag on the arrange's bar ruler starts a loop range drag as usual: the accent loop mark and the loop lines move to the new range and the heading shows 「ループ a–b」 for it. On release setLoop is sent, core ignores it (busy), no rev comes, and the preview is dropped (rev === before), so the mark jumps back. A plain press sends locate, which core ignores as well. Nothing tells the person why.

What should happen

The other drags of the arrange do not start while recording or counting in: clips only get chosen (Arrange.svelte:1381-1385), markers do not move (MarkerRow.svelte:65, moved = !busy && …), tempo points and flags only get chosen (TempoBand.svelte:99, :167), automation points do not move (AutoLane.svelte:131). The ruler should do the same: no loop preview while busy (architecture 10: actions not taken during the count-in and recording).

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Start recording (R). Drag on the ruler from bar 3 to bar 6: the loop mark shows 3–6 while dragging; release: it goes back to the old range.

Cause

rulerDown starts drag without looking at busy; rulerMove sets preview from it.

Suggested fix

Return early in rulerDown while busy (or do not set preview in rulerMove while busy). The piano roll's ruler has the same handlers (pianoroll/Roll.svelte:1028-1039, outside this area). No protocol or wording change.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). rulerDown / rulerMove (Arrange.svelte:1263-1273) have no busy check, so the preview range is shown; set_loop and locate return at once while busy() (crates/meloet-core/src/play.rs:1012-1015, 928-931), so the reply's rev equals before and the preview is dropped. The other drags of the arrange already refuse to start while busy.

One root with UI-ROLL-008 (which now holds UI-PARITY-005), UI-ARRANGE-009 and UI-PARITY-010 (parts that look usable while recording or counting in though Rust refuses): fix by the same rule, the busy the arrange already has.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte return early in rulerDown while busy (nothing shown, nothing sent); do the same in the piano roll's ruler handlers (ui/src/components/pianoroll/Roll.svelte:1028-1039, covered by UI-ROLL-008's fix if that one is done first). No protocol or wording change.

Fix

Arrange half. ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte rulerDown returns at once while busy (recording or count-in): no loop preview, no setLoop, no locate. The piano roll's ruler (pianoroll/Roll.svelte) is left to the piano roll fixer. Test: ui/tests/arrange.test.ts "while recording or counting in, a drag or a press on the ruler shows no loop range and sends nothing".

UI-BRIDGE-001lowbugfixedPeaks of the other takes of a comped clip are treated as unused and can be let go of while shown

docs/bugs/reports/UI-BRIDGE-001-peaks-of-the-other-takes-of-a-comped-clip-are-tr.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/bridge/peaks.ts:220-227 (songAudioIds: only c.audio.audio, the file of the shown take)
ui/src/bridge/index.ts:150 (peakStore.keepOnly(songAudioIds(...)) on every change of the song section)
ui/src/bridge/peaks.ts:186-201 (PeakStore.keepOnly: what is not in use goes to dropped, kept up to 4 MB, oldest let go of first)

What happens

A vocal clip with 「いいとこ取り」 (round 12 A8) plays, and the arrange draws, a piece of each chosen take, each from its own audio file (waveParts in ui/src/components/arrange/wave.ts:82-99 uses take.audio of AudioClipView.takes). The comp band draws every take of the clip (ui/src/components/comp/CompBand.svelte:65, b.take.audio). None of these files except the shown take's is in songAudioIds, so at every change of the song section their peaks are moved from ready to dropped, as if no clip used them.

peek still finds them in dropped, and WaveBook.sourceFor only reads with peek (it does not revive them), so they stay in dropped and grow old there. When the levels in dropped pass KEEP_DROPPED_PEAK_BYTES (4 MB), the oldest are let go of first, and those are exactly the comp takes that are on the screen. Then WaveBook.trim drops their entries, the pieces of the wave of the comped clip and the rows of the comp band are drawn without a wave until the whole level has been fetched again, and the next song change moves them to dropped again.

What should happen

songAudioIds says it gives "every audio file the song's clips play"; a comped clip plays the files of its comp pieces, and the comp band shows every take of the clip. These files are in use and should stay in ready, the same as the shown take's file. Only files no clip uses should go to dropped (the comment at peaks.ts:91 and the take rule of keepOnly in take.ts: "takes in use and the 16 newest").

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run.

  1. Record a vocal clip with many long takes (for example 8 takes, COMP_ROWS_MAX, of 5 minutes, stereo, 48 kHz) and make a comp of them (「いいとこ取り」), open the comp band.
  2. Zoom the arrange in and out so that the fine peak levels are fetched for every take (waveLevel picks level 0 below about 340 frames a px, level 1 below about 1365). Level 0 is 48000 × 60 / 256 peaks × 2 channels × 4 bytes = 90 KB a minute, so the 40 minutes of takes are 3.6 MB at level 0 and 0.9 MB at level 1: together over 4 MB, before any other file that really went out of use.
  3. Make any change to the song (move a clip, change a volume): each change runs keepOnly. Once dropped is over 4 MB, the oldest comp takes are let go of, and their waves disappear from the comped clip and the comp band until they are fetched again.

Cause

songAudioIds(song, recording) collects c.audio.audio only. AudioClipView.takes[].audio (every take of the clip, which the comp pieces and the comp band read) is not collected, so PeakStore.keepOnly takes those files for unused.

Suggested fix

In songAudioIds, also collect the audio of every entry of c.audio.takes (it holds the shown take too). No protocol, file-shape, wording or look change. A test in ui/tests/bridge.peaks.test.ts with a clip whose audio.takes names other files would watch it.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). songAudioIds (bridge/peaks.ts:220-227) collects only c.audio.audio (its own comment: "the shown take of each clip"); waveParts (arrange/wave.ts:82-99) draws each comp piece from take.audio of a.takes, and the comp band draws every take. PeakStore.keepOnly (186-201) moves every level whose file is not in the list to dropped and lets go of the oldest once dropped is over KEEP_DROPPED_PEAK_BYTES (4 MB). So the files of the other takes of a comped clip are treated as unused on every change of the song part. The numbers of the example (bytes per minute of a level) were not worked again; the fault does not depend on them, only how soon it shows. Low is right: the waves come back after a fetch.

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/bridge/peaks.ts songAudioIds also collects audio of every entry of c.audio.takes (widen the parameter type, update the doc comment); add a case to ui/tests/bridge.peaks.test.ts. No protocol change.

Fix

ui/src/bridge/peaks.ts songAudioIds also collects the audio of every entry of c.audio.takes (parameter type widened, doc comment updated); ui/src/bridge/index.ts unchanged (it already passes the song). Test: ui/tests/bridge.peaks.test.ts "audio the song uses: every take of a comped clip, not only the shown one".

UI-BRIDGE-002lowui-uxfixedIBM Plex Mono 600 is not loaded, so number fields at weight 600 get a made-up bold

docs/bugs/reports/UI-BRIDGE-002-ibm-plex-mono-600-is-not-loaded-so-number-fields.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/design/fonts.ts:6-7 (only @fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/400.css and 500.css are loaded)
The parts that ask for Mono at 600:
ui/src/components/arrange/FadeBox.svelte:102-116 (.fade-field, the box of 「曲の終わりにフェードアウトをつける…」)
ui/src/components/Arrange.svelte:2344-2358 (.repeat-field, the repeat count box of a clip)
ui/src/components/timeline/MarkerRow.svelte:379-393 (.space-field, the box of 「この前に空きを入れる…」)

What happens

The page declares @font-face rules for 'IBM Plex Mono' at 400 and 500 only. The three typed-number boxes above set font-family: var(--font-mono) with font-weight: var(--text-s-weight-600). With no 600 face in the family, the browser takes the 500 face and, because 600 counts as bold, draws a synthesized ("faux") bold of it (CSS Fonts font-synthesis-weight: auto, the default). The digits in these boxes are thicker and smeared compared with the real Plex Mono glyphs used for the numbers at 400 and 500. Any other Mono text that inherits weight 600 from its box gets the same.

What should happen

Spec 1 "Text": numbers are IBM Plex Mono, and 600 is an allowed weight of text.s. CLAUDE.md: the font is IBM Plex from the @fontsource woff2 files as they are. A weight the screen uses should have its real face loaded, as fonts.ts already does for IBM Plex Sans JP 400, 500 and 600.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Open the box of 「曲の終わりにフェードアウトをつける…」, the repeat count box of a clip, or the bars box of 「この前に空きを入れる…」 on a marker, and look at the digits typed in it next to other Mono numbers.

Cause

fonts.ts loads @fontsource/ibm-plex-mono 400 and 500, while parts ask for Mono at 600 (--text-s-weight-600). ui/node_modules/@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/600.css is there but not imported.

Suggested fix

Add import "@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/600.css"; to ui/src/design/fonts.ts (same package and license, OFL-1.1; node licenses/check.mjs should stay green). The other choice, if the lead prefers fewer font files, is to draw those three boxes at 500; that is a look decision.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (the look itself was not seen in the app). Facts checked:

  • ui/src/design/fonts.ts imports @fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/400.css and 500.css only; the package in ui/node_modules/@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono ships 100 to 700 (600.css is there).
  • Three typed-number boxes ask for Mono at 600 (FadeBox.svelte:112-114, Arrange.svelte:2356, MarkerRow.svelte:391); they follow the track name box of spec 2 ("Track name input box | … (background gray.0, border gray.10, weight 600)") with digits in it.
  • The weight is in the tokens: spec 1 line 142 "text.s | 12 / 15 | 16 / 20 | 400 / 500 / 600 | Body text, labels, buttons, numbers in tables", line 144 "text.l | … | 600 | Document headings, the count-in number", and line 138 "numbers, positions and values are IBM Plex Mono"; ui/src/design/tokens.ts:59-61 has the same weights. So Mono at 600 is asked for by the spec itself (a number in text.l, and any m-num digits inside a 600 heading inherit 600 too); the three boxes do not use a weight the tokens lack. The fix is the report's first choice, not the other way round.
  • That the browser then draws a synthesized bold of the 500 face is from the CSS Fonts rules (weight matching falls back to 500; font-synthesis-weight: auto) and from memory of WebKit's rule (a request of 600 or more with a face below it gets synthetic bold); not looked up in the installed WebKitGTK and not seen on the screen. Even without synthesis the face shown would be 500, not the 600 the tokens say.

Fix plan: add import "@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/600.css"; to ui/src/design/fonts.ts (files used as they are, OFL-1.1); run node licenses/check.mjs and check ui/src/components/about/ lists nothing per weight; look at the three boxes and one 600 heading with digits in tools/design/shoot_ui.py.

Fix

ui/src/design/fonts.ts: import "@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono/600.css"; (the files as they are, OFL-1.1). node licenses/check.mjs passes; the about dialog lists the package, not weights. Not looked at in a picture (no test: a font load).

UI-MIX-003lowbugfixedRight arrow does nothing at the bottom of compressor attack, delay interval and limiter release

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-003-right-arrow-does-nothing-at-the-bottom-of-compre.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/params/paramRows.ts:103-113 (keyStep: valueAt(p, fractionOf(p, value) + dir / 20))
ui/src/components/params/paramRows.ts:13-35 (decimals, roundTo)

What happens

On three log-scale rows, → (without Shift) at the lowest value gives back the same value, so the key does nothing: compressor 「おさえるまで」 (attack) at 0.1 ms, delay 「間隔」 (ms, sync off) at 1 ms, limiter 「もどるまで」 (release) at 1 ms. One twentieth of the bar from the bottom is ×1.41 or ×1.46 (0.141 ms, 1.41 ms, 1.46 ms), and rounding to the shown places (1, 0 and 0 decimals) brings it back to the bottom value. change() then sees v === shown and sends nothing. I checked every continuous parameter of EFFECT_PARAMS with a small script (python, the same math): these are the only three values where an arrow key does not move inside the range.

What should happen

Spec 4 "In the parameters: move the value / move it finely" (← →, Shift ← →): each press moves the value. Every other value of every parameter moves with →.

How to trigger

Read from the code and checked with a small python copy of fractionOf / valueAt / roundTo, not run in the app. Put a built-in limiter on an insert, select 「もどるまで」, press ← until it shows 1 ms, then press →: the value stays 1 ms. Shift → moves it to 2 ms.

Cause

keyStep steps by a share of the bar and then rounds to the shown places; at the bottom of these ranges the step is smaller than one unit of the last shown place.

Suggested fix

In keyStep, when the stepped value rounds back to the same value, move by one unit of the last shown place instead (as Shift does): e.g. const next = valueAt(...); return next !== roundTo(p, value) ? next : roundTo(p, value + dir / 10 ** decimals(p));. No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed with a test that was run (scratch worktree, not kept): for every continuous parameter of EFFECT_PARAMS, keyStep(p, min, +1, false) and keyStep(p, max, −1, false) were compared with the start value; exactly three did not move: compressor[2] (ms, at 0.1), delay[1] (ms, at 1) and limiter[2] (ms, at 1), as the report says. (Only the two ends of each range were run; the report's claim that no value inside a range is stuck was not run again.)

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/components/params/paramRows.ts keyStep, when the stepped value equals roundTo(p, value), step by one unit of the last shown place instead; add the three cases to the tests of paramRows.

Fix

ui/src/components/params/paramRows.ts keyStep: when one twentieth of the bar rounds back to the same value, it moves by one unit of the last shown place. Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "UI-MIX-003: → moves at the bottom of log-scale rows".

UI-MIX-005lowbugfixedTyped fader or send dB out of range keeps showing the typed number when Rust clamps it to the value it already had

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-005-typed-fader-or-send-db-out-of-range-keeps-showin.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts:118-125 (submit sets keyed = { value: v } with the typed value)
ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts:38-41 (keyed is cleared only when the song value changes)
ui/src/components/mixer/scale.ts:63-68 (parseDb leaves out-of-range values to Rust)

What happens

Typing a dB value into a fader or send box (Enter or double click on the number) keeps the typed number as keyed until the song value changes. Rust brings an out-of-range value into range (Gain::from_db in crates/meloet-model/src/mixer.rs:29: above +6 is +6, below −60 is −∞). When that result is the value the strip already has, the song does not change (unless_same), no state comes back, and keyed stays: the strip shows the typed number and puts the handle by it, though the sound and the song have the old value. Examples: a fader at +6.0 and typing 12 shows 「12.0」; a send at −∞ and typing −80 shows 「−80.0」 with the bar empty.

What should happen

The screen is a copy of Rust's state (CLAUDE.md; architecture 3, rule 5: only text being typed and the look during a drag may move first). After Enter the strip should show the song value, as it does when the clamped value differs from the old one.

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Open the mixer, raise a fader to the top (+6.0), double click its number, type 12, Enter: the number stays 「12.0」 until the fader is changed again.

Cause

submit stores the raw parsed value in keyed; it is dropped only by the $effect on opts.value(), which does not run when Rust's answer leaves the value as it was.

Suggested fix

Do not keep the typed value in keyed after send(opts.make(v, true)) (set keyed = null; the screen then shows the song value and Rust's answer), or clamp it the same way Rust does before keeping it (−60 floor to −∞, +6 top). No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). Lines corrected in Where. submit (control.svelte.ts:118-125) keeps the parsed number in keyed; keyed is cleared only by the effect on opts.value() (38-41); parseDb (scale.ts:63-68) does not clamp; Gain::from_db (crates/meloet-model/src/mixer.rs:29-36) makes above +6 → +6 and below −60 → −∞. When the clamped result is the value the strip already has, no state change comes and shown stays the typed number. Related: UI-PARITY-009 (typed values out of range say nothing); the two can be fixed in one pass over the same submit.

Fix plan: in ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts submit, do not keep the typed value past the answer: keep keyed only until send(...) resolves (then set it to null if it is still this one), so the strip shows the song value whatever Rust did; no clamp rule is copied to the screen. Test: fader at +6, type 12 → after the reply the shown value is +6.0.

Fix

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts submit: the typed value (keyed) is kept only until the state has reached the rev that answers it; then the strip shows the song value. Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "UI-MIX-005: a typed value Rust keeps as it was".

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-006-params-panel-drag-shows-the-start-outline-at-the.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

When an automation lane moves a built-in effect parameter or a sound knob (auto is set, so the handle is at the line's value), pressing the bar takes from from value, the parameter's own value in the song, not from the value shown. With a lane moving the parameter, auto !== value, so the outline is not where the handle was. mixControl takes from = opts.value(), which the strips feed with the shown (lane) value.

Fix

UI-MIX-007lowui-uxfixedGrabbing a fader or send within 1 s of arrow keys starts from the old value and the handle jumps back

docs/bugs/reports/UI-MIX-007-grabbing-a-fader-or-send-within-1-s-of-arrow-key.md · found 2026-11-25

Where

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts:49-57 (down: const from = opts.value(), the KeySteps wait is not flushed)
ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts:132-134 (shown shows keyed before the drag)

What happens

After ↑ ↓ (fader, send) or ← → (pan), the strip shows the new value (keyed) and the step is sent with commit: true only 1 second after the key is let go. If the mouse grabs the same control inside that second, down starts the drag from the song value, not from the shown one: the handle jumps back to the old place at the press and the drag moves from there. Then the KeySteps timer fires during the drag and sends commit: true with the key value (one step, and the sound jumps to it) while the screen shows the drag value; the next commit: false of the drag moves the sound back.

What should happen

The drag starts from where the handle is (spec 3 (86-7) for the keys; the header of control.svelte.ts: "It does not jump to the pressed point; only movement from the pressed point changes the value"). The keys' step should be made before a new hand starts on the control, as startTyping and Enter already do with keys.flush().

How to trigger

Read from the code, not run. Focus a fader with Tab, press ↑ three times (the value shows +3 dB over the old one), and within one second press the fader with the mouse: the handle goes back 3 dB at once.

Cause

down neither flushes the pending key step nor reads keyed.

Suggested fix

Call keys.flush() at the start of down and take from from the shown value (keyed ? keyed.value : opts.value()). No protocol change, no new wording.

Verification

Confirmed by reading (not run). Lines corrected in Where. down (control.svelte.ts:49-57) takes from = opts.value() and does not call keys.flush(); shown is keyed.value before the press and live.now = from after it, so the handle jumps back by the keyed amount; the KeySteps timer (mixDrag.ts:84, KEY_COMMIT_MS) then fires during the drag and sends commit: true with the key value. startTyping and Enter already flush.

Fix plan (as suggested): in down, keys.flush() first and from = keyed ? keyed.value : opts.value() (ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts). Test: ↑ three times, press within the wait → one commit: true with the keyed value before the drag, live.from is the keyed value.

Fix

ui/src/components/mixer/control.svelte.ts down: keys.flush() first, and the drag starts from keyed when there is one. Test: ui/tests/fix-ui-values.test.ts "UI-MIX-007: grabbing a fader right after arrow keys".

Fix plan (as suggested): in ui/src/components/automation/AutoLane.svelte, ui/src/components/timeline/TempoBand.svelte and MarkerRow.svelte, write the same text to dragReading.text whenever the local reading changes, and null on release, on cancel and on destroy (as Arrange.svelte does for clip drags).
